DOMAIN 1: ECOLOGICAL & SCIENTIFIC KNOWLEDGE
Understanding the science behind natural systems and environments


________________


DOMAIN OVERVIEW


Ecological and scientific knowledge forms the foundation of evidence-based environmental management. This domain covers the scientific understanding of how natural environments function—from the molecular level to entire landscapes—and how to apply this knowledge to conservation and restoration. Professionals with strong capabilities in this domain translate complex ecological processes into actionable management strategies, conduct research that advances the field, and ensure that decisions are grounded in sound science.


This domain encompasses nine skill areas spanning traditional disciplines (ecology, soils, forestry, wildlife) and emerging frontiers (climate adaptation, biotechnology, novel ecosystems, sustainable agriculture). While some professionals develop deep expertise in one area, increasingly complex environmental challenges demand integration across multiple areas and collaboration with social scientists, engineers, and communities.


Example Positions Within This Domain


Professionals whose work falls primarily within Domain 1 include (but are not limited to):


Ecosystem & Conservation Science Roles:
* Ecologist / Research Ecologist
* Conservation Biologist
* Restoration Ecologist

How Advanced Technologies Are Changing This Domain


Scientific understanding of natural systems is being revolutionized by technological breakthroughs that allow unprecedented observation, measurement, and prediction:


Genomics & Molecular Technologies:
* Environmental DNA (eDNA) detects species from water, soil, or air samples without direct observation
* Genome sequencing identifies adaptive traits and informs conservation breeding programs
* CRISPR and gene editing raise possibilities (and controversies) for conservation applications
* Metabarcoding reveals entire biological communities from single samples
* Population genomics tracks genetic diversity, gene flow, and evolutionary responses to change
* Microbiome analysis reveals hidden symbioses critical to ecosystem function


Remote Sensing & Earth Observation:
* Satellite imagery monitors ecosystem change across entire continents in near real-time
* LiDAR creates precise 3D maps of forest structure and topography
* Hyperspectral imaging detects plant stress, species composition, and chemical properties
* Drone-mounted sensors enable high-resolution monitoring at landscape scales
* Thermal imaging tracks wildlife, water stress, and fire behavior
* Synthetic aperture radar penetrates clouds and canopy to reveal ground conditions


Sensor Networks & IoT (Internet of Things):
* Distributed sensor networks continuously monitor soil moisture, temperature, and nutrients
* Acoustic monitoring records soundscapes revealing biodiversity patterns
* Camera trap networks with AI recognition automatically identify thousands of wildlife images
* Water quality sensors provide real-time data on streams and lakes
* Automated weather stations create fine-scale climate datasets
* GPS/satellite collars track animal movements revealing habitat use and migration


Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning:
* Computer vision automatically identifies species in millions of photos
* Predictive models forecast disease outbreaks, invasive species spread, and fire risk
* Deep learning discovers patterns in ecological datasets too complex for traditional analysis
* AI accelerates analysis of acoustic recordings for biodiversity monitoring
* Neural networks model species distributions under future climate scenarios
* Automated classification of satellite imagery maps land cover change globally


Big Data & Cloud Computing:
* Integration of massive datasets (climate, biodiversity, land use) reveals global patterns
* Cloud computing enables complex ecosystem models that once required supercomputers
* Open data platforms democratize access to environmental information
* Real-time data sharing accelerates scientific discovery and management response
* Citizen science platforms aggregate millions of observations
* Data visualization tools make complex patterns comprehensible


Advanced Modeling & Simulation:
* Earth system models integrate climate, hydrology, vegetation, and biogeochemistry
* Agent-based models simulate individual organism behavior scaling to populations
* Machine learning improves climate downscaling for local adaptation planning
* Digital twins of ecosystems test management scenarios before implementation
* Coupled human-natural systems models integrate social and ecological dynamics
* Bayesian networks handle uncertainty in complex ecological decisions
Field Technology Integration:
* Tablets and smartphones enable digital data collection with instant quality control
* Portable DNA sequencers identify species in the field within hours
* Handheld spectrometers analyze soil and plant chemistry on site
* Mobile GIS integrates real-time field data with spatial layers
* Ruggedized sensors withstand harsh field conditions for long-term deployment
* Satellite communication enables data transmission from remote locations


Challenges & Considerations:


These technological advances also create new challenges:
* Data deluge: Too much data can overwhelm capacity to analyze and act
* Technical barriers: Not all organizations can access or afford cutting-edge technology
* Validation needs: New methods require ground-truthing and validation
* Black box problems: AI decisions may lack transparency or interpretability
* Ethical concerns: Genetic technologies raise questions about intervention appropriateness
* Digital divide: Technology access inequities between well-funded and under-resourced areas
* Skills gaps: Traditional ecologists may lack training in computational methods
* Losing field skills: Over-reliance on remote sensing may erode field observation capabilities
* Privacy concerns: Wildlife tracking and camera networks raise surveillance questions


Future-Ready Skills:


Ecological scientists increasingly need:
* Computational and programming skills (R, Python, GIS)
* Statistical and machine learning literacy
* Ability to work with large, complex datasets
* Understanding of sensor technologies and data quality
* Critical evaluation of AI outputs and limitations
* Genomic data analysis and interpretation
* Integration of multiple data streams
* Interdisciplinary collaboration across science, technology, and social sciences
* Communication of complex, data-rich findings to non-technical audiences


The most effective scientists blend technological innovation with fundamental field skills—using technology to enhance observation and understanding while maintaining deep connection to living systems.


Cross-Sector Applications


Ecological and scientific knowledge is essential across all environmental sectors:


* Public Agencies: Research programs, resource assessments, monitoring, adaptive management frameworks
* Private Sector: Environmental impact assessments, sustainability science, natural resource management on private lands
* NGOs: Conservation science, restoration planning, advocacy supported by science, land protection prioritization
* Consulting: Ecological assessments, species surveys, habitat evaluations, impact analysis
* Academia: Research, teaching, knowledge creation, graduate training, long-term studies
* Tribal Nations: Traditional Ecological Knowledge integration, co-management science, cultural resource protection


Career Progression in This Domain


Professionals typically develop ecological and scientific expertise along three dimensions:


1. Taxonomic/System Focus: 
Generalist → specialist in ecosystem/taxon → integrative systems view


2. Methodological Depth: 
Basic methods → advanced techniques → method development/innovation


3. Scale of Application: 
Site-specific → landscape → regional → global patterns




________________




DOMAIN-LEVEL CAREER PATHWAYS


Pathway 1: Research Ecologist / Conservation Scientist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Field Technician, Research Assistant, Junior Biologist
  Focus: 1.1, 1.4 or 1.3 depending on system
  Typical setting: Universities, research stations, agencies, NGOs
  
Mid (3-7 years): Ecologist, Conservation Biologist, Research Associate
  Focus: Deep expertise in 1-2 areas, beginning to integrate across areas
  Typical setting: Research institutions, agencies, NGO science programs
  
Senior (7-15 years): Senior Scientist, Principal Investigator, Research Ecologist
  Focus: Multiple skill areas, leading research programs
  Typical setting: Lead roles in universities, agencies, large NGOs
  
Expert (15+ years): Chief Scientist, Research Director, Distinguished Scientist
  Focus: Strategic scientific leadership, shaping field directions
  Typical setting: Organizational leadership, national/international scientific bodies


Pathway 2: Applied Resource Specialist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Resource Technician, Field Specialist, Assistant Biologist
  Focus: 1.2, 1.3, or 1.4 with strong field skills
  Typical setting: Public lands, resource management agencies, private forestry
  
Mid (3-7 years): Resource Specialist, District Biologist, Forester
  Focus: Applied ecology for specific resource, integration with management
  Typical setting: Field positions on public/private lands
  
Senior (7-15 years): Resource Manager, Program Lead, Senior Specialist
  Focus: Program oversight, integration across resources
  Typical setting: Management units, program leadership
  
Expert (15+ years): Regional Ecologist, Chief Resource Officer, Program Director
  Focus: Strategic resource management, policy influence
  Typical setting: Regional/national leadership in agencies or large organizations


Pathway 3: Climate & Adaptation Scientist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Climate Research Assistant, Adaptation Technician
  Focus: 1.5, 1.6 with strong quantitative skills
  Typical setting: Universities, climate research centers, agencies
  
Mid (3-7 years): Climate Scientist, Adaptation Specialist, Climate Ecologist
  Focus: 1.5, 1.6, 1.8, climate-ecology integration
  Typical setting: Research institutions, agencies, climate-focused NGOs
  
Senior (7-15 years): Senior Climate Scientist, Adaptation Program Manager
  Focus: Leading climate programs, translating science to adaptation action
  Typical setting: Major climate programs in agencies, NGOs, international organizations
  
Expert (15+ years): Climate Science Director, Chief Adaptation Officer
  Focus: Strategic climate science leadership, policy influence
  Typical setting: National/international leadership in climate programs


Pathway 4: Restoration & Applied Ecologist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Restoration Technician, Ecological Field Crew
  Focus: 1.1, 1.2, basic restoration techniques
  Typical setting: Restoration firms, NGOs, agencies
  
Mid (3-7 years): Restoration Ecologist, Ecological Consultant
  Focus: 1.1, 1.2, 1.8, 1.9 applied restoration science
  Typical setting: Consulting, NGOs, agency restoration programs
  
Senior (7-15 years): Senior Restoration Ecologist, Restoration Program Manager
  Focus: Complex restoration projects, landscape-scale initiatives
  Typical setting: Leading restoration programs in agencies, large NGOs, consulting firms
  
Expert (15+ years): Restoration Science Director, Chief Restoration Officer
  Focus: Strategic restoration leadership, innovation in practice
  Typical setting: Organizational leadership, influence on restoration field

REPRESENTATIVE JOB ROLE PROFILES


Profile 1: Wildlife Biologist (Mid-Career)
Typical Employers: State/federal wildlife agencies, NGOs, consulting firms, tribal natural resources departments


Primary Skill Areas: 1.4 (Wildlife & Fisheries Biology), 1.1 (Ecosystem Science), 1.6 (Climate Adaptation)


Proficiency Requirements:
* 1.4.1-1.4.5: Advanced level
* 1.1.1, 1.1.5: Intermediate to Advanced
* 1.6.1: Intermediate


Typical Responsibilities:
* Conduct population surveys and monitoring for game and non-game species
* Analyze population trends and develop management recommendations
* Design and implement habitat improvement projects
* Coordinate with stakeholders on wildlife management issues
* Prepare technical reports and management plans
* Assess climate change impacts on wildlife populations


Related Credentials: 
Bachelor's in Wildlife Biology, Ecology, or related field required; Master's often preferred
The Wildlife Society Certified Wildlife Biologist® (CWB)
State-specific wildlife management certification


Salary Range: $55,000-$85,000 (varies by sector and region)


Profile 2: Soil Scientist (Senior)
Typical Employers: USDA NRCS, state agencies, consulting firms, agricultural companies, NGOs


Primary Skill Areas: 1.2 (Soil & Water Science), 1.9 (Sustainable Agriculture & Food Systems), 1.6 (Climate Adaptation)


Proficiency Requirements:
* 1.2.1-1.2.2: Advanced to Expert level
* 1.2.3-1.2.5: Advanced
* 1.9.2: Advanced (soil carbon)


Typical Responsibilities:
* Conduct detailed soil surveys and mapping
* Provide technical guidance on soil health and conservation practices
* Assess soil carbon sequestration potential for climate programs
* Evaluate land suitability for various uses
* Lead soil-related aspects of environmental assessments
* Train junior scientists and conservation planners in soil science


Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's or Master's in Soil Science, Agronomy, or related field
* Certified Professional Soil Scientist (CPSS) or Certified Professional Soil Classifier (CPSC)
* Professional Wetland Scientist (PWS) for those working in wetlands


Salary Range: $70,000-$105,000


Profile 3: Restoration Ecologist (Mid-Senior)
Typical Employers: NGOs, consulting firms, agencies with restoration programs, tribes


Primary Skill Areas: 1.1 (Ecosystem Science), 1.8 (Novel Ecosystem Management), 1.2 (Soil & Water Science)


Proficiency Requirements:
1.1.1, 1.1.3, 1.1.4: Advanced
1.8.1, 1.8.3: Intermediate to Advanced
1.2.1-1.2.2: Intermediate


Typical Responsibilities:
* Design ecological restoration plans for degraded ecosystems
* Oversee implementation of restoration projects
* Monitor restoration outcomes and adapt approaches
* Navigate novel conditions due to climate change and invasive species
* Coordinate with landowners, agencies, and contractors
* Secure funding through grants and partnerships
* Integrate scientific knowledge with traditional ecological knowledge


Related Credentials:
* Master's in Restoration Ecology, Ecology, or related field often required
* Society for Ecological Restoration (SER) Certified Ecological Restoration Practitioner (CERP)
* Professional Wetland Scientist (PWS) for wetland restoration


Salary Range: $65,000-$95,000


Profile 4: Climate Adaptation Scientist (Senior)
Typical Employers: Federal/state agencies, climate-focused NGOs, research institutions, international organizations


Primary Skill Areas: 1.5 (Climate Science), 1.6 (Climate Adaptation), 1.8 (Novel Ecosystem Management)


Proficiency Requirements:
* 1.5.1, 1.5.3: Advanced to Expert
* 1.6.1-1.6.4: Advanced
* 1.8.1, 1.8.4: Advanced


Typical Responsibilities:
* Conduct climate vulnerability assessments for species and ecosystems
* Develop climate adaptation strategies for natural resource management
* Lead scenario planning processes with managers and stakeholders
* Translate climate model outputs for local adaptation planning
* Identify and prioritize climate refugia for conservation
* Publish research on climate impacts and adaptation
* Provide technical assistance to managers implementing adaptation


Related Credentials:
* Master's or Ph.D. in Climate Science, Ecology, or related field
* Specialized training in climate modeling, scenario planning, or vulnerability assessment
* No standardized certification, but professional societies (Ecological Society of America, etc.)


Salary Range: $80,000-$120,000


Profile 5: Conservation Geneticist (Expert)
Typical Employers: Universities, research institutions, agencies with genetics programs, zoos/aquaria, NGOs


Primary Skill Areas: 1.7 (Biotechnology Applications), 1.4 (Wildlife & Fisheries Biology), 1.1 (Ecosystem Science)


Proficiency Requirements:
* 1.7.1-1.7.5: Advanced to Expert level
* 1.4.1, 1.4.3: Advanced
* Strong computational and statistical skills


Typical Responsibilities:
* Design and conduct population genetics studies for conservation
* Assess genetic diversity and structure of threatened populations
* Provide guidance on genetic management of small populations
* Lead eDNA monitoring programs for rare species
* Evaluate assisted gene flow and translocation proposals
* Collaborate with managers to integrate genetics into conservation practice
* Publish peer-reviewed research on conservation genetics
* Advise on policy related to genetic technologies in conservation


Related Credentials:
* Ph.D. in Genetics, Molecular Ecology, or related field typically required
* 10+ years research experience
* Publication record in peer-reviewed journals
* Membership in professional societies (Society for Conservation Biology, etc.)


Salary Range: $90,000-$140,000+ (varies widely by sector)

SKILL AREAS
1.1 ECOSYSTEM SCIENCE
Understanding how all parts of nature work together in a specific area


Skills related to understanding how living things interact with each other and their environment. This helps land managers see the big picture of how everything is connected in the landscapes they manage.
1.1.1 Ecosystem dynamics and processes
Understanding how energy and nutrients flow through natural systems, and how different species interact with each other over time.


Job Tasks:
1. Conduct field-based studies to observe food web interactions
2. Model nutrient cycling in different habitat types using ecological software
3. Develop restoration plans that consider species interdependencies


Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
Knowledge: Understands basic ecological concepts (food webs, nutrient cycles, energy flow)
Application: Recognizes ecosystem processes in the field
Indicators:
*   Explains trophic levels and energy transfer
*   Identifies major nutrient cycles (carbon, nitrogen, water)
*   Recognizes predator-prey relationships in ecosystems
*   Understands concept of ecosystem services
*   Collects field data on ecosystem processes with guidance


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
Knowledge: Applies ecosystem theory to real-world systems; understands feedback loops
Application: Analyzes ecosystem function and identifies limiting factors
Indicators:
*   Diagrams energy and nutrient flows in specific ecosystems
*   Identifies keystone species and their ecosystem roles
*   Recognizes trophic cascades and indirect effects
*   Analyzes how disturbances affect ecosystem processes
*   Uses ecosystem models to predict responses to change
*   Interprets ecosystem indicators of health or stress


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
Knowledge: Expert in ecosystem theory; understands complex interactions and thresholds
Application: Predicts ecosystem responses; designs experiments testing ecosystem function
Indicators:
*   Designs and implements ecosystem-level studies
*   Identifies and measures ecosystem state changes and regime shifts
*   Models complex multi-species interactions
*   Predicts ecosystem responses to multiple stressors
*   Publishes research on ecosystem processes
*   Integrates ecosystem science into adaptive management frameworks


EXPERT (10+ years)
Knowledge: Shapes understanding of ecosystem theory and application
Application: Leads transformative ecosystem research or management programs
Indicators:
*   Develops new conceptual frameworks for ecosystem understanding
*   Leads major research programs on ecosystem function
*   Influences how ecosystem science informs policy and management
*   Publishes influential papers advancing ecosystem ecology
*   Mentors ecosystem scientists across institutions


Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Ecosystem-based management, ecosystem service assessments
Private Sector: Natural capital accounting, ecosystem service valuation
NGOs: Ecosystem restoration, intact ecosystem protection strategies
Consulting: Ecosystem impact assessments, ecosystem service analysis
Academia: Ecosystem ecology research, theoretical development


---


1.1.2 Biodiversity assessment and monitoring Measuring and tracking the variety of plants, animals, and other organisms in an area to understand ecosystem health.
Job Tasks:
1. Design and implement biodiversity monitoring protocols
2. Analyze long-term biodiversity data to assess conservation impacts
3. Report findings to policymakers and recommend habitat protections
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands biodiversity concepts (genetic, species, ecosystem diversity) Application: Conducts basic biodiversity surveys Indicators:
* Explains importance of biodiversity for ecosystem function
* Conducts species inventories using standardized protocols
* Uses field guides and keys for species identification
* Records biodiversity data accurately
* Calculates basic diversity indices (richness, Shannon index)
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies biodiversity assessment methods; understands sampling design Application: Designs and implements monitoring programs Indicators:
* Designs statistically valid sampling protocols for biodiversity assessment
* Uses multiple survey methods appropriate to taxa and systems
* Analyzes spatial and temporal patterns in biodiversity
* Interprets biodiversity indicators of ecosystem condition
* Identifies priority areas for biodiversity conservation
* Integrates biodiversity data into management decisions
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in biodiversity science; understands conservation biogeography Application: Leads landscape-scale biodiversity assessments Indicators:
* Designs comprehensive biodiversity monitoring programs
* Analyzes biodiversity trends across spatial and temporal scales
* Models biodiversity responses to environmental change
* Evaluates effectiveness of conservation interventions on biodiversity
* Uses advanced techniques (eDNA, acoustic monitoring, remote sensing)
* Publishes biodiversity research
* Integrates biodiversity assessment with conservation planning
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes biodiversity science and conservation practice Application: Leads major biodiversity initiatives at regional/global scales Indicators:
* Directs national or international biodiversity programs
* Influences biodiversity policy and targets (e.g., post-2020 biodiversity framework)
* Develops innovative biodiversity assessment approaches
* Publishes influential biodiversity research
* Provides expert guidance on biodiversity to governments/organizations
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Biodiversity inventories for protected areas, Endangered Species Act assessments Private Sector: Biodiversity impact assessments, biodiversity net gain calculations NGOs: Biodiversity hotspot identification, conservation prioritization Consulting: Biodiversity surveys for development projects, offset calculations Academia: Biodiversity research, macroecology, conservation biology
________________


1.1.3 Ecological succession principles Understanding how plant and animal communities naturally change over time, such as how a field becomes a forest or how an area recovers after a fire.
Job Tasks:
1. Map stages of ecological succession in post-disturbance sites
2. Use historical data to predict future vegetation states in unmanaged lands
3. Recommend restoration techniques that align with natural successional pathways
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic succession concepts (primary, secondary succession; pioneer vs climax species) Application: Recognizes successional stages in the field Indicators:
* Identifies early, mid, and late successional species
* Explains difference between primary and secondary succession
* Recognizes how disturbance resets succession
* Understands concept of climax communities
* Documents successional trajectories with supervision
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies successional theory to predict vegetation change Application: Uses succession concepts in management and restoration Indicators:
* Predicts successional trajectories for different site conditions
* Uses succession to inform restoration planting strategies
* Identifies factors that facilitate or inhibit succession
* Recognizes when systems are arrested in early successional stages
* Applies successional concepts to habitat management
* Maps successional stages across landscapes
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in succession theory; understands alternative stable states Application: Designs management strategies leveraging or manipulating succession Indicators:
* Predicts multiple alternative successional pathways
* Designs restoration approaches for arrested succession
* Understands succession under novel conditions (climate change, invasives)
* Models successional trajectories quantitatively
* Recognizes threshold conditions preventing succession
* Publishes on successional dynamics and management
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Advances successional ecology theory and application Application: Shapes how succession informs landscape management Indicators:
* Develops new frameworks for understanding succession
* Leads research on succession under global change
* Influences how successional science informs restoration practice
* Publishes influential papers on successional ecology
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Post-fire management, vegetation management planning Private Sector: Reclamation of disturbed lands, habitat succession management NGOs: Restoration ecology, old-growth forest conservation Consulting: Restoration planning, vegetation trajectory prediction Academia: Succession research, community ecology
________________


1.1.4 Disturbance ecology Understanding how events like fires, floods, or storms affect ecosystems and how natural systems recover from these disruptions.
Job Tasks:
1. Analyze fire history data to understand recurring disturbance patterns
2. Assess ecosystem resilience following flood or wildfire events
3. Design post-disturbance recovery plans incorporating ecological thresholds
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic disturbance types and their ecological effects Application: Recognizes disturbance impacts in the field Indicators:
* Identifies major disturbance types (fire, flood, windthrow, insects, disease)
* Explains how disturbances create habitat heterogeneity
* Recognizes immediate disturbance effects on vegetation and wildlife
* Understands natural disturbance regimes for ecosystems
* Assists with post-disturbance assessments
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies disturbance ecology theory; understands disturbance regimes Application: Assesses disturbance impacts and predicts recovery Indicators:
* Reconstructs historical disturbance regimes
* Assesses severity and extent of disturbances
* Predicts recovery trajectories based on disturbance characteristics
* Evaluates how disturbance regimes have been altered by management
* Identifies species and ecosystems dependent on disturbance
* Uses disturbance ecology in habitat management planning
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in disturbance ecology; understands multiple interacting disturbances Application: Designs management incorporating or mimicking disturbance regimes Indicators:
* Analyzes compound disturbances and interactions
* Models disturbance regimes and their effects on landscapes
* Designs management that restores natural disturbance regimes
* Predicts ecosystem responses to altered disturbance regimes under climate change
* Identifies resilience and transformation thresholds
* Publishes disturbance ecology research
* Integrates disturbance ecology into landscape planning
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes understanding of disturbance ecology and application Application: Leads transformative research or management of disturbance-dependent systems Indicators:
* Advances disturbance ecology theory
* Influences policy on managing disturbance regimes
* Leads landscape-scale disturbance management programs
* Publishes influential research on disturbance ecology
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Fire management, post-disaster recovery, disturbance-based habitat management Private Sector: Natural disturbance risk assessment, post-disturbance salvage planning NGOs: Advocacy for natural disturbance regimes, fire ecology education Consulting: Disturbance impact assessments, restoration after disturbance Academia: Disturbance ecology research, fire ecology
________________


1.1.5 Habitat requirements analysis Determining what specific conditions different plants and animals need to survive and thrive in their environment.
Job Tasks:
1. Conduct field studies to document species-specific habitat needs
2. Evaluate habitat suitability using GIS-based landscape analysis
3. Integrate habitat data into land-use planning to minimize ecological impacts
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic habitat concepts (cover, forage, water, space) Application: Describes habitat for common species Indicators:
* Lists habitat components for focal species
* Uses habitat assessment protocols in the field
* Identifies obvious habitat limitations
* Measures basic habitat variables (vegetation structure, water availability)
* Understands concept of habitat quality
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies habitat ecology theory; understands limiting factors Application: Conducts habitat assessments and suitability analyses Indicators:
* Conducts comprehensive habitat assessments
* Identifies limiting habitat factors for species
* Uses GIS to map and analyze habitat
* Develops habitat suitability models
* Evaluates effects of land use on habitat quality and connectivity
* Makes habitat management recommendations
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in habitat ecology; understands scale-dependent habitat selection Application: Designs habitat management strategies across landscapes Indicators:
* Models habitat suitability at multiple spatial scales
* Designs habitat connectivity analyses
* Predicts habitat shifts under climate change
* Develops landscape-scale habitat conservation strategies
* Evaluates effectiveness of habitat management interventions
* Publishes habitat ecology research
* Integrates habitat science into regional conservation planning
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes habitat science and conservation practice Application: Leads transformative habitat conservation initiatives Indicators:
* Develops innovative habitat modeling approaches
* Influences habitat conservation policy
* Leads major habitat conservation programs
* Publishes influential habitat ecology research
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Critical habitat designation, habitat conservation plans Private Sector: Habitat mitigation, habitat conservation banking NGOs: Habitat protection prioritization, wildlife corridor design Consulting: Habitat assessments for development projects, mitigation design Academia: Habitat ecology research, species-habitat relationships
________________


1.2 SOIL & WATER SCIENCE
Understanding the physical foundation of landscapes
Skills related to understanding the properties and processes of soil and water systems, which are fundamental to all land management decisions.
1.2.1 Soil classification and mapping Identifying different soil types and creating maps that show where they occur across a landscape.
Job Tasks:
1. Conduct field surveys to classify soil horizons and types using USDA standards
2. Create digital soil maps using GIS and field data
3. Interpret soil maps to guide land use planning and conservation practices
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands soil formation factors and basic taxonomy Application: Describes soil profiles using standard terminology Indicators:
* Identifies soil horizons in profile pits
* Describes soil texture by feel method
* Records soil observations using standard forms
* Uses USDA soil taxonomy at order/suborder level
* Understands how parent material, climate, and topography affect soils
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies soil taxonomy to great group/subgroup; understands soil-landscape relationships Application: Conducts soil surveys and creates soil maps Indicators:
* Classifies soils to subgroup level using Soil Taxonomy
* Conducts transect surveys identifying soil series
* Creates digital soil maps using GIS and field observations
* Interprets aerial photos and LiDAR for soil mapping
* Uses soil survey data for planning and management
* Understands soil-vegetation relationships
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in soil classification and geomorphology Application: Leads complex soil survey projects Indicators:
* Leads soil survey projects for diverse landscapes
* Develops soil map unit descriptions and interpretations
* Correlates soils across geographic areas
* Updates official soil surveys and contributes to NRCS databases
* Trains junior soil scientists in classification and mapping
* Integrates soil data with ecological and hydrological models
* Publishes soil survey reports and technical papers
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes soil classification and survey standards Application: Provides national/international leadership in soil science Indicators:
* Contributes to development of soil taxonomy
* Leads major soil survey programs
* Influences soil survey standards and methods
* Recognized expert in regional soil geomorphology
* Publishes influential soil science research
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: NRCS soil surveys, land management planning, wetland delineation Private Sector: Site assessments, agricultural planning, development suitability NGOs: Conservation planning, restoration site assessment Consulting: Soil surveys for projects, wetland delineation, environmental site assessments Academia: Soil genesis research, pedology, soil geography
________________


1.2.2 Soil chemistry and biology Understanding the chemical composition of soils and the living organisms within them that contribute to soil health.
Job Tasks:
1. Collect and analyze soil samples to assess nutrient levels and microbial activity
2. Diagnose soil health problems related to contamination or nutrient deficiency
3. Recommend soil amendments to improve fertility and biological function
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic soil chemistry (pH, nutrients, CEC) and soil organisms Application: Collects soil samples and interprets basic tests Indicators:
* Collects representative soil samples following protocols
* Interprets soil test results for pH, N-P-K, organic matter
* Understands role of soil organisms in nutrient cycling
* Recognizes symptoms of nutrient deficiency or toxicity
* Follows laboratory safety protocols
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies soil chemistry and microbiology principles; understands biogeochemical cycles Application: Diagnoses soil problems and recommends management Indicators:
* Conducts comprehensive soil chemical analyses
* Interprets complex soil test results (micronutrients, contaminants)
* Assesses soil biological activity (respiration, enzyme assays)
* Recommends appropriate amendments for soil improvement
* Understands soil-plant nutrient relationships
* Evaluates contamination risks and remediation needs
* Uses soil health assessment frameworks
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in soil chemistry and biology; understands soil food webs and biogeochemistry Application: Solves complex soil problems; leads soil health initiatives Indicators:
* Diagnoses complex soil chemistry problems (salinity, sodicity, contamination)
* Designs soil health improvement programs
* Models soil carbon dynamics and nutrient cycling
* Evaluates emerging contaminants (PFAS, microplastics) in soils
* Applies advanced soil biological assessments (DNA sequencing, metabolomics)
* Publishes soil chemistry/biology research
* Trains practitioners in soil health assessment
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes soil chemistry and biology science and practice Application: Leads transformative soil health initiatives Indicators:
* Develops innovative soil health assessment methods
* Influences soil health policy and programs
* Leads major research programs on soil biogeochemistry
* Publishes influential soil science research
* Recognized authority in soil chemistry or soil biology
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Soil health programs, contaminated site assessment Private Sector: Agricultural soil management, soil carbon programs, brownfield remediation NGOs: Soil health advocacy, regenerative agriculture promotion Consulting: Contaminated site assessment, agricultural consulting Academia: Soil chemistry/biology research, biogeochemistry
________________


1.2.3 Watershed hydrology Understanding how water moves through a landscape, from rainfall to rivers and underground systems.
Job Tasks:
1. Measure streamflow, precipitation, and groundwater levels to model watershed behavior
2. Analyze hydrologic data to identify flooding risks and water availability trends
3. Develop watershed management plans to reduce runoff and improve infiltration
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands water cycle, watershed concepts, basic hydrology Application: Collects hydrologic data and assists with analysis Indicators:
* Delineates watersheds using topographic maps or GIS
* Measures streamflow using standard methods
* Installs and maintains precipitation gauges and stream gauges
* Records water level observations
* Understands surface and groundwater interactions
* Calculates basic watershed characteristics (area, slope, stream order)
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies hydrologic principles; understands watershed processes Application: Analyzes watershed hydrology and develops management recommendations Indicators:
* Conducts water budget analyses
* Analyzes streamflow data for trends and peak flows
* Uses hydrologic models (HEC-HMS, SWAT, etc.)
* Evaluates effects of land use on watershed hydrology
* Designs stream gauging networks
* Calculates flood frequencies and recurrence intervals
* Assesses groundwater recharge and discharge areas
* Integrates hydrology into watershed management planning
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in watershed science; understands complex hydrologic systems Application: Leads watershed studies and management programs Indicators:
* Designs and implements comprehensive watershed studies
* Models complex watersheds with multiple land uses
* Predicts hydrologic changes under climate scenarios
* Evaluates watershed restoration effectiveness
* Designs low-impact development and green infrastructure
* Integrates water quantity and quality modeling
* Publishes watershed hydrology research
* Leads watershed planning initiatives
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes watershed science and management practice Application: Provides leadership on major watershed initiatives Indicators:
* Develops innovative hydrologic modeling approaches
* Influences watershed management policy
* Leads large-scale watershed programs
* Publishes influential hydrology research
* Provides expert testimony on water resources
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Watershed management, flood risk assessment, water supply planning Private Sector: Water resource development, hydropower, stormwater management NGOs: Watershed protection, river restoration Consulting: Hydrologic studies, water resource engineering, stormwater design Academia: Hydrology research, watershed modeling
________________


1.2.4 Water quality assessment Testing and evaluating water conditions to determine if it is suitable for wildlife, recreation, or human use.
Job Tasks:
1. Collect water samples to test for pollutants, pH, turbidity, and other quality indicators
2. Interpret water quality results to determine compliance with environmental standards
3. Advise on remediation strategies for impaired streams or water bodies
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic water quality parameters and standards Application: Collects water samples and conducts field measurements Indicators:
* Collects water samples following proper protocols
* Measures field parameters (temperature, pH, dissolved oxygen, conductivity)
* Maintains chain of custody for samples
* Uses water quality testing equipment properly
* Interprets basic water quality standards
* Recognizes obvious pollution sources
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies water quality science; understands pollutant fate and transport Application: Designs monitoring programs and interprets complex results Indicators:
* Designs water quality monitoring programs
* Conducts comprehensive water quality assessments
* Analyzes trends in water quality data
* Identifies pollution sources using multiple lines of evidence
* Interprets water quality standards and criteria
* Evaluates biological indicators of water quality
* Develops water quality improvement recommendations
* Prepares water quality reports for regulatory compliance
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in water quality science; understands aquatic ecosystems Application: Leads water quality programs and develops solutions Indicators:
* Develops watershed-scale water quality programs
* Models pollutant loading and transport
* Evaluates emerging contaminants (pharmaceuticals, PFAS, microplastics)
* Designs and evaluates water quality improvement projects
* Develops TMDLs (Total Maximum Daily Loads)
* Integrates water quality with ecological and human health risk assessment
* Publishes water quality research
* Provides expert technical guidance on complex water quality issues
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes water quality science and policy Application: Leads transformative water quality initiatives Indicators:
* Influences water quality standards and policy
* Develops innovative water quality assessment methods
* Leads major water quality research or management programs
* Publishes influential water quality research
* Provides expert testimony on water quality issues
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Water quality monitoring, NPDES permitting, impaired waters programs Private Sector: Industrial discharge monitoring, water treatment, compliance NGOs: Water quality advocacy, stream monitoring programs Consulting: Water quality assessments, NPDES compliance, remediation design Academia: Water quality research, aquatic chemistry, limnology
________________


1.2.5 Erosion processes and control Understanding how soil gets carried away by wind or water, and techniques to prevent unwanted erosion.
Job Tasks:
1. Assess slope stability and identify erosion-prone areas using field and remote sensing data
2. Implement erosion control measures such as check dams, vegetation buffers, or terraces
3. Monitor effectiveness of erosion control structures and adapt management accordingly
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands erosion processes (sheet, rill, gully, streambank, wind) Application: Identifies erosion problems and installs basic controls Indicators:
* Recognizes different erosion types in the field
* Identifies erosion-prone areas based on slope, soil type, vegetation
* Installs erosion control BMPs (silt fences, straw wattles, mulch)
* Understands factors affecting erosion (rainfall intensity, slope, soil erodibility)
* Monitors erosion control effectiveness
* Follows erosion control plans
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies erosion science; understands sediment transport Application: Designs erosion control strategies for sites Indicators:
* Assesses erosion rates using models (RUSLE, MUSLE, WEPP)
* Designs comprehensive erosion control plans
* Specifies appropriate erosion control techniques for site conditions
* Evaluates streambank erosion and designs stabilization
* Uses bioengineering approaches for erosion control
* Monitors and adapts erosion control measures
* Integrates erosion control with restoration and land management
* Ensures erosion control meets regulatory requirements
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in erosion and sediment science Application: Solves complex erosion problems at landscape scale Indicators:
* Designs complex erosion control for challenging sites (steep slopes, high rainfall, erodible soils)
* Models watershed-scale sediment production and delivery
* Evaluates geomorphic processes driving erosion
* Designs stream channel restoration for erosion control
* Integrates erosion control with ecosystem restoration
* Evaluates long-term effectiveness of erosion control
* Publishes on erosion processes and control
* Trains practitioners in erosion assessment and control
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes erosion science and management practice Application: Leads major erosion control or watershed sediment programs Indicators:
* Develops innovative erosion control approaches
* Influences erosion control policy and standards
* Leads watershed-scale sediment reduction programs
* Publishes influential erosion science research
* Provides expert consultation on major erosion problems
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Road erosion control, mine reclamation, agricultural conservation programs Private Sector: Construction erosion control, mine reclamation, agricultural operations NGOs: Stream restoration, erosion control education Consulting: Erosion control design, SWPPP development, stream restoration Academia: Erosion research, geomorphology, sediment transport
________________


1.3 FORESTRY SCIENCE
Understanding forest ecosystems and their management
Skills related to understanding tree growth, forest health, and sustainable management of forested landscapes.
________________


1.3.1 Silviculture principles and practices Knowledge of techniques for growing and cultivating healthy forests for various objectives.
Job Tasks:
1. Develop silvicultural prescriptions tailored to stand objectives and site conditions
2. Implement thinning, planting, or prescribed fire treatments to meet management goals
3. Monitor treatment areas to assess success and adapt practices
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic silvicultural systems (even-aged vs uneven-aged) and treatments Application: Implements silvicultural treatments under supervision Indicators:
* Describes common silvicultural systems (clearcutting, shelterwood, selection)
* Recognizes different stand development stages
* Assists with timber marking and thinning operations
* Plants trees following proper techniques
* Understands basic tree biology and growth requirements
* Measures treatment outcomes (survival, growth)
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies silvicultural principles to diverse forest types and objectives Application: Develops silvicultural prescriptions independently Indicators:
* Writes silvicultural prescriptions for specific stands and objectives
* Selects appropriate silvicultural system for site and goals
* Marks timber for harvest or thinning
* Designs planting plans (species, spacing, site preparation)
* Uses prescribed fire as silvicultural tool
* Monitors and evaluates treatment effectiveness
* Adapts silviculture to ecological and economic objectives
* Integrates wildlife habitat considerations
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in silviculture; understands complex forest dynamics Application: Designs innovative silvicultural approaches for complex objectives Indicators:
* Develops silvicultural strategies for multi-objective management
* Designs treatments for restoration of degraded forests
* Applies silviculture under novel conditions (climate change, new pests)
* Models long-term forest development under different treatments
* Evaluates silvicultural effectiveness across multiple stands
* Integrates carbon sequestration into silvicultural planning
* Trains foresters in silvicultural practices
* Publishes silviculture research or guidance
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes silviculture science and practice Application: Leads transformative silvicultural programs or research Indicators:
* Develops innovative silvicultural systems
* Influences silvicultural policy and standards
* Leads landscape-scale silvicultural programs
* Publishes influential silviculture research
* Recognized expert in silviculture for specific forest types
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: National forest management, state forestry programs Private Sector: Industrial forestry, family forest management, timber investment NGOs: Forest restoration, working forest conservation Consulting: Forest management planning, timber appraisal Academia: Silviculture research, forest ecology
________________


1.3.2 Forest mensuration and inventory Measuring and cataloging forest resources, such as tree size, volume, and distribution across a landscape.
Job Tasks:
1. Measure tree height, diameter, and age using field instruments
2. Conduct forest inventories to estimate timber volume and species composition
3. Input inventory data into forest management databases for analysis
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic mensuration concepts and tools Application: Measures trees and collects inventory data Indicators:
* Measures tree diameter (DBH) using diameter tape or calipers
* Estimates tree height using clinometer or laser
* Records tree data accurately on inventory forms
* Understands basic volume and basal area concepts
* Establishes inventory plots following protocols
* Uses GPS to locate plot centers
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies mensuration and sampling theory Application: Designs and implements forest inventories Indicators:
* Designs statistically valid sampling schemes
* Estimates timber volume using volume tables or equations
* Calculates stand-level metrics (basal area, volume, trees per acre)
* Uses forest inventory software
* Conducts growth and yield assessments
* Determines appropriate sampling intensity for objectives
* Prepares inventory reports with statistical confidence
* Uses remote sensing to stratify inventories
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in forest biometrics and inventory design Application: Leads complex inventory programs Indicators:
* Designs inventories for diverse objectives (timber, carbon, biodiversity)
* Develops or adapts volume equations for local conditions
* Integrates remote sensing (LiDAR, multispectral) with field inventory
* Conducts growth modeling and yield prediction
* Evaluates inventory precision and cost-effectiveness
* Develops forest inventory databases and systems
* Trains crews in inventory techniques
* Publishes forest mensuration research
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes forest inventory science and practice Application: Develops innovative inventory approaches Indicators:
* Develops new mensuration techniques or models
* Leads national or regional inventory programs (e.g., FIA)
* Integrates advanced technologies into operational inventory
* Publishes influential biometrics research
* Influences inventory standards and methods
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Forest inventory programs (FIA), national forest planning Private Sector: Timber cruising, carbon inventory, forest valuation NGOs: Forest monitoring, carbon project verification Consulting: Timber inventory, appraisal, carbon accounting Academia: Forest biometrics research, inventory design
________________


1.3.3 Stand dynamics and growth modeling Predicting how forests will develop over time under different conditions or management actions.
Job Tasks:
1. Use software tools to simulate forest growth under different management scenarios
2. Analyze stand development stages and predict yield over time
3. Recommend silvicultural interventions to optimize growth or habitat structure
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic forest growth concepts (diameter growth, height growth, mortality) Application: Uses growth models with guidance Indicators:
* Explains factors affecting tree and stand growth
* Describes stand development stages (establishment, stem exclusion, understory reinitiation, old growth)
* Measures tree growth (increment cores, growth plots)
* Uses basic growth and yield tables
* Understands density-dependent growth
* Interprets growth model outputs
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies growth modeling; understands forest dynamics Application: Uses models to predict forest development Indicators:
* Uses forest growth simulators (FVS, Organon, etc.)
* Projects stand development under different management scenarios
* Analyzes trade-offs between growth, yield, and structural objectives
* Calibrates models for local conditions
* Integrates climate data into growth projections
* Uses models to support management decisions
* Validates model predictions with field data
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in forest dynamics and modeling Application: Develops growth models or leads modeling programs Indicators:
* Develops or adapts growth models for specific applications
* Models complex stand dynamics (mixed species, uneven-aged)
* Incorporates disturbance, climate change, and management into projections
* Conducts uncertainty and sensitivity analysis for models
* Uses individual-tree and landscape-scale models
* Evaluates model performance and accuracy
* Publishes growth modeling research
* Trains practitioners in growth modeling
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes forest modeling science Application: Develops transformative modeling approaches Indicators:
* Develops widely-used growth models
* Advances forest modeling theory and methods
* Integrates ecology, economics, and climate in models
* Leads major modeling initiatives
* Publishes influential modeling research
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Forest planning, growth and yield programs Private Sector: Timber production modeling, rotation age optimization NGOs: Carbon sequestration modeling, habitat projection Consulting: Growth and yield analysis, management planning Academia: Forest dynamics research, model development
________________

1. Survey forests for signs of pest outbreaks, disease, or stress
2. Diagnose biotic and abiotic stressors impacting tree health
3. Propose mitigation strategies such as sanitation cuts or biological controls
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Recognizes common forest pests and diseases Application: Identifies obvious forest health problems Indicators:
* Recognizes symptoms of common pests and diseases
* Collects samples for expert identification
* Documents pest/disease occurrence and severity
* Understands basic pest life cycles
* Follows forest health monitoring protocols
* Recognizes stress from drought, air pollution, or other abiotic factors
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Diagnoses forest health problems; understands pest ecology Application: Conducts forest health assessments and develops recommendations Indicators:
* Identifies wide range of forest insects, diseases, and disorders
* Conducts systematic forest health surveys
* Assesses severity and extent of outbreaks
* Predicts pest spread and impact
* Recommends appropriate management responses
* Uses integrated pest management principles
* Understands host-pest-environment relationships
* Monitors treatment effectiveness
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in forest pathology/entomology Application: Leads forest health programs Indicators:
* Diagnoses complex forest health problems
* Develops forest health monitoring systems
* Predicts and models pest outbreaks
* Designs integrated management strategies for forest health
* Responds to novel pests and diseases
* Evaluates forest vulnerability to climate-driven health threats
* Publishes forest health research
* Trains foresters in forest health assessment
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes forest health science and policy Application: Leads major forest health initiatives Indicators:
* Influences forest health policy and response
* Develops innovative forest health management approaches
* Leads response to major forest health crises
* Publishes influential forest pathology/entomology research
* Recognized expert in specific pest or disease systems
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Forest health monitoring, pest response programs Private Sector: Timber damage assessment, forest health management NGOs: Forest health monitoring, native tree conservation Consulting: Forest health surveys, treatment recommendations Academia: Forest pathology/entomology research
________________

* Assists with timber volume estimation
* Understands harvest equipment and capabilities
* Follows best management practices during harvest
* Monitors harvest operations for contract compliance
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies timber management principles; integrates multiple objectives Application: Develops timber harvest plans independently Indicators:
* Designs harvest units meeting ecological and economic objectives
* Estimates timber value and stumpage rates
* Writes timber sale contracts and specifications
* Coordinates timber sale administration
* Ensures compliance with Best Management Practices
* Integrates wildlife habitat, water quality, recreation in harvest planning
* Manages post-harvest site preparation and regeneration
* Uses GIS to plan and track harvest units
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in sustainable timber management Application: Leads comprehensive timber management programs Indicators:
* Develops long-term sustainable harvest strategies
* Optimizes harvest scheduling across landscapes
* Integrates carbon, biodiversity, and timber objectives
* Ensures third-party certification (FSC, SFI) compliance
* Evaluates and improves harvest practices for ecological outcomes
* Manages complex timber programs across ownership types
* Trains foresters in timber management

EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes sustainable forestry policy and practice Application: Leads transformative timber management initiatives Indicators:

* Provides expert consultation on complex timber issues
