DOMAIN 2: TECHNICAL & TECHNOLOGICAL SKILLS
Using modern data and technology to manage places and resources more effectively
DOMAIN OVERVIEW
Technical and technological skills enable environmental professionals to leverage digital tools, spatial analysis, data science, and emerging technologies to make more informed decisions, work more efficiently, and address environmental challenges at unprecedented scales. This domain bridges traditional field-based environmental work with cutting-edge computational approaches, transforming how we monitor, analyze, and manage natural resources.
This domain encompasses seven skill areas spanning from established technologies (GIS, databases, field equipment) to emerging frontiers (AI, immersive technologies, advanced analytics). While technology evolves rapidly, the fundamental principles of spatial thinking, data quality, analytical rigor, and appropriate tool selection remain constant. Professionals may specialize deeply in specific technologies or develop broad capabilities across multiple tools depending on their role and career trajectory.
Example Positions Within This Domain
Professionals whose work falls primarily within Domain 2 include (but are not limited to):
Geospatial Technology Roles:
* GIS Analyst / GIS Specialist
* Geospatial Scientist
* Remote Sensing Specialist / Analyst
* Cartographer / GIS Cartographer
* Spatial Analyst
* GIS Developer / Programmer
* GIS Manager / Coordinator
* LiDAR Analyst
* Drone/UAS Operator and Analyst
Data Science & Analytics Roles:
* Environmental Data Scientist
* Data Analyst / Environmental Analyst
* Statistical Analyst
* Database Administrator / Manager
* Data Engineer
* Machine Learning Engineer (Environmental Applications)
* Bioinformatician (Conservation/Ecology)
* Quantitative Ecologist
* Modeling Specialist
Field Technology Roles:
* Field Technology Specialist
* Drone/UAS Program Manager
* Environmental Sensor Technician
* Field Data Manager
* Mobile Technology Coordinator
* IoT Specialist (Environmental)
* Instrumentation Technician
Advanced Analytics & AI Roles:
* Machine Learning Scientist (Environmental)
* Environmental Modeler
* Predictive Analytics Specialist
* Decision Support Systems Developer
* AI/ML Applications Scientist
* Computer Vision Specialist (Environmental)
* Computational Ecologist
Digital Collaboration & Systems Roles:
* Environmental Informatics Specialist
* Knowledge Management Specialist
* Digital Systems Administrator
* IT Manager (Environmental Organizations)
* Collaboration Technology Coordinator
* Digital Transformation Specialist
Immersive Technology Roles:
* AR/VR Developer (Environmental Applications)
* Digital Twin Specialist
* 3D Visualization Specialist
* Immersive Experience Designer
* Metaverse Environmental Designer
How Advanced Technologies Are Changing This Domain
Technical and technological capabilities in environmental management are experiencing exponential advancement, fundamentally transforming what's possible:
Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning Revolution:
* Computer vision automates species identification in millions of camera trap and drone images
* Deep learning detects subtle patterns in environmental data invisible to human analysis
* Predictive models forecast ecological change, pest outbreaks, and fire risk with increasing accuracy
* Natural language processing extracts insights from decades of unstructured field notes and reports
* Generative AI assists with code writing, data analysis workflows, and technical documentation
* AI-powered edge computing enables real-time decision-making from field sensors
* Transfer learning allows models trained in one region/ecosystem to work in others with limited local data
Cloud Computing & Big Data Infrastructure:
* Planetary-scale environmental datasets accessible without local infrastructure
* Distributed computing tackles analyses impossible on desktop machines
* Cloud-native tools democratize access to advanced analytics
* Real-time data streaming and processing enables immediate response
* Open data platforms accelerate discovery and collaboration
* Reproducible, shareable computational workflows enhance transparency
* Cloud-based collaboration tools enable global teams working on shared datasets
Internet of Things (IoT) & Sensor Networks:
* Dense networks of low-cost sensors provide continuous environmental monitoring
* Satellite-connected devices transmit data from remote locations
* Smart sensors self-calibrate and detect anomalies
* Edge computing reduces data transmission needs and enables local intelligence
* Integration of diverse sensor types (acoustic, chemical, optical, meteorological)
* Long-life batteries and solar power enable years of autonomous operation
* Mesh networks allow sensors to communicate and extend range
Advanced Remote Sensing & Earth Observation:
* Daily global satellite coverage tracks change in near-real-time
* Commercial satellites provide sub-meter resolution imagery
* Hyperspectral sensors measure hundreds of wavelengths revealing chemistry and stress
* Synthetic aperture radar penetrates clouds and darkness
* LiDAR creates precise 3D models of terrain and vegetation structure
* CubeSats and satellite constellations provide unprecedented temporal resolution
* Fusion of multiple sensor types extracts maximum information
Autonomous Systems & Robotics:
* Autonomous drones conduct surveys without human piloting
* Underwater robots explore aquatic environments continuously
* Ground robots monitor and sample hazardous environments
* Swarms of coordinated robots cover large areas efficiently
* Autonomous vehicles reduce field crew time and increase safety
* Robots equipped with sensors perform tasks impossible for humans
Digital Twins & Simulation:
* Virtual replicas of ecosystems enable scenario testing without field experiments
* Real-time integration of sensor data with models enables adaptive management
* Simulations predict outcomes of management alternatives
* Digital twins integrate physical, ecological, and social dynamics
* Virtual environments train staff on procedures and decision-making
* "What-if" scenarios explore consequences of uncertainty
Blockchain & Distributed Systems:
* Transparent tracking of environmental credits and conservation outcomes
* Immutable records of monitoring data enhance credibility
* Decentralized platforms enable community-based data collection
* Smart contracts automate payment for ecosystem services
* Supply chain transparency for sustainable products
Quantum Computing (Emerging):
* Optimization of conservation reserve networks at landscape scales
* Complex ecosystem modeling with numerous interacting variables
* Climate model refinement and downscaling
* Molecular simulations for understanding ecological processes
Challenges & Considerations:
These technological advances create significant challenges:
* Skills gap: Traditional environmental professionals may lack computational training
* Data overload: Too much data can overwhelm capacity to extract insights and take action
* Technology access inequity: Digital divides limit who can benefit from advanced tools
* Black box algorithms: AI decisions may lack transparency critical for management
* Validation needs: New methods require rigorous testing and ground-truthing
* Rapidly changing tools: Continuous learning required to stay current
* Infrastructure requirements: Advanced tech needs computing power, storage, and connectivity
* Data privacy and ethics: Environmental monitoring may capture sensitive information
* Over-reliance risk: Technology may replace rather than augment human judgment
* E-waste and energy use: Technology's environmental footprint must be considered
* Vendor lock-in: Proprietary systems may limit flexibility
* Cybersecurity: Environmental data and control systems vulnerable to attacks
Future-Ready Skills:
Environmental technology professionals increasingly need:
* Programming and scripting (Python, R, JavaScript)
* Cloud computing platforms (AWS, Google Earth Engine, Azure)
* Machine learning and AI fundamentals
* Statistical analysis and modeling
* Data visualization and communication
* Version control and reproducible workflows (Git, Docker)
* Database design and management (SQL, NoSQL)
* API integration and web services
* Geospatial analysis and remote sensing
* Critical evaluation of technology appropriateness
* Ethical considerations in AI and data use
* Ability to translate between technical and non-technical stakeholders
* Continuous learning mindset to adapt to evolving technology
The most effective technical professionals combine deep technological expertise with solid understanding of ecological systems and management needs—using technology as a powerful tool to achieve conservation and sustainability outcomes, not as an end in itself.
Cross-Sector Applications
Technical and technological skills are universally valuable across all environmental sectors:
Public Agencies: GIS-based planning, monitoring systems, data management, decision support tools Private Sector: Environmental compliance tracking, sustainability reporting, supply chain monitoring NGOs: Conservation planning, impact monitoring, data visualization for advocacy Consulting: Technical analysis services, data management, custom tool development Academia: Research computing, data analysis, modeling, reproducible science Tribal Nations: Resource monitoring, mapping traditional territories, data sovereignty
Career Progression in This Domain
Professionals typically develop technical capabilities along three dimensions:
1. Tool Proficiency: Single tool user → multi-tool practitioner → tool developer/customizer
2. Analytical Complexity: Descriptive analysis → predictive modeling → prescriptive decision support
3. Scale of Impact: Personal productivity → team efficiency → organizational capability building
________________


DOMAIN-LEVEL CAREER PATHWAYS
Pathway 1: Geospatial Analyst / GIS Professional Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
Entry (0-3 years): GIS Technician, Geospatial Analyst I, Junior GIS Specialist
* Focus: 2.1 (Geospatial Technologies), basic 2.2 (Data Management)
* Typical setting: Agencies, consulting firms, NGOs
Mid (3-7 years): GIS Analyst, Senior Geospatial Analyst, GIS Specialist
* Focus: Advanced 2.1, 2.2, 2.4 (spatial analytics)
* Typical setting: Agency GIS units, consulting, corporate sustainability
Senior (7-15 years): Senior GIS Analyst, GIS Manager, Geospatial Scientist
* Focus: 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 2.6 (AI integration), leadership
* Typical setting: GIS program management, enterprise GIS
Expert (15+ years): GIS Director, Chief Geospatial Officer, Geospatial Science Lead
* Focus: Strategic geospatial technology leadership
* Typical setting: Organizational leadership, major programs
Pathway 2: Environmental Data Scientist Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
Entry (0-3 years): Data Analyst, Junior Data Scientist, Research Technician
* Focus: 2.2 (Data Management & Analysis), programming basics
* Typical setting: Research institutions, agencies, consulting
Mid (3-7 years): Data Scientist, Environmental Analyst, Statistical Analyst
* Focus: 2.2, 2.4 (Advanced Analytics), 2.6 (AI/ML)
* Typical setting: Research organizations, tech companies, large NGOs
Senior (7-15 years): Senior Data Scientist, Lead Analyst, Data Science Manager
* Focus: Advanced 2.4, 2.6, leading analytical projects
* Typical setting: Data science teams, research leadership
Expert (15+ years): Chief Data Officer, Director of Analytics, Principal Data Scientist
* Focus: Strategic data science leadership, innovation
* Typical setting: Organizational data strategy, major initiatives
Pathway 3: Field Technology & Monitoring Specialist Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
Entry (0-3 years): Field Technician, Drone Operator, Sensor Technician
* Focus: 2.3 (Field Technology), basic 2.1
* Typical setting: Field operations, monitoring programs
Mid (3-7 years): Field Technology Specialist, UAS Program Manager, Monitoring Coordinator
* Focus: 2.3, 2.1 (data integration), 2.2 (field data management)
* Typical setting: Monitoring programs, field operations management
Senior (7-15 years): Technology Program Manager, Monitoring Systems Lead, Innovation Specialist
* Focus: Technology integration, program design, 2.3, 2.6
* Typical setting: Program leadership, technology innovation
Expert (15+ years): Director of Technology & Innovation, Chief Technology Officer (Environmental)
* Focus: Strategic technology direction, innovation leadership
* Typical setting: Organizational technology strategy
Pathway 4: Environmental Informatics / Systems Developer Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
Entry (0-3 years): Database Analyst, Junior Developer, IT Support (Environmental)
* Focus: 2.2 (databases), 2.5 (Digital Collaboration), basic programming
* Typical setting: IT departments, environmental organizations
Mid (3-7 years): Environmental Informatics Specialist, Systems Developer, Data Engineer
* Focus: 2.2, 2.4, 2.5, custom tool development
* Typical setting: Environmental informatics units, consulting
Senior (7-15 years): Lead Developer, Environmental Informatics Manager, Solutions Architect
* Focus: Enterprise systems, integration, all of Domain 2
* Typical setting: Organizational IT leadership, large programs
Expert (15+ years): Chief Information Officer, Director of Environmental Informatics
* Focus: Strategic information systems direction
* Typical setting: Organizational leadership
________________


REPRESENTATIVE JOB ROLE PROFILES
Profile 1: GIS Analyst (Mid-Career) Typical Employers: Federal/state agencies, local governments, consulting firms, utilities, NGOs
Primary Skill Areas: 2.1 (Geospatial Technologies), 2.2 (Data Management & Analysis), 2.4 (Spatial Analytics)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 2.1.1-2.1.5: Advanced level
* 2.2.1, 2.2.3: Intermediate to Advanced
* 2.4.4: Intermediate
Typical Responsibilities:
* Create and maintain spatial databases and map products
* Conduct spatial analysis for environmental planning and assessments
* Develop custom GIS tools and automate workflows
* Integrate remote sensing data with field observations
* Support decision-making with spatial visualization and analysis
* Train staff in GIS applications
* Manage geospatial data quality and standards
Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's in Geography, GIS, Environmental Science, or related field
* GISP (GIS Professional) certification
* Proficiency in Esri ArcGIS, QGIS, and programming (Python)
Salary Range: $55,000-$85,000 (varies by sector and region)
________________


Profile 2: Environmental Data Scientist (Senior) Typical Employers: Research institutions, federal agencies, tech companies, large NGOs, consulting firms
Primary Skill Areas: 2.2 (Data Management & Analysis), 2.4 (Advanced Analytics), 2.6 (AI-Enhanced Monitoring)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 2.2.2, 2.2.4: Advanced to Expert level
* 2.4.1, 2.4.2: Advanced
* 2.6.1-2.6.3: Advanced
Typical Responsibilities:
* Design and implement machine learning models for environmental prediction
* Develop data pipelines for large-scale environmental datasets
* Conduct advanced statistical analyses of ecological data
* Create predictive models for species distributions, fire risk, or ecosystem change
* Develop decision support tools and visualizations
* Publish research on environmental data science methods
* Collaborate with ecologists and managers to translate models to action
Related Credentials:
* Master's or Ph.D. in Data Science, Statistics, Computer Science, Quantitative Ecology
* Strong programming skills (Python, R, SQL)
* Experience with machine learning frameworks (TensorFlow, PyTorch, scikit-learn)
* Cloud computing experience (AWS, Google Cloud)
Salary Range: $85,000-$130,000

* 2.1.2, 2.1.5: Intermediate to Advanced

* Conduct aerial surveys for vegetation mapping, wildlife monitoring, infrastructure inspection

* Train staff in drone operations and data processing
* Integrate drone data with GIS and other platforms
* Evaluate and acquire new drone technology
Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's in related field
* FAA Part 107 Remote Pilot Certificate (required)
* Photogrammetry and remote sensing training
* Drone piloting experience across platforms
Salary Range: $60,000-$95,000

2.1 GEOSPATIAL TECHNOLOGIES
Using digital maps and location-based information
Skills related to creating, analyzing, and using digital maps and location data to understand spatial patterns and relationships on the landscape.
________________


2.1.1 Geographic Information Systems (GIS) operation Using specialized mapping software to create, analyze, and display maps and geographic information.
Job Tasks:
1. Create and update spatial layers representing land use, vegetation, and infrastructure
2. Analyze spatial data to support environmental assessments and planning decisions
3. Generate printable and digital maps for reports, briefings, and field crews
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands GIS concepts (layers, attributes, projections, spatial relationships) Application: Performs basic GIS operations Indicators:
* Creates simple maps using existing data layers
* Edits features and attributes in spatial databases
* Understands coordinate systems and projections
* Performs basic queries and selections
* Symbolizes features appropriately for map communication
* Exports maps and data in various formats
* Uses online mapping platforms (ArcGIS Online, Google Earth)
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies spatial analysis principles; understands data structures Application: Conducts spatial analysis and manages GIS data Indicators:
* Designs and builds geodatabases
* Performs multi-step spatial analyses (buffer, overlay, proximity)
* Creates custom symbology and layout templates
* Uses geoprocessing tools and model builder
* Integrates GPS data with GIS
* Manages coordinate system transformations
* Creates web maps and story maps
* Troubleshoots common GIS problems
* Maintains data quality and accuracy
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in GIS architecture, spatial analysis, and automation Application: Develops custom GIS solutions and enterprise systems Indicators:
* Designs enterprise GIS architectures
* Writes Python scripts to automate workflows
* Performs advanced spatial statistics and modeling
* Develops custom GIS tools and extensions
* Integrates GIS with databases and other systems
* Manages large spatial datasets efficiently
* Implements GIS standards and best practices
* Trains and mentors GIS staff
* Evaluates and implements new GIS technologies
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes GIS strategy and standards Application: Provides GIS leadership at organizational level Indicators:
* Develops organizational GIS strategy
* Leads enterprise GIS implementation
* Influences GIS standards and best practices
* Provides expert consultation on complex spatial problems
* Publishes on GIS methods and applications
* Represents organization in GIS community leadership
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Land management planning, resource mapping, permit tracking Private Sector: Site analysis, environmental compliance, supply chain visualization NGOs: Conservation prioritization, land acquisition planning, advocacy mapping Consulting: Spatial analysis for clients, environmental impact mapping Academia: Research GIS, spatial analysis methods, teaching
________________


2.1.2 Remote sensing data analysis Interpreting information collected from satellites or aircraft to monitor land conditions without physically visiting every location.
Job Tasks:
1. Interpret satellite and aerial imagery to detect changes in vegetation, soil, or water
2. Process and classify multispectral imagery to map land cover types
3. Integrate remote sensing data with field observations for accuracy validation
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands remote sensing basics (spectral bands, resolution, platforms) Application: Views and interprets imagery Indicators:
* Identifies features in aerial photos and satellite imagery
* Understands different remote sensing platforms and sensors
* Distinguishes true color, false color, and other band combinations
* Downloads and displays imagery from public sources
* Recognizes spectral signatures of common land covers
* Understands image resolution (spatial, spectral, temporal, radiometric)
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies image processing and classification techniques Application: Processes imagery and extracts information Indicators:
* Performs atmospheric and geometric corrections
* Creates image mosaics and composites
* Conducts supervised and unsupervised classification
* Calculates vegetation indices (NDVI, EVI, etc.)
* Detects changes using multi-temporal imagery
* Validates classifications with accuracy assessment
* Uses multiple sensors (Landsat, Sentinel, MODIS, aerial)
* Integrates imagery with GIS data
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in advanced remote sensing methods and applications Application: Develops remote sensing-based monitoring programs Indicators:
* Uses advanced classification methods (object-based, machine learning)
* Analyzes hyperspectral and LiDAR data
* Develops custom spectral indices for specific applications
* Models biophysical variables from imagery
* Implements change detection and time series analysis
* Processes SAR and thermal imagery
* Uses cloud computing platforms (Google Earth Engine)
* Publishes remote sensing research
* Trains practitioners in remote sensing methods
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes remote sensing science and applications Application: Leads major remote sensing initiatives Indicators:
* Develops innovative remote sensing approaches
* Influences remote sensing standards and methods
* Leads large-scale monitoring programs using remote sensing
* Publishes influential remote sensing research
* Provides expert consultation on remote sensing
* Advances operational use of remote sensing
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Land cover mapping, vegetation monitoring, disaster assessment Private Sector: Crop monitoring, forest inventory, infrastructure monitoring NGOs: Deforestation monitoring, habitat mapping, conservation effectiveness Consulting: Remote sensing analysis for projects, change detection Academia: Remote sensing research, algorithm development
________________


2.1.3 GPS data collection and management Using Global Positioning System devices to accurately record locations in the field and manage that information.
Job Tasks:
1. Use handheld or mounted GPS units to collect accurate field location data
2. Transfer and clean GPS datasets for integration into GIS systems
3. Maintain GPS equipment and train staff on field use protocols
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands GPS basics (satellites, accuracy, coordinate systems) Application: Collects GPS points in the field Indicators:
* Uses handheld GPS to record waypoints
* Understands factors affecting GPS accuracy
* Sets appropriate coordinate systems
* Records attributes with GPS points
* Downloads waypoints to computer
* Displays GPS data in GIS or mapping software
* Maintains GPS units (batteries, firmware updates)
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies GPS principles; understands differential correction Application: Manages GPS data collection programs Indicators:
* Designs GPS data collection protocols
* Uses GPS with sub-meter accuracy (DGPS, RTK)
* Collects lines and polygons, not just points
* Performs post-processing differential correction
* Integrates GPS with mobile data collection apps
* Manages GPS databases and data quality
* Troubleshoots GPS equipment and data issues
* Uses GPS for navigation and mapping
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in GNSS technology and high-precision positioning Application: Implements enterprise GPS/GNSS programs Indicators:
* Uses high-precision GNSS equipment (survey-grade)
* Establishes base stations and control networks
* Performs centimeter-level positioning (RTK, PPK)
* Integrates multiple GNSS constellations
* Develops GPS data collection standards
* Evaluates and acquires GPS technology
* Uses GPS with other sensors (inertial, cameras)
* Trains field staff in GPS methods
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes GPS/GNSS applications in environmental field Application: Provides strategic leadership on positioning technology Indicators:
* Develops innovative GPS applications
* Influences positioning standards
* Leads major positioning technology programs
* Publishes on GPS methods and applications
* Provides expert consultation on positioning
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Resource inventory, boundary surveys, field data collection Private Sector: Asset tracking, precision agriculture, site surveys NGOs: Species locations, monitoring sites, land acquisition Consulting: Field surveys, site mapping, boundary determination Academia: Research plot locations, wildlife tracking, experimental design
________________


2.1.4 Spatial analysis and modeling Using specialized techniques to analyze patterns across landscapes and predict how they might change.
Job Tasks:
1. Analyze terrain, habitat connectivity, or resource distribution using GIS tools
2. Build predictive spatial models to support conservation planning
3. Evaluate scenarios using spatial overlays and suitability analyses
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic spatial analysis concepts Application: Performs simple spatial analyses Indicators:
* Uses buffer and overlay analysis
* Performs distance and proximity calculations
* Conducts basic terrain analysis (slope, aspect)
* Creates viewsheds and line-of-sight analyses
* Understands spatial relationships and queries
* Interprets spatial analysis results
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies spatial analysis methods and statistics Application: Conducts complex spatial analyses Indicators:
* Performs cost-distance and least-cost path analysis
* Conducts spatial statistics (hot spot, clustering)
* Uses raster analysis and map algebra
* Develops habitat suitability models
* Analyzes landscape connectivity
* Performs watershed delineation and flow analysis
* Uses spatial interpolation methods
* Conducts multi-criteria evaluation
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in spatial modeling and advanced spatial statistics Application: Develops sophisticated spatial models Indicators:
* Builds species distribution models
* Performs landscape pattern analysis
* Uses spatial optimization for conservation planning (Marxan, Zonation)
* Conducts network analysis (corridors, connectivity)
* Applies machine learning to spatial prediction
* Models spatial processes and dynamics
* Validates spatial models rigorously
* Publishes spatial analysis research
* Develops custom spatial analysis tools
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes spatial analysis science and applications Application: Leads major spatial modeling initiatives Indicators:
* Develops innovative spatial modeling approaches
* Influences spatial analysis standards and methods
* Leads landscape-scale spatial planning programs
* Publishes influential spatial analysis research
* Provides expert consultation on complex spatial problems
* Advances integration of spatial analysis with decision-making
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Conservation planning, site selection, impact analysis Private Sector: Site suitability, risk modeling, optimization NGOs: Conservation prioritization, corridor design, threat assessment Consulting: Spatial modeling services, impact assessment Academia: Spatial ecology research, landscape ecology
________________

Job Tasks:

3. Develop interactive or web-based map applications for public or internal use

* Creates clear map layouts with standard elements
* Uses color effectively for map communication
* Understands map scale and generalization
* Exports maps in appropriate formats
* Follows organizational map standards
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies advanced cartographic design principles Application: Creates professional, publication-quality maps Indicators:
* Designs custom maps for diverse audiences
* Uses typography effectively
* Applies color theory and visual hierarchy
* Creates multi-page map series and atlases
* Designs web maps and story maps
* Uses data-driven pages and automated map production
* Incorporates charts, graphs, and inset maps
* Ensures accessibility in map design
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in cartographic design and visualization Application: Develops innovative visualization approaches Indicators:
* Creates sophisticated interactive visualizations
* Develops custom web mapping applications

* Applies user experience (UX) design to maps
* Develops organizational cartographic standards
* Trains staff in cartographic design
* Creates award-quality maps
* Uses emerging visualization technologies
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes cartographic practice and visualization Application: Provides leadership on visualization strategy Indicators:
* Develops innovative visualization approaches
* Influences cartographic standards
* Leads organizational visualization programs
* Publishes on cartographic methods
* Achieves recognition for cartographic excellence
* Advances state of the art in environmental visualization
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Public information maps, planning maps, web mapping Private Sector: Client presentations, environmental reports, dashboards NGOs: Advocacy maps, public engagement, fundraising materials Consulting: Project maps, permit applications, presentations Academia: Research maps, teaching materials, journal figures

2.2 DATA MANAGEMENT & ANALYSIS
Organizing and making sense of environmental information
Skills related to collecting, storing, analyzing, and interpreting data about natural resources and landscapes.
________________


2.2.1 Database design and management Creating and maintaining organized systems to store and access large amounts of environmental information.
Job Tasks:
1. Create relational databases to store environmental monitoring data
2. Manage data entry, queries, and updates for consistency and usability
3. Develop data backup and archiving procedures to ensure long-term accessibility
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands database concepts (tables, fields, records, relationships) Application: Uses existing databases and enters data Indicators:
* Enters data accurately into databases
* Performs basic queries to retrieve information
* Understands primary keys and foreign keys
* Uses database forms and reports
* Follows data entry protocols
* Recognizes data quality issues
* Backs up databases following procedures
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies database design principles and SQL Application: Designs and manages databases Indicators:
* Designs normalized database schemas
* Writes SQL queries to extract and analyze data
* Creates data entry forms and validation rules
* Develops database reports and views
* Implements data integrity constraints
* Manages user permissions and security
* Performs database optimization and maintenance
* Documents database structure and procedures
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in database architecture and advanced database systems Application: Develops enterprise database solutions Indicators:
* Designs complex multi-table database systems
* Uses advanced SQL (joins, subqueries, stored procedures)
* Implements both SQL and NoSQL databases appropriately
* Develops database APIs for application integration
* Optimizes database performance for large datasets
* Implements database replication and high availability
* Manages database migrations and version control
* Trains staff in database use and management
* Develops organizational data standards
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes database strategy and data architecture Application: Provides leadership on enterprise data management Indicators:
* Develops organizational data architecture
* Influences data management standards and best practices
* Leads enterprise database programs
* Provides expert consultation on complex data management
* Publishes on environmental data management
* Advances data management in environmental sector
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Resource inventory databases, monitoring data systems, permit tracking Private Sector: Environmental compliance tracking, sustainability metrics NGOs: Project databases, species observations, conservation metrics Consulting: Client data management, project tracking systems Academia: Research databases, specimen collections, experimental data

2.2.2 Statistical analysis and interpretation Using mathematical techniques to find patterns, test relationships, and draw conclusions from collected data.
Job Tasks:
1. Apply statistical tests to evaluate treatment effects on ecological variables
2. Analyze trends in long-term monitoring datasets to support decision-making
3. Create visualizations to communicate key patterns and uncertainties
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic statistics (mean, variance, correlation, hypothesis testing) Application: Conducts basic statistical analyses Indicators:
* Calculates descriptive statistics
* Creates appropriate graphs and charts
* Conducts t-tests and simple ANOVA
* Understands p-values and confidence intervals
* Uses statistical software (Excel, basic R or SPSS)
* Recognizes when statistical help is needed
* Interprets statistical outputs appropriately
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies diverse statistical methods appropriately Application: Designs and conducts statistical analyses Indicators:
* Uses regression analysis (linear, multiple, logistic)
* Conducts multivariate analyses (PCA, cluster analysis)
* Applies appropriate statistical tests for data types
* Checks statistical assumptions and uses transformations
* Analyzes time series and spatial data
* Uses R or Python for statistical analysis
* Creates publication-quality visualizations
* Interprets results and communicates uncertainty
* Designs statistically valid sampling schemes
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in advanced statistical methods and modeling Application: Leads complex statistical analyses and modeling projects Indicators:
* Uses generalized linear models (GLM, GLMM)
* Conducts Bayesian statistical analyses
* Applies machine learning algorithms
* Develops custom statistical models
* Uses hierarchical and mixed effects models
* Conducts power analysis and sample size determination
* Performs meta-analysis
* Publishes statistical methods research
* Trains practitioners in statistical methods
* Provides statistical consulting
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes statistical practice in environmental field Application: Provides statistical leadership and innovation Indicators:
* Develops innovative statistical approaches
* Influences statistical standards and methods
* Leads major statistical programs
* Publishes influential statistical research
* Provides expert statistical consultation
* Advances statistical practice in environmental science
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Monitoring program analysis, population assessments, effectiveness evaluation Private Sector: Quality assurance, impact assessment, research analysis NGOs: Program evaluation, research analysis, conservation effectiveness Consulting: Statistical analysis services, study design Academia: Research statistics, experimental design, biostatistics
________________


2.2.3 Data quality control and validation Ensuring that information collected is accurate, consistent, and reliable before it's used for decision-making.
Job Tasks:
1. Develop quality assurance protocols for field and lab data collection
2. Conduct regular data audits to identify and correct errors or anomalies
3. Train staff and partners on data validation standards and workflows
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands importance of data quality Application: Follows data quality procedures Indicators:
* Enters data carefully and double-checks entries
* Recognizes obvious data errors
* Follows data collection protocols precisely
* Uses data validation features in forms
* Reports data quality issues to supervisors
* Understands concepts of accuracy, precision, completeness
* Documents data collection conditions
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies data quality principles and methods Application: Implements quality control procedures Indicators:
* Develops data validation rules and checks
* Conducts systematic data quality audits
* Identifies and corrects outliers and anomalies
* Implements automated data validation
* Tracks data quality metrics
* Develops data quality reports
* Trains data collectors on quality standards
* Investigates and resolves data quality issues
* Documents quality control procedures
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in quality assurance/quality control (QA/QC) systems Application: Designs comprehensive QA/QC programs Indicators:
* Develops organizational QA/QC frameworks
* Implements ISO quality standards
* Uses statistical process control
* Designs quality control sampling schemes
* Evaluates measurement system accuracy and bias
* Develops automated data validation pipelines
* Conducts data quality assessments across programs
* Trains practitioners in QA/QC methods
* Integrates QA/QC into data management systems
* Publishes on data quality methods
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes data quality standards and practices Application: Provides leadership on data quality Indicators:
* Develops data quality standards for sector
* Influences organizational data quality policy
* Leads enterprise data quality programs
* Provides expert consultation on data quality
* Publishes influential work on data quality
* Advances data quality practices in environmental field
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Monitoring program QA/QC, regulatory data quality Private Sector: Laboratory QA/QC, environmental compliance data NGOs: Citizen science data validation, program data quality Consulting: Data quality services, QA/QC for projects Academia: Research data quality, laboratory QA/QC
________________


2.2.4 Programming and scripting Writing computer code to automate repetitive tasks or create custom tools for analyzing environmental data.
Job Tasks:
1. Write custom scripts to automate repetitive data processing tasks
2. Use programming languages (e.g., R, Python) for spatial or statistical analysis
3. Develop dashboards or reporting tools to streamline access to results
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands programming basics (variables, loops, functions) Application: Writes simple scripts Indicators:
* Modifies existing scripts for new purposes
* Writes basic scripts to automate tasks
* Understands fundamental programming concepts
* Uses one scripting language (Python, R, or JavaScript)
* Reads and interprets code written by others
* Uses version control basics (Git)
* Debugs simple code errors
* Follows coding best practices
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies programming principles across multiple languages Application: Develops functional programs and tools Indicators:
* Writes well-structured, documented code
* Uses multiple programming languages appropriately
* Develops data processing pipelines
* Creates custom analytical tools
* Uses APIs to access and integrate data
* Implements error handling and logging
* Writes automated tests for code
* Uses version control collaboratively
* Optimizes code for performance

2.4.5 Time series analysis Analyzing data collected over time to identify trends, cycles, or changes that might not be obvious from single snapshots.
Job Tasks:
1. Analyze long-term temperature, precipitation, or streamflow records
2. Detect trends, cycles, and anomalies in ecological monitoring datasets
3. Apply forecasting techniques to anticipate resource needs or environmental stress
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands time series concepts (trend, seasonality, autocorrelation) Application: Visualizes and describes time series Indicators:
* Plots time series data appropriately
* Calculates moving averages and smoothing
* Identifies obvious trends and patterns
* Understands temporal autocorrelation
* Recognizes seasonal patterns
* Compares time series visually
* Documents temporal data characteristics
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies time series analysis methods Application: Conducts time series analyses Indicators:
* Decomposes time series (trend, seasonal, residual)
* Tests for trends (Mann-Kendall, linear regression)
* Analyzes autocorrelation and partial autocorrelation
* Identifies changepoints and breakpoints
* Uses time series software (R forecast package, Python statsmodels)
* Detects anomalies in time series
* Accounts for missing data in time series
* Compares multiple time series
* Interprets time series results for management
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in time series methods and forecasting Application: Develops sophisticated time series models Indicators:
* Fits ARIMA and state-space models
* Implements multivariate time series analysis
* Uses dynamic linear models and Kalman filtering
* Develops forecasting models
* Analyzes non-stationary and irregular time series
* Uses spectral analysis for periodicities
* Implements intervention analysis
* Publishes time series research
* Trains practitioners in time series methods
* Develops early warning systems based on time series
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes time series analysis practice Application: Provides leadership on temporal analysis Indicators:
* Develops innovative time series approaches
* Influences time series standards and methods
* Leads major time series initiatives
* Publishes influential time series research
* Provides expert time series consultation
* Advances temporal analysis in environmental science
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Trend monitoring, climate analysis, population dynamics Private Sector: Demand forecasting, quality control, operations monitoring NGOs: Long-term monitoring, impact evaluation, trend detection Consulting: Time series analysis services, forecasting Academia: Time series research, climate science, population ecology

2.6 AI-ENHANCED ENVIRONMENTAL MONITORING
Using artificial intelligence to automate and improve environmental observation and analysis
Skills related to applying AI and machine learning specifically to environmental monitoring, from automated species detection to predictive analytics for resource management.
________________


2.6.1 Computer vision for environmental applications Using AI to automatically identify and classify objects in images and video, such as species identification from camera traps or vegetation mapping from drone imagery.
Job Tasks:
1. Train computer vision models to identify species from camera trap images
2. Use object detection algorithms to count wildlife or map vegetation from aerial imagery
3. Validate and improve model accuracy using ground-truth data
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands computer vision concepts and applications Application: Uses pre-trained computer vision models Indicators:
* Explains how computer vision works (CNNs, object detection)
* Uses existing CV models for classification
* Prepares image datasets for training
* Understands concepts like training/validation/test splits
* Labels images for supervised learning
* Uses image augmentation techniques
* Recognizes when CV is appropriate for problems
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies computer vision methods and architectures Application: Trains and deploys CV models Indicators:
* Trains image classification models
* Uses object detection (YOLO, Faster R-CNN)
* Implements semantic segmentation for habitat mapping
* Uses transfer learning with pre-trained models
* Fine-tunes models for specific species or features
* Evaluates model performance (precision, recall, mAP)
* Deploys models for batch processing of images
* Uses PyTorch or TensorFlow for CV
* Processes camera trap, satellite, or drone imagery with AI
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in computer vision architectures and optimization Application: Develops sophisticated CV systems Indicators:
* Develops custom CV architectures
* Implements instance segmentation and tracking
* Uses attention mechanisms and transformers for vision
* Deploys models to edge devices (cameras, drones)
* Implements active learning to improve models efficiently
* Addresses class imbalance and rare species detection
* Develops real-time CV applications
* Publishes CV methodology research
* Trains practitioners in computer vision
* Ensures ethical and responsible AI use
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes computer vision applications in environmental science Application: Provides CV leadership and innovation Indicators:
* Develops novel CV approaches for environmental problems
* Influences CV standards and best practices
* Leads major CV initiatives
* Publishes influential CV research
* Provides expert CV consultation
* Advances responsible CV in environmental monitoring
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Automated species monitoring, vegetation mapping, infrastructure inspection Private Sector: Crop monitoring, quality control, automated inspections NGOs: Wildlife monitoring, deforestation detection, conservation automation Consulting: CV analysis services, model development Academia: CV research, biodiversity monitoring, method development
________________


2.6.2 Acoustic monitoring and analysis with AI Using machine learning to process and interpret environmental sounds, from bird songs to underwater acoustics, enabling continuous monitoring at scales impossible with human observers.
Job Tasks:

3. Analyze acoustic data to assess biodiversity patterns and detect rare species

* Trains acoustic classifiers for species detection
* Uses deep learning for sound classification

* Calculates acoustic indices and soundscape metrics
* Uses tools like Raven, Kaleidoscope, or custom Python
* Validates model performance with manual verification
* Analyzes temporal patterns in acoustic data
* Integrates acoustic data with other monitoring
* Trains others in acoustic monitoring
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in bioacoustics and acoustic AI Application: Develops acoustic monitoring systems Indicators:
* Develops custom acoustic detection models

Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Bird and bat monitoring, marine mammal surveys, soundscape management Private Sector: Environmental compliance monitoring, noise assessment NGOs: Biodiversity monitoring, conservation effectiveness, rare species detection Consulting: Acoustic surveys, impact monitoring Academia: Bioacoustics research, acoustic ecology

2.6.5 AI model interpretation and explainability Understanding and communicating how AI models make decisions, ensuring transparency and trust in AI-assisted environmental management.

2. Visualize model decision-making processes for stakeholders and managers
3. Ensure AI systems are transparent and accountable in environmental decisions

* Explains what a model predicts
* Identifies key input variables
* Understands model limitations
* Recognizes when models are "black boxes"
* Communicates model outputs clearly
* Questions unexpected predictions
* Documents model assumptions
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies interpretability methods Application: Analyzes and explains model behavior Indicators:
* Uses feature importance methods
* Creates partial dependence plots
* Implements SHAP values for interpretation
* Uses LIME for local explanations
* Visualizes model decisions
* Identifies when models use spurious correlations
* Communicates model reasoning to non-technical audiences
* Documents model interpretability
* Evaluates trade-offs between accuracy and interpretability
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in explainable AI and model transparency Application: Ensures AI transparency in systems Indicators:
* Implements comprehensive model interpretation frameworks
* Uses attention mechanisms and saliency maps
* Develops model cards documenting AI systems
* Ensures AI fairness and bias detection
* Uses counterfactual explanations
* Balances model performance with interpretability
* Develops interpretability tools and visualizations
* Publishes on explainable AI
* Trains practitioners in model interpretation
* Addresses ethical implications of AI decisions
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes explainable AI practice Application: Provides leadership on AI transparency Indicators:
* Develops innovative interpretability approaches
* Influences AI transparency standards
* Leads responsible AI initiatives
* Publishes influential explainable AI research

* Publishes influential 3D modeling research
* Provides expert 3D modeling consultation
* Advances 3D modeling in environmental applications
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Planning visualization, impact assessment, public engagement Private Sector: Site planning, visual impact assessment, design visualization NGOs: Conservation visualization, before/after scenarios Consulting: 3D modeling services, environmental visualization Academia: Research visualization, landscape architecture, teaching
DOMAIN 3: MANAGEMENT & ADMINISTRATIVE SKILLS
Organizing people, resources, and projects to achieve stewardship goals
DOMAIN OVERVIEW
Management and administrative skills transform scientific knowledge and technical capabilities into real-world conservation and sustainability outcomes. This domain encompasses the essential skills for planning, organizing, leading, and administering environmental programs—from managing individual projects to leading organizational strategy. These skills ensure that environmental work is completed effectively, efficiently, and with lasting impact.
This domain encompasses seven skill areas spanning from project execution to strategic leadership, financial stewardship to emerging sustainability frameworks. While some professionals focus on operational management, increasingly complex environmental challenges require integration of traditional management with innovation in corporate sustainability, circular economy thinking, and adaptive approaches. Effective environmental leaders combine management competence with deep understanding of ecological systems and stakeholder needs.
Example Positions Within This Domain
Professionals whose work falls primarily within Domain 3 include (but are not limited to):
Project & Program Management Roles:
* Project Manager / Environmental Project Manager
* Program Manager / Program Coordinator
* Conservation Program Manager
* Restoration Project Manager
* Grant Manager
* Contract Manager
* Portfolio Manager
Strategic Planning & Leadership Roles:
* Strategic Planning Director
* Conservation Director
* Executive Director (NGO)
* Chief Operating Officer
* Vice President of Operations
* Program Director
* Planning Manager
Resource & Administrative Roles:
* Administrative Manager
* Operations Manager
* Human Resources Manager (Environmental Organizations)
* Facilities Manager
* Procurement Manager
* Administrative Services Director
Financial Management Roles:
* Finance Manager / Financial Analyst
* Budget Manager
* Controller
* Chief Financial Officer
* Grants Administrator
* Fee Program Manager
Leadership & Organizational Development Roles:
* Executive Director
* Chief Executive Officer
* Deputy Director
* Change Management Specialist
* Organizational Development Consultant
* Leadership Coach (Environmental Sector)
Corporate Sustainability & ESG Roles:
* Sustainability Manager / Director
* Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Manager
* ESG (Environmental, Social, Governance) Manager
* Chief Sustainability Officer
* Sustainability Analyst
* ESG Reporting Specialist
* Circular Economy Manager
Circular Economy & Systems Roles:
* Circular Economy Specialist
* Zero Waste Coordinator
* Waste Reduction Manager
* Sustainable Supply Chain Manager
* Product Stewardship Manager
* Materials Management Specialist
How Advanced Technologies Are Changing This Domain
Management and administrative practices are being transformed by digital tools and data-driven approaches:
Digital Project Management:
* Cloud-based project management platforms enable real-time collaboration across dispersed teams
* AI-powered scheduling optimizes resource allocation and identifies bottlenecks
* Automated reporting reduces administrative burden
* Mobile apps enable field-based project tracking
* Integration of project management with GIS and monitoring systems
* Predictive analytics forecast project risks and delays
* Digital twins simulate project implementation and outcomes
Financial Management Innovation:
* Blockchain enables transparent tracking of conservation finance
* AI detects anomalies and fraud in financial transactions
* Automated accounting reduces manual bookkeeping
* Real-time financial dashboards improve decision-making
* Digital payment systems streamline transactions
* Cloud-based financial systems accessible from anywhere
* Predictive modeling forecasts financial needs and scenarios
Data-Driven Leadership:
* Business intelligence dashboards provide real-time organizational metrics
* Predictive analytics inform strategic planning
* Sentiment analysis reveals organizational culture insights
* Network analysis maps stakeholder relationships
* Machine learning identifies patterns in organizational data
* Digital feedback systems enable continuous improvement
* Data visualization communicates complex information to boards
ESG & Sustainability Technology:
* IoT sensors track environmental metrics continuously
* Blockchain verifies sustainability claims and supply chains
* AI analyzes ESG risk and opportunities
* Automated sustainability reporting platforms
* Carbon accounting software quantifies emissions
* Life cycle assessment tools evaluate product impacts
* Digital platforms connect buyers and suppliers of sustainable products
Virtual & Hybrid Management:
* Virtual meeting platforms enable distributed teams
* Digital collaboration tools maintain team cohesion remotely
* AI-powered translation breaks language barriers
* Virtual reality enables immersive training and team building
* Digital whiteboards facilitate creative collaboration
* Time zone management tools coordinate global teams
* Remote work monitoring balances productivity and autonomy
Circular Economy Platforms:
* Material marketplaces connect waste generators with users
* Digital product passports track materials through lifecycles
* IoT enables product-as-a-service business models
* Blockchain tracks recycled content and certifications
* AI optimizes reverse logistics and material flows
* Platform models enable sharing economy
* Digital twins optimize circular systems
Challenges & Considerations:
These technological advances create management challenges:
* Digital divide: Not all staff have equal technology access or comfort
* Change fatigue: Continuous technology adoption exhausts organizations
* Data overload: Too many metrics can obscure key insights
* Privacy concerns: Monitoring systems may feel invasive
* Technology dependence: System failures disrupt operations
* Cost: Advanced tools require significant investment
* Integration complexity: Multiple systems may not communicate well
* Skills gaps: Staff may lack technical management skills
* Human connection: Virtual work can erode organizational culture
* Vendor lock-in: Proprietary systems limit flexibility
Future-Ready Management Skills:
Environmental managers increasingly need:
* Data literacy and dashboard interpretation
* Digital project management tools proficiency
* Change management for technology adoption
* Virtual team leadership and remote management
* Data-informed decision-making
* ESG frameworks and sustainability reporting
* Circular economy business models
* Systems thinking for complex challenges
* Adaptive management and learning orientation
* Balance of technology with human relationships
* Critical evaluation of management fads and tools
* Ethical leadership in digital age
The most effective managers blend technological capability with traditional management fundamentals—using technology to enhance efficiency and insight while maintaining human-centered leadership and adaptive approaches.
Cross-Sector Applications
Management and administrative skills are universally essential across all environmental sectors:
Public Agencies: Program management, budget administration, strategic planning, personnel management Private Sector: Corporate sustainability, ESG management, operations, project delivery NGOs: Nonprofit management, fundraising, program development, organizational strategy Consulting: Client project management, business development, firm operations Academia: Grant management, lab administration, program coordination, institutional leadership Tribal Nations: Tribal enterprise management, program administration, resource management
Career Progression in This Domain
Professionals typically develop management capabilities along three dimensions:
1. Scope of Responsibility: Individual contributor → project lead → program manager → organizational leader
2. Resource Scale: Small projects → major initiatives → enterprise portfolios
3. Strategic Influence: Operational execution → tactical planning → strategic direction
________________


DOMAIN-LEVEL CAREER PATHWAYS
Pathway 1: Project/Program Manager to Director Entry → Mid → Senior → Executive
Entry (0-3 years): Project Coordinator, Program Assistant, Junior Project Manager
* Focus: 3.1 (Project Management), basic administrative skills
* Typical setting: Any environmental organization
Mid (3-7 years): Project Manager, Program Manager, Grant Manager
* Focus: 3.1, 3.2 (Strategic Planning), 3.4 (Financial Management)
* Typical setting: Managing specific programs or projects
Senior (7-15 years): Senior Program Manager, Director of Programs, Portfolio Manager
* Focus: 3.1, 3.2, 3.4, 3.5 (Leadership), managing multiple initiatives
* Typical setting: Program leadership, managing managers
Executive (15+ years): Vice President, Chief Operating Officer, Executive Director
* Focus: Organizational strategy, all management domains
* Typical setting: Senior organizational leadership
Pathway 2: Administrative/Operations Manager to COO Entry → Mid → Senior → Executive
Entry (0-3 years): Administrative Coordinator, Operations Assistant
* Focus: 3.3 (Resource Administration), basic 3.4
* Typical setting: Organizational support roles
Mid (3-7 years): Administrative Manager, Operations Manager, HR Manager
* Focus: 3.3, 3.4, 3.5 (people management)
* Typical setting: Managing organizational operations
Senior (7-15 years): Director of Operations, Director of Administration
* Focus: 3.3, 3.4, 3.5, strategic operations
* Typical setting: Senior operations leadership
Executive (15+ years): Chief Operating Officer, Deputy Executive Director
* Focus: Organizational effectiveness, all operations
* Typical setting: C-suite leadership
Pathway 3: Sustainability/ESG Specialist to Chief Sustainability Officer Entry → Mid → Senior → Executive
Entry (0-3 years): Sustainability Coordinator, ESG Analyst, CSR Assistant
* Focus: 3.6 (Corporate Sustainability & ESG), 3.7 (Circular Economy)
* Typical setting: Corporate sustainability departments, NGOs
Mid (3-7 years): Sustainability Manager, ESG Manager, Circular Economy Manager
* Focus: 3.6, 3.7, 3.1 (project management)
* Typical setting: Leading sustainability programs
Senior (7-15 years): Director of Sustainability, Head of ESG, Circular Economy Director
* Focus: 3.6, 3.7, 3.2 (strategic planning), 3.5 (leadership)
* Typical setting: Senior sustainability leadership
Executive (15+ years): Chief Sustainability Officer, VP of Sustainability
* Focus: Enterprise sustainability strategy
* Typical setting: C-suite or senior executive
Pathway 4: Finance Manager to Chief Financial Officer Entry → Mid → Senior → Executive
Entry (0-3 years): Budget Analyst, Accounting Coordinator, Grants Specialist
* Focus: 3.4 (Financial Management)
* Typical setting: Finance departments
Mid (3-7 years): Finance Manager, Budget Manager, Controller
* Focus: 3.4, 3.1 (project budgeting), 3.3 (resource management)
* Typical setting: Managing organizational finances
Senior (7-15 years): Director of Finance, Controller, Senior Financial Manager
* Focus: 3.4, 3.2 (financial strategy), 3.5 (leadership)
* Typical setting: Senior financial leadership
Executive (15+ years): Chief Financial Officer, VP of Finance
* Focus: Organizational financial strategy
* Typical setting: C-suite leadership
________________


REPRESENTATIVE JOB ROLE PROFILES
Profile 1: Environmental Project Manager (Mid-Career) Typical Employers: Federal/state agencies, NGOs, consulting firms, corporations
Primary Skill Areas: 3.1 (Project Management), 3.4 (Financial Management), 3.5 (Leadership)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 3.1.1-3.1.5: Advanced level
* 3.4.1-3.4.2: Intermediate to Advanced
* 3.5.1, 3.5.4: Intermediate
Typical Responsibilities:
* Manage environmental projects from initiation through completion
* Develop project plans, timelines, and budgets
* Coordinate cross-functional teams and contractors
* Monitor project progress and adjust plans as needed
* Manage project risks and resolve issues
* Report to stakeholders and leadership
* Ensure projects meet environmental and quality standards
Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's in Environmental Science, Management, or related field
* PMP (Project Management Professional) certification preferred
* 3-7 years project management experience
Salary Range: $60,000-$90,000
________________


Profile 2: Sustainability Manager (Mid-Senior) Typical Employers: Corporations, large NGOs, universities, municipalities
Primary Skill Areas: 3.6 (Corporate Sustainability & ESG), 3.7 (Circular Economy), 3.1 (Project Management)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 3.6.1-3.6.5: Advanced level
* 3.7.1-3.7.3: Intermediate to Advanced
* 3.1.1-3.1.3: Intermediate
Typical Responsibilities:
* Develop and implement organizational sustainability strategies
* Manage ESG reporting and disclosure (GRI, SASB, TCFD)
* Set and track sustainability goals and metrics
* Engage employees in sustainability initiatives
* Manage sustainability projects and programs
* Report to leadership, board, and external stakeholders
* Identify sustainability risks and opportunities
* Build partnerships for sustainability initiatives
Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's or Master's in Sustainability, Environmental Management, Business
* LEED AP, TRUE, or other sustainability certifications
* Experience with ESG frameworks and reporting
Salary Range: $70,000-$110,000
________________


Profile 3: Conservation Program Director (Senior) Typical Employers: Large NGOs, foundations, agencies
Primary Skill Areas: 3.2 (Strategic Planning), 3.5 (Leadership), 3.1 (Project Management), 3.4 (Financial Management)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 3.2.1-3.2.5: Advanced level
* 3.5.1-3.5.5: Advanced
* 3.1.5, 3.4.1-3.4.3: Advanced
Typical Responsibilities:
* Develop strategic vision and priorities for conservation programs
* Lead program teams and manage multiple projects
* Oversee program budgets ($5M-$50M+)
* Build and maintain partner relationships
* Secure program funding through grants and fundraising
* Represent organization with external stakeholders
* Evaluate program effectiveness and impact
* Mentor and develop program staff
Related Credentials:
* Master's degree in related field often required
* 10-15 years progressive experience
* Track record of successful program leadership
Salary Range: $90,000-$150,000
________________


Profile 4: Chief Operating Officer (Executive) Typical Employers: Large NGOs, agencies, environmental organizations
Primary Skill Areas: All Domain 3 skill areas, organizational strategy
Proficiency Requirements:
* 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5: Expert level
* 3.1: Advanced
* Strong cross-domain integration
Typical Responsibilities:
* Oversee all organizational operations
* Develop and implement operational strategy
* Manage senior leadership team
* Ensure organizational effectiveness and efficiency
* Oversee budget development and financial management
* Lead organizational change and improvement
* Ensure HR, facilities, IT, and administrative functions
* Report to CEO/Executive Director and Board
Related Credentials:
* Master's degree (MBA, MPA, or related)
* 15-20+ years progressive leadership experience
* Track record of organizational leadership success
Salary Range: $120,000-$250,000+
________________


Profile 5: Chief Sustainability Officer (Executive) Typical Employers: Major corporations, large institutions, global NGOs
Primary Skill Areas: 3.6 (Corporate Sustainability & ESG), 3.7 (Circular Economy), 3.2 (Strategic Planning), 3.5 (Leadership)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 3.6.1-3.6.5: Expert level
* 3.7.1-3.7.5: Advanced to Expert
* 3.2, 3.5: Expert
Typical Responsibilities:
* Lead enterprise sustainability strategy
* Oversee ESG performance and reporting
* Advise CEO and Board on sustainability issues
* Manage sustainability team and budget
* Set ambitious sustainability goals
* Drive circular economy transformation
* Engage investors, customers, and stakeholders on sustainability
* Influence public policy on sustainability
* Build sustainability into business strategy and operations
Related Credentials:
* Master's degree (MBA, Sustainability, or related)
* 15-20+ years sustainability leadership experience
* Recognition as sustainability leader in sector
Salary Range: $150,000-$400,000+ (varies widely by company size)

3.1 PROJECT MANAGEMENT
Planning and overseeing conservation work from start to finish
Skills related to organizing and managing specific land management projects to ensure they're completed on time, within budget, and achieve their intended outcomes.
________________


3.1.1 Project planning and scheduling Breaking down complex conservation work into manageable tasks, creating timelines, and determining the resources needed.
Job Tasks:
1. Break down large conservation efforts into phased implementation plans
2. Develop and maintain Gantt charts to track project timelines and dependencies
3. Coordinate with cross-functional teams to align fieldwork with seasonal windows
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands project management basics and terminology Application: Assists with project planning activities Indicators:
* Contributes to project task lists
* Updates project schedules with status
* Understands project phases and milestones
* Uses project management software (MS Project, Asana, Smartsheet)
* Identifies task dependencies
* Recognizes schedule conflicts
* Participates in project planning meetings
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies project management methods systematically Application: Develops project plans independently Indicators:
* Creates comprehensive project plans
* Develops realistic schedules with critical paths
* Identifies resource needs and constraints
* Uses work breakdown structures (WBS)
* Coordinates multiple workstreams
* Manages schedule changes and impacts
* Creates Gantt charts and network diagrams
* Aligns projects with organizational priorities
* Facilitates project kickoff meetings
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in project planning methodologies (Waterfall, Agile, Hybrid) Application: Manages complex, multi-year projects Indicators:
* Plans programs with multiple interrelated projects
* Optimizes schedules across portfolio
* Uses advanced PM techniques (earned value, critical chain)
* Develops program-level roadmaps
* Manages dependencies across projects
* Plans for uncertainty and incorporates buffers
* Mentors project managers in planning
* Develops organizational PM standards
* Selects appropriate PM methodologies for contexts
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes project management practice in environmental field Application: Provides strategic PM leadership Indicators:
* Develops innovative PM approaches for environmental work
* Influences PM standards and best practices
* Leads enterprise project management offices
* Provides expert PM consultation
* Publishes on environmental project management
* Advances PM in environmental sector
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Restoration project planning, land acquisition phasing Private Sector: Environmental compliance projects, sustainability initiatives NGOs: Conservation project planning, campaign scheduling Consulting: Client project planning, proposal development Academia: Research project planning, grant project management
________________


3.1.2 Budget development and management Creating financial plans for projects and tracking expenses to ensure work can be completed with available funds.
Job Tasks:
1. Prepare project budgets that align with grant or program requirements
2. Track actual costs against projected expenses and update forecasts
3. Justify budget adjustments based on project scope changes or risks
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands budget categories and cost tracking Application: Tracks project expenses Indicators:
* Records project expenditures accurately
* Uses budget tracking templates
* Compares actual to budgeted costs
* Identifies budget variances
* Maintains expense documentation
* Follows procurement procedures
* Understands budget line items
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies budgeting principles and cost estimation Application: Develops and manages project budgets Indicators:
* Develops realistic project budgets
* Estimates costs using historical data
* Allocates indirect costs appropriately
* Forecasts remaining project costs
* Manages budget contingencies
* Identifies cost-saving opportunities
* Prepares budget justifications
* Manages budget changes and amendments
* Reports financial status to stakeholders
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in project finance and cost management Application: Manages complex, multi-source budgets Indicators:
* Develops budgets for large, complex projects
* Manages multiple funding sources
* Optimizes resource allocation across projects
* Conducts cost-benefit analyses
* Uses earned value management
* Forecasts long-term project finances
* Negotiates budgets with funders and contractors
* Trains project managers in budgeting
* Develops budget management systems
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes financial management in environmental programs Application: Provides strategic financial leadership for projects Indicators:
* Develops innovative project financing approaches
* Influences budget practices and standards
* Oversees program and portfolio budgets
* Provides expert financial consultation
* Advances project financial management
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Grant budgets, appropriated funds management Private Sector: Project budgets, capital expenditures NGOs: Grant budgets, fundraising-linked project finance Consulting: Project budgets, client billing, profit management Academia: Grant budgets, research project finance
________________


3.1.3 Risk assessment and mitigation Identifying potential problems that could affect project success and developing strategies to prevent or address them.
Job Tasks:
1. Identify risks such as weather delays or regulatory barriers early in project design
2. Create contingency plans and mitigation strategies for critical risks
3. Review project progress regularly and adjust based on emerging issues
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic risk concepts Application: Identifies obvious project risks Indicators:
* Lists potential project risks
* Recognizes when issues arise
* Follows risk mitigation procedures
* Escalates risks appropriately
* Documents risk events
* Participates in risk discussions
* Understands common environmental project risks
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies risk management frameworks Application: Conducts systematic risk assessments Indicators:
* Conducts risk identification workshops
* Assesses risk likelihood and impact
* Develops risk matrices and registers
* Creates risk mitigation plans
* Assigns risk owners
* Monitors risks throughout project
* Develops contingency plans
* Uses risk management software
* Reports on risk status
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in risk management and uncertainty Application: Manages complex, multi-faceted risks Indicators:
* Conducts quantitative risk analysis
* Develops sophisticated mitigation strategies
* Manages portfolio-level risks
* Uses Monte Carlo simulation for risk modeling
* Integrates risk management with decision-making
* Develops risk management frameworks
* Trains project managers in risk assessment
* Manages risks in highly uncertain environments
* Creates risk-aware organizational culture
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes risk management practice Application: Provides strategic risk leadership Indicators:
* Develops innovative risk management approaches
* Influences risk management standards
* Leads enterprise risk management
* Provides expert risk consultation
* Publishes on risk management
* Advances risk management in environmental field
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Environmental compliance risks, regulatory risks Private Sector: Project delivery risks, liability management NGOs: Funding risks, partnership risks, reputational risks Consulting: Client project risks, contractual risks Academia: Research risks, compliance risks


3.1.4 Contract and agreement administration Managing formal arrangements with vendors, partners, or contractors who provide services for land management projects.
Job Tasks:
1. Draft and review scopes of work for contractors and partners
2. Manage invoicing and compliance tracking for contract deliverables
3. Ensure agreements meet procurement, legal, and environmental requirements
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands contract basics and terms Application: Assists with contract administration Indicators:
* Maintains contract files and documentation
* Tracks contract deliverables
* Reviews invoices against contracts
* Understands contract terms and conditions
* Follows contract procedures
* Communicates with contractors appropriately
* Identifies contract issues for escalation
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies contracting principles and procedures Application: Manages contracts independently Indicators:
* Drafts scopes of work
* Reviews and negotiates contract terms
* Manages contractor performance
* Ensures contract compliance
* Handles contract modifications
* Resolves contract issues
* Coordinates with legal and procurement staff
* Documents contract administration
* Ensures environmental requirements in contracts
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in contract law and negotiation Application: Manages complex contracting programs Indicators:
* Develops contracting strategies
* Negotiates complex agreements
* Manages multiple concurrent contracts
* Develops contract templates and standards
* Handles contract disputes and claims
* Ensures regulatory and legal compliance
* Trains staff in contract management
* Develops contracting policies
* Manages high-value or high-risk contracts
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes contracting practice Application: Provides strategic contracting leadership Indicators:
* Develops innovative contracting approaches
* Influences contracting policy
* Leads enterprise contracting programs
* Provides expert contract consultation
* Manages major partnership agreements
* Advances contracting in environmental sector
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Federal/state procurement, cooperative agreements Private Sector: Vendor contracts, subcontracts, service agreements NGOs: Grant agreements, partnership MOUs, service contracts Consulting: Subcontractor management, client contracts Academia: Research agreements, institutional contracts
________________

3. Prepare final project evaluations and lessons learned summaries

* Participates in evaluation meetings

INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies M&E frameworks and methods Application: Designs and implements project M&E Indicators:
* Develops project indicators and metrics
* Designs M&E plans
* Analyzes project performance data
* Conducts project evaluations
* Uses logic models and theories of change
* Reports results to stakeholders
* Documents lessons learned
* Adjusts projects based on monitoring
* Uses M&E software and tools
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in evaluation methods and adaptive management Application: Leads M&E programs and evaluations Indicators:

* Facilitates adaptive management processes
* Evaluates programs and portfolios
* Develops organizational M&E frameworks
* Trains practitioners in M&E

EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes M&E practice in environmental field Application: Provides M&E strategic leadership Indicators:

3.2 STRATEGIC PLANNING
Creating long-term visions and plans for land management
Skills related to developing the big-picture direction for land management programs and the practical steps to achieve long-term goals.
________________

3. Compile organizational assessment reports to inform leadership decisions

* Contributes to SWOT analyses
* Collects assessment data
* Participates in assessment discussions
