DOMAIN 4: FIELD OPERATIONS SKILLS SKILL AREAS
________________


4.1 FIELD ASSESSMENT & MONITORING
Systematically observing and documenting conditions on the ground
Skills related to collecting accurate field data about ecosystems, species, and landscape conditions to inform management decisions.
________________


4.1.1 Vegetation sampling and assessment
Measuring plant communities and characteristics using standardized methods to track changes over time.
Job Tasks:
1. Establish vegetation monitoring plots and collect data on species composition, cover, and density
2. Use digital data collection tools and GPS to record precise plot locations and measurements
3. Identify plant species in the field using keys, apps, or expert consultation
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands basic vegetation sampling concepts and common plant identification
* Application: Conducts vegetation surveys following established protocols
* Indicators:
   * Sets up monitoring plots according to protocols
   * Accurately records species presence and basic cover estimates
   * Uses plant identification guides and apps effectively
   * Collects voucher specimens when needed for verification
   * Operates GPS and mobile data collection devices
   * Recognizes when environmental conditions affect data quality
   * Maintains organized field notes and photographs
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies vegetation ecology principles; proficient in regional flora
* Application: Designs sampling strategies and leads field crews
* Indicators:
   * Selects appropriate sampling methods for objectives and vegetation types
   * Identifies most regional plant species to genus or species in the field
   * Estimates cover, density, and frequency accurately
   * Recognizes vegetation condition and successional stage
   * Adapts protocols to site-specific conditions appropriately
   * Trains and supervises field crew members
   * Uses vegetation data to assess management effects
   * Integrates drone or satellite imagery with ground data
   * Ensures data quality through validation and review
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in vegetation assessment methodology and plant ecology
* Application: Develops monitoring programs and analyzes complex vegetation patterns
* Indicators:
   * Designs comprehensive vegetation monitoring programs
   * Analyzes long-term vegetation trends and management responses
   * Uses multivariate analysis to interpret vegetation patterns
   * Integrates multiple data sources (field, remote sensing, historical)
   * Publishes or presents vegetation assessment results
   * Develops or adapts monitoring protocols for novel conditions
   * Trains practitioners in vegetation sampling methods
   * Uses vegetation data to guide adaptive management
   * Recognizes rare or sensitive plant communities requiring special protection
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes vegetation monitoring practices in field
* Application: Provides strategic leadership on vegetation assessment programs
* Indicators:
   * Directs large-scale vegetation monitoring initiatives
   * Develops innovative monitoring approaches adopted widely
   * Influences vegetation monitoring standards and protocols
   * Provides expert consultation on complex vegetation issues
   * Publishes influential vegetation research and methods
   * Achieves recognition for vegetation monitoring excellence
   * Mentors vegetation monitoring professionals across organizations
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Rangeland monitoring, forest inventory, threatened plant surveys
* Private Sector: Environmental compliance monitoring, restoration success tracking
* NGOs: Preserve monitoring, invasive species tracking, habitat assessment
* Consulting: Vegetation surveys for NEPA, baseline documentation
* Academia: Research plots, experimental treatments, long-term ecological research
________________


4.1.2 Wildlife observation and sign interpretation
Detecting and recording wildlife presence, abundance, and behavior through direct observation and indirect evidence.
Job Tasks:
1. Conduct wildlife surveys using appropriate methods (transects, point counts, camera traps)
2. Identify and interpret wildlife sign (tracks, scat, nests, feeding sign)
3. Use digital tools to document observations and manage wildlife data
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Familiar with common regional wildlife and basic survey methods
* Application: Conducts wildlife surveys following protocols
* Indicators:
   * Identifies common wildlife species by sight and sound
   * Recognizes obvious wildlife sign (tracks, scat, nests)
   * Follows survey protocols accurately and consistently
   * Records observations using standardized datasheets or apps
   * Uses binoculars, spotting scopes, and field guides effectively
   * Deploys and checks camera traps following protocols
   * Understands observer bias and detection probability concepts
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Proficient in wildlife identification; understands survey design
* Application: Designs and implements wildlife monitoring programs
* Indicators:
   * Selects appropriate survey methods for species and objectives
   * Identifies wildlife to species from subtle sign or brief observations
   * Interprets behavior and habitat use from observations and sign
   * Uses distance sampling and occupancy modeling concepts
   * Manages camera trap data and uses AI identification tools
   * Trains field crews in wildlife survey techniques
   * Analyzes survey data to estimate abundance or trends
   * Integrates wildlife observations with habitat data
   * Uses drones or thermal imaging for wildlife surveys when appropriate
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in wildlife survey methodology and population ecology
* Application: Leads comprehensive wildlife monitoring programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs statistically rigorous survey programs
   * Uses advanced techniques (mark-recapture, telemetry, eDNA)
   * Analyzes population trends and habitat relationships
   * Implements multi-species monitoring efficiently
   * Uses occupancy modeling and N-mixture models
   * Evaluates survey effectiveness and optimizes methods
   * Publishes wildlife monitoring results
   * Develops wildlife monitoring protocols and standards
   * Integrates citizen science with professional surveys
   * Uses wildlife data to inform adaptive management
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes wildlife monitoring practices at landscape scale
* Application: Directs major wildlife assessment initiatives
* Indicators:
   * Leads regional or national wildlife monitoring programs
   * Develops innovative monitoring approaches adopted widely
   * Influences wildlife monitoring policy and standards
   * Provides expert consultation on complex wildlife issues
   * Publishes influential wildlife monitoring research
   * Achieves recognition for wildlife assessment excellence
   * Mentors wildlife monitoring professionals
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Species inventory, population monitoring, endangered species surveys
* Private Sector: Wildlife surveys for development projects, mine reclamation monitoring
* NGOs: Conservation monitoring, rare species tracking, wildlife corridor assessment
* Consulting: Baseline wildlife surveys, impact monitoring, mitigation effectiveness
* Academia: Research surveys, population studies, wildlife ecology
________________


4.1.3 Water quality testing and aquatic surveys Measuring physical, chemical, and biological characteristics of water bodies and streams.
Job Tasks:
1. Collect water samples and conduct field measurements (temperature, pH, dissolved oxygen, turbidity)
2. Conduct aquatic biological surveys (macroinvertebrates, fish, algae)
3. Use water quality sensors and data logging equipment
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic water quality parameters and sampling procedures Application: Collects water samples and conducts field measurements Indicators:
* Measures field parameters accurately using calibrated equipment
* Collects water samples following proper protocols
* Maintains chain of custody for laboratory samples
* Records data accurately with appropriate metadata
* Recognizes obvious water quality problems (algae blooms, pollution)
* Maintains and calibrates field equipment
* Follows safety protocols for water work
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies water quality assessment methods; understands aquatic ecology Application: Designs and implements water quality monitoring programs Indicators:
* Designs sampling strategies appropriate to objectives and water bodies
* Conducts biological assessments (macroinvertebrate, fish surveys)
* Uses water quality data to assess stream health and impairment
* Deploys and manages water quality sensors and data loggers
* Analyzes water quality trends over time
* Identifies pollution sources using multiple lines of evidence
* Coordinates with laboratories for sample analysis
* Uses water quality data to inform management actions
* Integrates eDNA sampling for aquatic species detection
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in aquatic monitoring and water quality science Application: Leads comprehensive aquatic assessment programs Indicators:
* Develops watershed-scale water quality monitoring programs
* Uses multiple assessment methods (chemical, physical, biological)
* Conducts load calculations and pollutant source tracking
* Evaluates water quality against standards and criteria
* Uses real-time sensor networks for continuous monitoring
* Implements automated data validation and quality control
* Analyzes complex water quality datasets
* Develops water quality improvement recommendations
* Publishes water quality assessment results
* Trains practitioners in aquatic monitoring methods
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes water quality monitoring practices Application: Directs major water quality assessment initiatives Indicators:
* Leads regional water quality monitoring networks
* Develops innovative aquatic assessment approaches
* Influences water quality monitoring standards and methods
* Provides expert consultation on water quality issues
* Publishes influential water quality research
* Achieves recognition for aquatic monitoring excellence
* Advances integration of technology in water quality assessment
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Stream monitoring, lake assessments, drinking water protection Private Sector: Compliance monitoring, stormwater quality, industrial discharge NGOs: Watershed monitoring, stream restoration assessment, volunteer monitoring Consulting: Water quality surveys, TMDL studies, mitigation monitoring Academia: Research monitoring, experimental treatments, water quality methods
________________


4.1.4 Soil characterization and sampling Assessing soil properties, health, and function through field observation and sampling.
Job Tasks:
1. Describe soil profiles and horizons using standard terminology and methods
2. Collect soil samples for laboratory analysis (texture, nutrients, carbon, contaminants)
3. Conduct field tests of soil properties (texture by feel, infiltration, compaction)
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic soil concepts and sampling procedures Application: Collects soil samples and describes simple profiles Indicators:
* Digs and describes soil profile pits following protocols
* Identifies major soil horizons and properties
* Collects representative soil samples avoiding contamination
* Uses soil texture by feel method
* Records soil observations using standard forms
* Uses GPS to document sampling locations accurately
* Follows laboratory submission procedures
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies soil science principles; understands soil-plant-water relationships Application: Conducts comprehensive soil assessments Indicators:
* Describes soils using official NRCS methodology
* Classifies soils to subgroup level when needed
* Conducts soil health assessments using multiple indicators
* Designs soil sampling strategies for different objectives
* Interprets soil test results for management recommendations
* Uses field tests (infiltration, penetrometer, aggregate stability)
* Recognizes soil problems (compaction, erosion, contamination)
* Integrates soil data with vegetation and site conditions
* Uses soil information to guide restoration or management
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in soil characterization and pedology Application: Leads soil survey and assessment programs Indicators:
* Conducts detailed soil surveys for complex landscapes
* Uses soil information for landscape-level planning
* Correlates soils across geographic areas
* Evaluates soil carbon sequestration potential
* Designs soil monitoring programs for restoration or management
* Uses proximal soil sensing and digital soil mapping
* Analyzes long-term soil changes from management
* Publishes soil assessment results
* Trains practitioners in soil characterization
* Develops soil assessment protocols and standards
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes soil assessment practices in field Application: Directs major soil survey and monitoring initiatives Indicators:
* Leads landscape-scale soil assessment programs
* Develops innovative soil assessment approaches
* Influences soil survey and monitoring standards
* Provides expert consultation on complex soil issues
* Publishes influential soil research
* Achieves recognition for soil science excellence
* Advances integration of technology in soil assessment
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Soil surveys, rangeland health assessments, erosion monitoring Private Sector: Agricultural soil testing, contaminated site assessment, carbon programs NGOs: Restoration site assessment, soil health advocacy Consulting: Soil surveys for development, wetland delineation, Phase I/II assessments Academia: Soil research plots, experimental treatments, soil genesis studies
________________


4.1.5 Digital data collection and management Using mobile devices, apps, and systems to collect, manage, and share field data efficiently and accurately.
Job Tasks:
1. Use mobile data collection apps to record field observations in real-time
2. Ensure data quality through validation, GPS accuracy checks, and photo documentation
3. Sync field data with office databases and GIS systems
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Familiar with mobile devices and basic data collection apps Application: Collects data using established digital forms and protocols Indicators:
* Uses smartphones or tablets for field data collection
* Follows digital data collection protocols accurately
* Takes geotagged photos to document observations
* Syncs data from field devices when connectivity available
* Recognizes data quality issues (GPS accuracy, missing data)
* Maintains charged devices and backup power
* Works effectively with offline data collection when needed
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Understands data management principles and mobile GIS Application: Designs digital data collection systems and manages field data Indicators:
* Designs custom forms in Survey123, Fulcrum, or similar platforms
* Implements data validation rules and logic in forms
* Uses mobile GIS for field mapping and navigation
* Manages data workflows from collection through analysis
* Ensures data security and backup procedures
* Trains field crews on digital data collection
* Troubleshoots common device and app issues in field
* Integrates field data with GIS and databases
* Uses offline mapping and data collection capabilities
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in field data systems and digital workflows Application: Develops comprehensive field data management systems Indicators:
* Develops integrated field data collection and management systems
* Implements automated data validation and quality control
* Creates real-time dashboards displaying field data
* Uses APIs to integrate multiple data systems
* Implements cloud-based collaborative data platforms
* Develops mobile apps for specific field needs
* Ensures data meets metadata and archival standards
* Evaluates and adopts emerging field technology
* Trains practitioners in field data management
* Develops organizational field data standards
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes field data practices in environmental sector Application: Leads digital transformation of field operations Indicators:
* Directs enterprise field data strategies
* Develops innovative field data approaches adopted widely
* Influences field data standards and best practices
* Integrates AI and automation into field data systems
* Provides expert consultation on field informatics
* Publishes on field data management practices
* Advances technology adoption in field operations
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Standardized monitoring protocols, compliance tracking, resource inventory Private Sector: Quality control, environmental compliance, asset management NGOs: Volunteer data collection, preserve monitoring, citizen science platforms Consulting: Project-specific data collection, client reporting systems Academia: Research data collection, experimental protocols, student training
________________


4.2 LAND MANAGEMENT IMPLEMENTATION
Conducting hands-on treatments and improvements on the land
Skills related to carrying out restoration, maintenance, and improvement activities that enhance ecosystem health and function.
________________


4.2.1 Restoration techniques and implementation Applying practical methods to restore degraded ecosystems toward desired conditions.
Job Tasks:
1. Implement restoration treatments including native planting, invasive removal, and soil amendments
2. Operate restoration equipment (seed drills, tree planters, mulch blowers)
3. Monitor restoration sites and adapt techniques based on success
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic restoration principles and common techniques Application: Implements restoration activities following plans Indicators:
* Plants native species using proper techniques for survival
* Removes invasive species using appropriate methods
* Prepares sites for restoration (clearing, soil preparation)
* Operates basic restoration tools and equipment safely
* Follows erosion control and stormwater BMPs
* Recognizes when conditions affect restoration success
* Documents restoration activities with photos and notes
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies restoration ecology principles; understands regional ecosystems Application: Leads restoration implementation and adapts techniques Indicators:
* Selects appropriate restoration techniques for site conditions
* Implements diverse restoration methods (seeding, planting, natural regeneration)
* Uses specialized equipment (hydroseeders, no-till drills, trackhoes)
* Sources appropriate plant materials (seeds, plugs, poles)
* Implements erosion control appropriate to site conditions
* Adapts restoration techniques based on monitoring results
* Supervises restoration crews and contractors
* Develops site-specific restoration plans
* Uses drones for site assessment and monitoring
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in restoration methods; skilled in adaptive approaches Application: Designs and leads complex restoration projects Indicators:
* Designs comprehensive restoration approaches for degraded sites
* Applies novel restoration techniques for challenging conditions
* Integrates multiple treatment types (vegetation, hydrology, soil)
* Uses assisted migration or climate-adapted restoration when appropriate
* Manages large-scale restoration projects (contracting, budgets, scheduling)
* Evaluates restoration success using multiple metrics
* Adapts restoration approaches based on climate change
* Trains practitioners in restoration techniques
* Publishes or presents restoration outcomes
* Develops restoration guidance for ecosystems or regions
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes restoration practice at landscape scale Application: Leads transformative restoration initiatives Indicators:
* Directs landscape-scale restoration programs
* Develops innovative restoration approaches adopted widely
* Influences restoration policy and standards
* Provides expert consultation on complex restoration challenges
* Publishes influential restoration research
* Achieves recognition for restoration excellence
* Advances restoration practice through leadership
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Post-fire restoration, mine reclamation, wetland restoration Private Sector: Mitigation site implementation, corporate restoration commitments NGOs: Preserve restoration, community restoration projects Consulting: Restoration implementation for clients, contractor oversight Academia: Experimental restoration, demonstration projects
________________


4.2.2 Invasive species control Removing or controlling non-native plants, animals, or pathogens that threaten ecosystem health.
Job Tasks:
1. Implement invasive species control using mechanical, chemical, or biological methods
2. Use integrated pest management approaches minimizing environmental impacts
3. Monitor treatment effectiveness and adapt strategies
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Identifies common invasive species; understands control methods Application: Implements invasive control treatments following protocols Indicators:
* Identifies priority invasive species accurately
* Uses mechanical removal methods (hand-pulling, cutting, grubbing)
* Applies herbicides following label instructions and safety protocols
* Disposes of invasive plant material appropriately
* Uses personal protective equipment correctly
* Documents treatments (locations, methods, timing)
* Understands principles of early detection and rapid response
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies integrated pest management; understands invasion ecology Application: Designs and implements invasive species strategies Indicators:
* Develops site-specific invasive control strategies
* Selects appropriate control methods based on species, site, and season
* Uses specialized equipment (weed wrenches, basal bark applicators)
* Implements biocontrol when appropriate
* Schedules treatments for optimal effectiveness
* Integrates control with revegetation
* Holds pesticide applicator certification
* Monitors treatment effectiveness and adapts approaches
* Supervises invasive control crews or contractors
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in invasive species management; skilled in restoration integration Application: Leads comprehensive invasive species programs Indicators:
* Develops landscape-scale invasive management strategies
* Integrates invasive control with broader restoration goals
* Uses adaptive management for persistent invasive problems
* Manages invasive species programs (crews, contractors, budgets)
* Evaluates new control methods and technologies
* Uses predictive modeling to prioritize control efforts
* Develops invasive species management plans
* Trains practitioners in invasive control
* Publishes or presents invasive species management results
* Engages volunteers in early detection and control
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes invasive species management at regional scale Application: Leads transformative invasive species initiatives Indicators:
* Directs regional invasive species programs
* Develops innovative control approaches adopted widely
* Influences invasive species policy and regulations
* Coordinates multi-organization invasive species efforts
* Provides expert consultation on invasive species challenges
* Publishes influential invasive species research
* Achieves recognition for invasive species management excellence
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Invasive species programs on public lands, aquatic invasive management Private Sector: Vegetation management, right-of-way control, agricultural weed management NGOs: Preserve invasive control, volunteer weed pulls, advocacy Consulting: Invasive management for clients, treatment planning Academia: Invasive species research, biocontrol testing
________________


4.2.3 Prescribed burning and fire management Using fire as a management tool to achieve ecological objectives while ensuring safety.
Job Tasks:
1. Assist with prescribed fire operations following burn plans
2. Monitor fire behavior and smoke during burns
3. Maintain fire equipment and ensure crew safety
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands fire behavior basics and safety protocols Application: Functions as prescribed fire crew member Indicators:
* Completes required fire training (S-130, S-190, etc.)
* Follows burn plan and crew boss directions
* Uses firing devices (drip torches, fusees) safely and effectively
* Maintains fireline during burns
* Monitors fire behavior and communicates observations
* Uses radio communications protocols
* Maintains personal protective equipment and tools
* Understands role of fire in ecosystems
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies fire behavior science; understands fire effects Application: Serves in prescribed fire leadership roles Indicators:
* Holds single resource boss qualifications (RXB2, RXFT)
* Assists with burn plan development
* Predicts fire behavior under varying conditions
* Leads firing operations for portions of burns
* Monitors fuel moisture and weather for burn windows
* Uses hand tools and pumps for fire control
* Conducts test fires to assess fire behavior
* Manages ignition patterns to achieve objectives
* Coordinates with smoke management agencies
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in prescribed fire; skilled in complex burning situations Application: Leads prescribed fire operations and programs Indicators:
* Holds burn boss (RXB1) and incident commander qualifications
* Develops comprehensive burn plans
* Conducts prescribed burns in varying conditions and complexities
* Manages prescribed fire programs (planning, resources, compliance)
* Uses fire modeling software for burn planning
* Evaluates fire effects and burn success
* Trains and mentors prescribed fire practitioners
* Conducts after-action reviews for learning
* Represents organization in prescribed fire councils or networks
* Integrates fire with other management objectives
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes prescribed fire practice at landscape scale Application: Leads transformative fire management initiatives Indicators:
* Directs fire management programs across large landscapes
* Develops innovative prescribed fire approaches
* Influences fire policy and regulations
* Provides expert consultation on complex fire management issues
* Publishes fire ecology and management research
* Achieves recognition for fire management leadership
* Advances landscape-scale fire restoration
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Fire management on public lands, hazardous fuels reduction Private Sector: Private land burning, utility right-of-way management NGOs: Preserve prescribed fire programs, fire advocacy Consulting: Prescribed fire services for clients, burn planning Academia: Experimental fires, fire ecology research
________________


4.2.4 Erosion control and watershed treatment Implementing practices that reduce soil loss and protect water quality.
Job Tasks:
1. Install erosion control structures (check dams, wattles, silt fences)
2. Implement bioengineering techniques for streambank stabilization
3. Monitor erosion control effectiveness and adapt treatments
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands erosion processes and basic control techniques Application: Installs erosion control measures following plans Indicators:
* Installs silt fences, straw wattles, and erosion control blankets
* Stakes erosion control fabrics and materials properly
* Applies mulch and seed for erosion control
* Recognizes active erosion and areas at risk
* Follows stormwater management BMPs
* Uses hand tools and equipment for erosion control
* Understands how weather affects erosion
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies erosion science; understands watershed hydrology Application: Designs and implements erosion control strategies Indicators:
* Assesses erosion risk and develops site-specific control plans
* Selects appropriate erosion control techniques for conditions
* Installs bioengineering structures (fascines, brush layers, live stakes)
* Implements grade control structures (check dams, grade stabilizers)
* Designs erosion control for construction sites
* Uses erosion modeling tools (RUSLE, WEPP)
* Monitors erosion control performance
* Adapts treatments based on storm events
* Ensures compliance with erosion control regulations
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in erosion control and stream restoration Application: Leads comprehensive watershed treatment programs Indicators:
* Designs complex erosion control and stream restoration projects
* Integrates erosion control with broader watershed restoration
* Uses engineered solutions for challenging erosion problems
* Manages erosion control contractors and projects
* Evaluates long-term erosion control effectiveness
* Applies watershed-scale sediment reduction strategies
* Uses drones and GIS for erosion assessment
* Trains practitioners in erosion control
* Publishes or presents erosion control outcomes
* Develops erosion control guidance for regions or landscapes
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes erosion control practice at watershed scale Application: Leads transformative watershed restoration initiatives Indicators:
* Directs landscape-scale watershed restoration programs
* Develops innovative erosion control approaches
* Influences erosion control policy and standards
* Provides expert consultation on complex erosion challenges
* Publishes influential erosion and sediment research
* Achieves recognition for watershed restoration excellence
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Road erosion control, stream restoration, post-fire rehabilitation Private Sector: Construction erosion control, mine reclamation, agricultural conservation NGOs: Stream restoration, erosion control demonstration projects Consulting: Erosion control design, SWPPP development, stream restoration Academia: Erosion research, demonstration projects, experimental treatments
________________


4.2.5 Trail construction and maintenance Building and maintaining trails that provide access while minimizing environmental impacts.
Job Tasks:
1. Construct sustainable trail segments following design plans
2. Maintain existing trails through brushing, drainage work, and tread repair
3. Use appropriate tools and techniques for different trail types and conditions
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands sustainable trail principles and basic techniques Application: Performs trail maintenance and assists with construction Indicators:
* Clears brush and maintains trail corridors
* Cleans drainage features (water bars, ditches)
* Repairs trail tread (filling ruts, removing obstacles)
* Uses hand tools safely and effectively (Pulaskis, McLeods, shovels)
* Operates power tools (chainsaws, brushcutters) with proper training
* Recognizes trail problems (erosion, poor drainage, user damage)
* Follows trail maintenance standards
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies trail design principles; understands drainage and sustainability Application: Leads trail crews and constructs new trail segments Indicators:
* Constructs new trail segments following sustainable design
* Installs drainage features (water bars, culverts, turnpikes)
* Builds trail structures (steps, retaining walls, bridges)
* Operates mechanical trail equipment (excavators, rock drills)
* Supervises trail crew members and volunteers
* Assesses trail sustainability and designs improvements
* Uses GPS and GIS for trail planning and mapping
* Implements accessibility standards when appropriate
* Adapts techniques to soil types and terrain
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in trail design and construction; skilled in complex projects Application: Designs and manages trail programs Indicators:
* Designs comprehensive trail systems
* Manages trail construction projects (contractors, budgets, permits)
* Constructs challenging trail features (switchbacks, rock work, boardwalks)
* Uses mechanized trail construction efficiently
* Develops trail maintenance standards and schedules
* Conducts trail assessments and prioritizes work
* Integrates trails with resource protection goals
* Trains trail crew leaders and contractors
* Uses drones for trail planning and assessment
* Develops trail management plans
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes trail practices at landscape scale Application: Leads transformative trail initiatives Indicators:
* Directs trail programs across large trail systems
* Develops innovative trail techniques adopted widely
* Influences trail policy and standards
* Provides expert consultation on complex trail projects
* Publishes trail construction and management guidance
* Achieves recognition for trail program excellence
* Advances sustainable trail practices
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Trail systems on public lands, backcountry trails, urban trails Private Sector: Resort trails, private land trails, trail design-build NGOs: Preserve trail systems, volunteer trail programs Consulting: Trail planning and design services, trail assessments Academia: Campus trails, research site access


4.3 FIRE MANAGEMENT
Managing wildland fire for resource benefit and public safety
Skills related to using fire as a management tool and responding to wildfires to protect lives, property, and resources.
________________


4.3.1 Wildland firefighting Suppressing unwanted fires on wildlands using appropriate tactics and ensuring firefighter safety.
Job Tasks:
1. Construct and maintain fireline using hand tools or equipment
2. Implement firing operations to remove fuel ahead of fire
3. Mop up hot spots and ensure fire containment
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands fire behavior basics, safety protocols, and firefighting fundamentals Application: Functions effectively as wildland firefighter on crews Indicators:
* Completes required training (S-130, S-190, work capacity test)
* Constructs handline using appropriate tools
* Follows crew boss directions and tactical plans
* Recognizes hazardous fire conditions and communicates observations
* Uses water and foam effectively for suppression
* Maintains situational awareness and safety zones
* Uses radio communications protocols
* Maintains physical fitness for arduous fire duty
* Cares for equipment and personal protective equipment
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies fire behavior science; understands tactical decision-making Application: Serves in tactical leadership roles on fires Indicators:
* Holds crew boss, engine boss, or squad boss qualifications (FFT1, ENGB, SQAD)
* Sizes up fire situations and develops tactical plans
* Leads crews or resources in fire suppression operations
* Uses firing operations to achieve containment
* Operates fire engines or heavy equipment for suppression
* Conducts risk assessments and implements LCES (lookouts, communications, escape routes, safety zones)
* Mentors less experienced firefighters
* Completes incident documentation and reports
* Maintains proficiency across multiple firefighting tactics
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in fire operations; skilled in complex incident management Application: Serves in incident management positions Indicators:
* Holds division supervisor, operations section chief, or incident commander qualifications
* Manages multiple resources and complex fire operations
* Develops strategic incident objectives and tactical plans
* Makes decisions balancing suppression effectiveness with firefighter safety
* Manages large fire crews and resources (50+ personnel)
* Represents agency in interagency coordination
* Conducts after-action reviews for organizational learning
* Mentors developing incident managers
* Applies incident command system effectively
* Manages extended attack and large fire incidents
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes fire operations practices at national level Application: Provides strategic fire management leadership Indicators:
* Serves on Type 1 or Type 2 incident management teams
* Manages major wildfire incidents affecting multiple jurisdictions
* Develops fire management policy and doctrine
* Provides expert consultation on complex fire management issues
* Trains incident management personnel
* Achieves recognition for fire management excellence
* Advances firefighter safety culture nationally
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Federal/state wildland fire response, incident management Private Sector: Contract firefighting crews, wildfire response services NGOs: Fire management for preserves, community fire response Consulting: Fire management planning, expert testimony Academia: Fire research support, fire training programs
________________


4.3.2 Prescribed fire planning and execution Developing burn plans and safely conducting prescribed fires to achieve ecological objectives.
Job Tasks:
1. Develop comprehensive burn plans including objectives, prescriptions, and contingencies
2. Conduct prescribed burns as burn boss ensuring objectives are met safely
3. Monitor fire effects and document burn outcomes
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands prescribed fire basics and ecological role of fire Application: Supports prescribed fire operations as crew member Indicators:
* Assists with burn plan preparation (collecting data, mapping)
* Implements firing techniques following burn plan
* Monitors fire behavior during burns
* Assists with smoke monitoring and reporting
* Documents burn activities and conditions
* Understands desired fire effects for common ecosystems
* Recognizes when conditions fall outside prescription
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies fire ecology; understands burn planning process Application: Develops burn plans and serves in prescribed fire leadership roles Indicators:
* Develops burn plans for moderate complexity burns
* Holds prescribed fire qualifications (RXFT, RXB2)
* Predicts fire behavior and smoke dispersion
* Leads ignition operations achieving desired patterns and effects
* Conducts pre-burn site preparation and assessments
* Monitors fuel moisture and weather to identify burn windows
* Coordinates with air quality and fire management agencies
* Evaluates fire effects relative to objectives
* Adapts burning techniques to seasonal and site conditions
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in prescribed fire; skilled in complex burning situations Application: Serves as burn boss for complex prescribed fires Indicators:
* Develops burn plans for complex burns with multiple objectives
* Holds burn boss (RXB1) certification
* Manages prescribed burns in challenging conditions (smoke-sensitive areas, high-value resources)
* Uses fire modeling software (BehavePlus, FOFEM, CONSUME) for planning
* Develops landscape-scale prescribed fire strategies
* Trains and mentors prescribed fire practitioners
* Represents organization in prescribed fire councils
* Publishes or presents on prescribed fire techniques and outcomes
* Integrates prescribed fire with broader restoration goals
* Manages prescribed fire programs (scheduling, resources, compliance)
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes prescribed fire practice regionally or nationally Application: Provides strategic leadership on prescribed fire Indicators:
* Directs prescribed fire programs across landscapes
* Develops innovative prescribed fire techniques adopted widely
* Influences prescribed fire policy and regulations
* Provides expert consultation on complex prescribed fire challenges
* Publishes influential prescribed fire research
* Achieves national recognition for prescribed fire leadership
* Advances prescribed fire as ecological management tool
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Prescribed fire programs on public lands, ecosystem restoration Private Sector: Private land burning programs, forestry burning NGOs: Preserve prescribed fire, fire advocacy, landowner assistance Consulting: Prescribed fire services, burn planning Academia: Experimental fires, fire ecology research
________________


4.3.3 Fuels management Reducing wildfire risk and intensity by treating vegetation and fuels on the landscape.
Job Tasks:
1. Assess fuels and develop treatment strategies (mechanical thinning, prescribed fire, grazing)
2. Implement fuels treatments using appropriate methods and equipment
3. Monitor treatment effectiveness using fuel models and fire behavior analysis
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands fuel types and basic treatment methods Application: Implements fuels treatments following plans Indicators:
* Recognizes different fuel types and their fire behavior implications
* Implements mechanical fuels treatments (hand thinning, pruning, chipping)
* Piles slash and debris appropriately
* Operates chippers and brush mowers safely
* Creates defensible space around structures
* Documents treatment areas and activities
* Understands how fuels treatments reduce fire risk
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies fuels management principles; understands fire behavior relationships Application: Designs and implements fuels treatment projects Indicators:
* Assesses fuels using standard fuel models (Scott and Burgan)
* Develops site-specific fuels treatment prescriptions
* Selects appropriate treatment methods for objectives and conditions
* Supervises fuels treatment crews and contractors
* Uses mechanical equipment for larger scale treatments
* Integrates multiple treatment types (mechanical, prescribed fire, grazing)
* Monitors and evaluates treatment effectiveness
* Uses FlamMap or similar tools to model treatment effects
* Coordinates treatments with wildfire risk reduction goals
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in fuels management; skilled in landscape planning Application: Leads comprehensive fuels management programs Indicators:
* Develops landscape-scale fuels treatment strategies
* Uses fire behavior modeling to prioritize treatments (FARSITE, FSim)
* Designs treatments creating strategic fuel breaks
* Manages complex fuels projects (budgets, contractors, compliance)
* Evaluates treatment longevity and maintenance needs
* Integrates fuels management with ecosystem management
* Develops community wildfire protection plans
* Uses LiDAR and remote sensing for fuels assessment
* Trains practitioners in fuels management
* Publishes or presents fuels treatment outcomes
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes fuels management practice at regional scale Application: Provides strategic leadership on wildfire risk reduction Indicators:
* Directs landscape-scale fuels management programs
* Develops innovative fuels treatment approaches
* Influences fuels management policy and funding
* Provides expert consultation on complex fuels issues
* Publishes influential fuels management research
* Achieves recognition for wildfire risk reduction leadership
* Advances integration of fuels management with community protection
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Hazardous fuels reduction, wildfire risk mitigation, community protection Private Sector: Forest management, wildfire risk reduction services NGOs: Fuels treatments on preserves, community wildfire programs Consulting: Fuels assessments, treatment planning, CWPP development Academia: Fuels research, fire behavior studies
________________


4.3.4 Fire weather and behavior analysis Understanding and predicting how weather and fuels interact to produce fire behavior.
Job Tasks:
1. Monitor fire weather and calculate fire danger indices
2. Predict fire behavior using weather, fuels, and topography data
3. Brief fire personnel on expected fire behavior and weather trends
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic fire weather elements and fire behavior concepts Application: Monitors weather and recognizes fire behavior patterns Indicators:
* Takes and records weather observations (temperature, humidity, wind)
* Calculates fuel moisture from weather data
* Understands fire danger rating components
* Recognizes weather patterns affecting fire behavior
* Observes and documents fire behavior on incidents
* Uses fire weather websites and apps
* Understands how topography affects fire spread
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies fire behavior science; interprets weather forecasts Application: Predicts fire behavior and provides fire weather briefings Indicators:
* Completes S-290 (Intermediate Fire Behavior) and S-390 (Fire Behavior Calculations)
* Calculates flame length, rate of spread, and intensity
* Interprets spot weather forecasts for fire operations
* Uses nomograms and calculation tools for fire behavior prediction
* Provides fire behavior briefings to crews
* Recognizes critical fire weather patterns (Red Flag conditions, dry cold fronts)
* Monitors Fire Weather Watches and Red Flag Warnings
* Applies fire behavior knowledge to tactical decision-making
* Uses BehavePlus software for fire behavior modeling
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in fire behavior and meteorology; skilled in complex analysis Application: Serves as fire behavior analyst on incidents or in predictive services Indicators:
* Holds Fire Behavior Analyst (FBAN) qualification
* Provides fire behavior analysis on complex incidents
* Develops long-term fire behavior forecasts
* Uses advanced fire behavior models (FARSITE, FSPro, FlamMap)
* Identifies weather-driven windows for successful suppression or burnout operations
* Analyzes fuels, weather, and topography interactions comprehensively
* Briefs incident management teams on fire behavior forecasts
* Conducts post-fire fire behavior analysis for learning
* Trains fire personnel in fire behavior interpretation
* Contributes to fire behavior research or case studies
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes fire behavior science and operational application Application: Provides strategic fire behavior expertise nationally Indicators:
* Serves in Predictive Services or National Fire Weather Program
* Develops fire behavior analysis methods and tools
* Influences fire behavior training and doctrine
* Provides expert consultation on major fire incidents
* Publishes fire behavior research and analysis
* Achieves recognition for fire behavior expertise
* Advances integration of fire behavior science into operations
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: Predictive services, fire behavior analysis on incidents, long-range planning Private Sector: Fire weather services, wildfire risk modeling NGOs: Fire behavior support for prescribed fire programs Consulting: Fire behavior analysis services, expert testimony Academia: Fire behavior research, fire weather studies
________________


4.3.5 Post-fire rehabilitation Implementing treatments to reduce erosion, prevent invasive species establishment, and promote recovery after wildfire.
Job Tasks:
1. Assess post-fire conditions and erosion risk using BAER protocols
2. Implement emergency stabilization treatments (mulching, seeding, barriers)
3. Monitor post-fire recovery and adapt restoration approaches
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands post-fire processes and basic stabilization techniques Application: Implements post-fire treatments following plans Indicators:
* Assists with post-fire site assessments
* Applies erosion control treatments (mulch, seed, barriers)
* Installs check dams and sediment barriers
* Removes hazard trees in safe situations
* Monitors post-fire invasive species establishment
* Documents treatment implementation
* Understands urgency and timing of post-fire treatments
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies BAER assessment methods; understands fire effects Application: Conducts BAER assessments and develops treatment plans Indicators:
* Conducts Burned Area Emergency Response (BAER) assessments
* Evaluates soil burn severity and erosion risk
* Develops post-fire treatment prescriptions
* Designs erosion control strategies for burned watersheds
* Implements native seeding appropriate to burned areas
* Manages post-fire rehabilitation contractors
* Monitors treatment effectiveness after storms
* Coordinates post-fire treatments across multiple resources
* Uses burn severity maps and watershed models
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in post-fire ecology and watershed rehabilitation Application: Leads comprehensive post-fire recovery programs Indicators:
* Leads BAER teams for complex large fires
* Develops landscape-scale post-fire rehabilitation strategies
* Integrates post-fire recovery with long-term restoration
* Uses modeling tools (ERMiT, WEPP) to predict post-fire erosion
* Manages major post-fire rehabilitation projects
* Evaluates long-term recovery trajectories
* Addresses post-fire invasive species strategically
* Trains BAER team members
* Publishes or presents post-fire recovery outcomes
* Develops post-fire treatment guidance
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes post-fire rehabilitation practices nationally Application: Provides strategic leadership on post-fire recovery Indicators:
* Directs post-fire recovery programs across regions
* Develops innovative post-fire rehabilitation approaches
* Influences post-fire policy and funding
* Provides expert consultation on complex post-fire challenges
* Publishes influential post-fire recovery research
* Achieves recognition for post-fire rehabilitation excellence
* Advances integration of emergency response with long-term restoration
Sector-Specific Applications:
Public Agencies: BAER teams, post-fire recovery programs, watershed protection Private Sector: Post-fire restoration services, watershed rehabilitation NGOs: Post-fire recovery on preserves, volunteer rehabilitation projects Consulting: BAER assessments, post-fire treatment planning Academia: Post-fire ecology research, fire effects studies
________________

Preventing accidents and responding effectively to emergencies
Skills related to maintaining safe field operations and managing emergency situations that arise during land management work.
________________

1. Conduct job hazard analyses before field activities

3. Investigate incidents and near-misses to prevent recurrence
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:

* Uses personal protective equipment correctly
* Identifies hazards in work areas
* Follows safety protocols and standard operating procedures
* Participates in safety briefings
* Reports unsafe conditions and incidents
* Uses equipment safely following training
* Maintains situational awareness during field work
* Stops work when conditions become unsafe
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies risk management principles; understands safety regulations Application: Conducts safety assessments and leads safe operations Indicators:
* Conducts job hazard analyses for field activities
* Leads safety briefings for crews
* Ensures PPE availability and proper use
* Identifies and mitigates hazards proactively
* Maintains safety equipment and first aid supplies
* Investigates incidents and near-misses
* Implements corrective actions after incidents
* Ensures crew compliance with safety requirements

ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in safety management; understands safety culture Application: Develops and manages comprehensive safety programs Indicators:

1. Assess injuries and provide first aid in remote locations
2. Coordinate medical evacuations when necessary
3. Maintain first aid supplies and medical certifications
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Holds basic first aid and CPR certification Application: Provides basic first aid for minor injuries Indicators:
* Holds current First Aid/CPR certification

* Recognizes serious injuries requiring medical attention
* Activates emergency response appropriately
* Maintains basic first aid kit
* Documents injuries and treatments
* Follows bloodborne pathogen protocols
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Holds Wilderness First Responder or EMT certification Application: Manages medical emergencies in remote settings Indicators:
* Holds Wilderness First Responder (WFR) or EMT certification

* Manages medical emergencies until evacuation possible
* Improvises medical care with limited resources
* Coordinates helicopter or ground evacuations
* Communicates medical information to responders
* Maintains advanced first aid and trauma supplies
* Provides medical support for field crews
* Makes evacuation vs. care-in-place decisions
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Holds advanced medical certifications; understands wilderness medicine Application: Provides advanced medical care and leads medical response Indicators:
* Holds Wilderness EMT, Paramedic, or similar advanced certification

4.4.3 Search and rescue operations Locating and assisting lost, injured, or stranded people in wildland areas.

2. Navigate to remote locations using map, compass, and GPS
3. Provide emergency care and assist with evacuation
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years) Knowledge: Understands basic search and rescue concepts and lost person behavior Application: Functions as searcher on search operations Indicators:
* Completes basic SAR training
* Executes search assignments following instructions
* Uses map, compass, and GPS for navigation
* Recognizes clues and evidence
* Maintains proper spacing in line searches
* Communicates findings via radio
* Provides basic care to subjects when found
* Maintains fitness for demanding searches
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years) Knowledge: Applies search theory and tactics; skilled in wilderness navigation Application: Serves as team leader on search operations Indicators:
* Leads search teams in field operations
* Uses probability of area calculations to prioritize search segments
* Conducts hasty searches and confinement operations
* Tracks and interprets sign
* Uses technical rescue skills (rope rescue basics, swiftwater awareness)
* Coordinates with search dogs and air resources
* Navigates efficiently in varied terrain and conditions
* Completes SAR Management training
* Participates in evidence recovery when appropriate
ADVANCED (6-10 years) Knowledge: Expert in search management and lost person behavior Application: Plans and manages search operations Indicators:
* Serves as search manager or operations section chief
* Develops search strategies using behavioral profiles
* Manages multi-team search operations
* Uses search management software (SARTopo, CalTopo)
* Coordinates air and ground resources
* Holds technical rescue certifications (rope rescue, swiftwater, etc.)
* Trains SAR personnel
* Conducts searches in challenging environments
* Manages search missions using Incident Command System
* Evaluates search effectiveness and adapts strategies
EXPERT (10+ years) Knowledge: Shapes search and rescue practices regionally or nationally Application: Provides SAR strategic leadership Indicators:
* Directs SAR programs for regions or organizations

4.4.4 Hazardous materials awareness and response
Recognizing and safely responding to hazardous materials encountered during field operations.
Job Tasks:
1. Identify potential hazardous materials in field settings
2. Implement appropriate containment and notification procedures
3. Use personal protective equipment for hazmat situations
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Completes HAZWOPER Awareness training; recognizes common hazards
* Application: Identifies hazardous materials and reports appropriately
* Indicators:
   * Completes OSHA HAZWOPER Awareness training
   * Recognizes potential hazardous materials (fuel spills, chemicals, asbestos)
   * Uses Safety Data Sheets (SDS) appropriately
   * Reports hazmat discoveries through proper channels
   * Evacuates areas when hazmat suspected
   * Understands when NOT to attempt cleanup
   * Follows decontamination procedures when exposed
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Completes HAZWOPER Operations training; understands containment
* Application: Responds to hazmat incidents within training limits
* Indicators:
   * Completes OSHA HAZWOPER Operations (24-hour) training
   * Contains small spills using appropriate methods and PPE
   * Uses hazmat reference materials (ERG, shipping papers)
   * Establishes isolation zones and access control
   * Coordinates with hazmat teams when needed
   * Manages fuel and chemical storage safely
   * Implements spill prevention measures
   * Documents hazmat incidents properly
   * Conducts annual HAZWOPER refresher training
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Completes HAZWOPER Technician training; expert in site remediation
* Application: Manages hazmat response and remediation projects
* Indicators:
   * Completes OSHA HAZWOPER Technician (40-hour) training
   * Manages hazmat incidents and cleanup operations
   * Selects appropriate PPE for various hazmat situations
   * Coordinates with regulatory agencies (EPA, state agencies)
   * Manages contaminated site remediation projects
   * Develops hazmat response plans for organizations
   * Trains staff in hazmat awareness and response
   * Ensures regulatory compliance for hazmat management
   * Manages hazmat contractor oversight
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes hazmat practices in environmental operations
* Application: Provides strategic hazmat program leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs organizational hazmat programs
   * Develops innovative hazmat management approaches
   * Influences hazmat policy and regulations
   * Provides expert consultation on complex hazmat situations
   * Achieves recognition for hazmat program excellence
   * Mentors hazmat professionals
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Illegal dump cleanups, abandoned site remediation, spill response
* Private Sector: Industrial site management, contaminated site cleanup
* NGOs: Preserve hazmat management, community cleanup events
* Consulting: Environmental site assessments, remediation management
* Academia: Laboratory safety, research site management

4.5 EQUIPMENT OPERATION
Using tools and machinery safely and effectively
Skills related to operating, maintaining, and managing equipment used in field operations.
________________


4.5.1 Hand tool proficiency
Using non-powered tools effectively and safely for field work.
Job Tasks:
1. Select and use appropriate hand tools for vegetation management, trail work, and restoration
2. Maintain tools in safe working condition through sharpening and repair
3. Transport and store tools safely
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands basic hand tools and their uses
* Application: Uses common hand tools safely and effectively
* Indicators:
   * Uses shovels, rakes, and hoes effectively
   * Operates Pulaskis, McLeods, and fire tools properly
   * Uses hand saws and loppers safely
   * Maintains proper body mechanics to prevent injury
   * Carries tools safely while hiking
   * Recognizes when tools are dull or damaged
   * Cleans tools after use
   * Stores tools properly to prevent damage
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Understands tool selection and maintenance principles
* Application: Uses diverse tools efficiently and maintains them properly
* Indicators:
   * Selects optimal tools for specific tasks and conditions
   * Uses specialized tools (weed wrenches, dibbles, bark spuds)
   * Sharpens tools properly (files, stones, grinders)
   * Makes minor tool repairs and adjustments
   * Works efficiently with proper technique
   * Maintains large tool inventories
   * Trains others in proper tool use
   * Improvises tool solutions for unique situations
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in tool selection, use, and program management
* Application: Manages tool programs and teaches advanced techniques
* Indicators:

4.5.2 Power equipment operation
Safely operating motorized equipment including chainsaws, brush cutters, and pumps.
Job Tasks:
1. Operate chainsaws safely for tree falling, bucking, and limbing
2. Use string trimmers, brush cutters, and mowers for vegetation management
3. Maintain and troubleshoot small engines
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Completes required power equipment training and safety certification
* Application: Operates basic power equipment under supervision
* Indicators:
   * Completes chainsaw safety training (e.g., S-212)
   * Uses chainsaws safely for basic cutting operations
   * Operates string trimmers and brush cutters properly
   * Performs pre-operational checks
   * Mixes two-stroke fuel correctly
   * Uses appropriate PPE (chaps, helmet, hearing protection)
   * Recognizes equipment problems and stops for maintenance
   * Follows lockout/tagout procedures
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Holds advanced power equipment certifications
* Application: Operates power equipment independently for complex tasks
* Indicators:
   * Falls trees safely using proper techniques
   * Completes advanced chainsaw training (tree felling, hazard trees)
   * Operates multiple types of power equipment (saws, cutters, mowers, pumps)
   * Performs routine maintenance (air filters, spark plugs, chains)
   * Sharpens chainsaw chains properly
   * Diagnoses and repairs minor equipment problems
   * Works safely around other equipment operators
   * Trains others in equipment operation
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in power equipment operation and maintenance
* Application: Manages equipment programs and specializes in complex operations
* Indicators:
   * Certified sawyer or equipment specialist
   * Operates equipment in hazardous situations (storm cleanup, fire operations)
   * Performs advanced maintenance and repairs
   * Manages equipment fleets and maintenance programs
   * Procures equipment appropriate to needs
   * Develops equipment operation standards and training
   * Evaluates new equipment and technology
   * Ensures equipment safety and compliance
   * Trains equipment operators and instructors
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes power equipment practices
* Application: Provides strategic equipment program leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs organizational equipment programs
   * Develops innovative equipment applications
   * Influences equipment standards and procurement
   * Achieves recognition for equipment expertise
   * Mentors equipment specialists
   * Advances equipment safety practices
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Chainsaw programs, equipment maintenance shops

* Academia: Research equipment, campus grounds equipment

3. Process drone data to create orthomosaics, 3D models, or vegetation maps

   * Conducts pre-flight checks and safety assessments
   * Captures aerial photos and video

   * Maintains drone batteries and equipment
   * Recognizes when weather prevents safe flight

   * Plans automated flight missions for mapping

   * Develops drone Standard Operating Procedures
   * Trains others in drone operations

* Indicators:
   * Manages organizational drone programs

   * Integrates drones into operational workflows

4.6 SUSTAINABLE AGRICULTURE IMPLEMENTATION
Applying regenerative farming practices on the ground
Skills related to implementing agricultural practices that build soil health, conserve resources, and promote ecological function.
________________


4.6.1 Regenerative practice implementation
Carrying out farming practices that restore and enhance soil health, biodiversity, and ecosystem function.
Job Tasks:
1. Implement cover cropping, diverse rotations, and reduced tillage systems
2. Integrate livestock using rotational grazing or other regenerative approaches
3. Monitor soil health indicators and adapt practices
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands regenerative agriculture principles and practices
* Application: Implements regenerative practices following plans
* Indicators:
   * Plants and manages cover crops
   * Implements reduced tillage or no-till practices
   * Sets up and manages rotational grazing systems
   * Applies compost and organic amendments
   * Recognizes soil health improvements
   * Documents practice implementation
   * Understands ecological principles underlying practices
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies regenerative agriculture science and systems thinking
* Application: Designs and manages regenerative farming operations
* Indicators:
   * Develops crop rotations maximizing diversity and soil health
   * Selects cover crop mixes appropriate to goals and conditions
   * Manages mob grazing or adaptive multi-paddock grazing
   * Integrates crops and livestock effectively
   * Monitors soil health using multiple indicators
   * Adapts practices based on outcomes
   * Uses biological pest control and habitat enhancement
   * Develops regenerative farm plans
   * Mentors farmers in regenerative practices
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in regenerative agriculture systems
* Application: Leads regenerative farming programs and transformations
* Indicators:
   * Designs comprehensive regenerative farming systems
   * Implements complex multi-enterprise regenerative operations
   * Achieves measurable soil health improvements
   * Evaluates economic and environmental outcomes
   * Develops farm-specific regenerative strategies
   * Trains farmers and technical service providers
   * Conducts on-farm research and demonstration
   * Publishes or presents regenerative agriculture outcomes
   * Builds regenerative agriculture networks
   * Addresses barriers to regenerative practice adoption
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes regenerative agriculture practice regionally
* Application: Provides strategic leadership on regenerative agriculture
* Indicators:
   * Directs landscape-scale regenerative agriculture initiatives
   * Develops innovative regenerative approaches adopted widely
   * Influences agricultural policy toward regenerative practices
   * Achieves recognition for regenerative agriculture leadership
   * Demonstrates significant ecological and economic outcomes
   * Mentors regenerative agriculture practitioners
   * Advances regenerative agriculture science and practice
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: NRCS conservation programs, demonstration farms
* Private Sector: Regenerative farm operations, corporate agriculture
* NGOs: Regenerative agriculture programs, farmer training
* Consulting: Regenerative agriculture services, farm planning
* Academia: Research farms, agricultural extension, agroecology

4.6.3 Integrated pest management application
Implementing IPM strategies that minimize pesticide use while maintaining crop health and productivity.
Job Tasks:
1. Scout crops for pests and monitor population levels against action thresholds
2. Implement cultural, biological, and chemical pest control as part of IPM programs
3. Document pest management activities and evaluate effectiveness
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands IPM principles and common pests
* Application: Scouts crops and implements IPM tactics
* Indicators:
   * Identifies common crop pests and beneficial organisms
   * Scouts crops systematically following protocols
   * Records pest observations accurately
   * Implements cultural controls (sanitation, rotation, resistant varieties)
   * Deploys pheromone traps and monitoring tools
   * Applies pesticides following label and IPM guidelines
   * Holds pesticide applicator license if required
   * Recognizes economic thresholds for pest action
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies IPM strategies and pest ecology
* Application: Develops and manages IPM programs
* Indicators:
   * Develops IPM plans for crops or operations
   * Uses economic thresholds to guide treatment decisions
   * Implements biological control (beneficial releases, habitat)
   * Selects selective pesticides minimizing non-target impacts
   * Uses pheromone disruption for pest management
   * Monitors for pesticide resistance and rotates modes of action
   * Evaluates IPM program effectiveness
   * Integrates multiple IPM tactics strategically
   * Trains farmers or staff in IPM practices
   * Uses IPM decision support tools
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in IPM and pest management systems
* Application: Leads comprehensive IPM programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs landscape-scale IPM strategies
   * Manages complex multi-crop IPM programs
   * Develops biological control programs
   * Addresses pesticide resistance through IPM
   * Conducts IPM research and demonstration
   * Evaluates new IPM technologies and tactics
   * Provides IPM consulting and training
   * Develops IPM guidance for crops or regions
   * Publishes or presents IPM outcomes
   * Influences IPM policy and practices
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes IPM practices regionally or nationally
* Application: Provides strategic IPM leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs IPM programs at landscape scale
   * Develops innovative IPM approaches adopted widely
   * Influences pesticide policy toward IPM
   * Achieves recognition for IPM leadership
   * Demonstrates significant pesticide reduction through IPM
   * Mentors IPM practitioners
   * Advances IPM science and implementation
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: IPM extension programs, public lands pest management
* Private Sector: Commercial IPM, crop consulting, IPM services
* NGOs: IPM advocacy, farmer IPM programs
* Consulting: IPM planning and implementation services
* Academia: IPM research, extension programs, entomology

   * Maintains irrigation equipment
   * Uses soil moisture sensors with guidance

   * Documents irrigation applications
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)

* Indicators:
   * Develops irrigation schedules based on ET and soil moisture

   * Operates multiple irrigation system types
   * Calculates irrigation efficiency and uniformity
   * Implements drip or micro-irrigation systems
   * Practices deficit irrigation when appropriate
   * Troubleshoots irrigation system problems
   * Implements water conservation practices
   * Evaluates irrigation system performance
   * Trains others in irrigation management
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in irrigation systems and water management
* Application: Designs and manages comprehensive irrigation programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs water-efficient irrigation systems
   * Uses weather-based irrigation controllers and automation
   * Implements precision irrigation with variable rate application
   * Manages irrigation for multiple crops or operations
   * Addresses salinity and drainage issues
   * Evaluates emerging irrigation technologies
   * Conducts irrigation audits and improvements
   * Develops irrigation water management plans
   * Provides irrigation consulting and training
   * Publishes or presents irrigation outcomes
   * Integrates irrigation with broader water resource management
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes irrigation practices in agriculture
* Application: Provides strategic water management leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs irrigation programs at landscape scale
   * Develops innovative irrigation approaches
   * Influences agricultural water policy

   * Demonstrates significant water conservation
   * Mentors irrigation professionals

4.6.5 Soil health assessment and management
Monitoring and improving soil biological, chemical, and physical properties to support productive and resilient agriculture.

1. Conduct soil health assessments using field tests and laboratory analysis

3. Monitor soil health trends and adapt management

* Indicators:
   * Conducts comprehensive soil health assessments

   * Implements soil health practices (cover crops, diverse rotations, compost, reduced tillage)
   * Monitors soil organic carbon and other soil health metrics
   * Addresses soil compaction and structure problems
   * Evaluates soil health program effectiveness
   * Uses soil health frameworks (NRCS Soil Health Assessment)
   * Trains farmers in soil health management

ADVANCED (6-10 years)

* Indicators:
   * Designs landscape-scale soil health initiatives
   * Uses advanced soil health assessments (microbial analysis, soil food webs)

   * Evaluates soil carbon sequestration programs
   * Addresses degraded soil restoration
   * Provides soil health consulting and training

   * Publishes or presents soil health outcomes
   * Influences soil health policy and programs
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes soil health practices in agriculture
* Application: Provides strategic soil health leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs soil health programs regionally or nationally
   * Develops innovative soil health approaches
   * Influences agricultural policy toward soil health
   * Achieves recognition for soil health leadership
   * Demonstrates significant soil health improvements at scale
   * Mentors soil health practitioners
   * Advances soil health science and practice
Sector-Specific Applications:

4.7 URBAN ENVIRONMENTAL INFRASTRUCTURE
Managing environmental systems in cities and developed areas
Skills related to implementing green infrastructure, urban forestry, and environmental systems in urban and suburban settings.

Installing systems that manage stormwater, reduce heat, and provide ecological benefits in urban areas.

Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
