* Indicators:
   * Assists with bioswale and rain garden construction
   * Plants vegetation for green infrastructure
   * Installs erosion control during construction
   * Follows installation plans and specifications
   * Uses hand tools and basic equipment safely
   * Recognizes when installations don't meet design intent
   * Documents installation activities
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies green infrastructure design principles and installation techniques
* Application: Leads green infrastructure installation projects
* Indicators:
   * Installs complete bioswales, rain gardens, and infiltration systems
   * Constructs permeable pavement systems
   * Installs green roof and living wall systems
   * Ensures proper grading and drainage
   * Selects appropriate plants for green infrastructure
   * Establishes vegetation in green infrastructure
   * Conducts installation quality control
   * Manages green infrastructure contractors
   * Troubleshoots installation problems
   * Monitors performance after installation
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in green infrastructure systems and performance
* Application: Designs and manages comprehensive green infrastructure programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs green infrastructure installations
   * Manages large-scale green infrastructure projects
   * Evaluates green infrastructure performance (infiltration, water quality)
   * Develops green infrastructure maintenance protocols
   * Integrates multiple green infrastructure types
   * Uses monitoring data to improve designs
   * Trains green infrastructure installers
   * Develops green infrastructure standards
   * Publishes or presents green infrastructure outcomes
   * Provides green infrastructure consulting
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes green infrastructure practices
* Application: Provides strategic green infrastructure leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs city-wide green infrastructure programs
   * Develops innovative green infrastructure approaches
   * Influences green infrastructure policy and codes
   * Achieves recognition for green infrastructure leadership
   * Demonstrates significant stormwater benefits from green infrastructure
   * Mentors green infrastructure professionals
   * Advances green infrastructure technology and practice
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Municipal green infrastructure, stormwater programs
* Private Sector: Commercial green infrastructure, landscape construction
* NGOs: Community green infrastructure, demonstration projects
* Consulting: Green infrastructure design and installation services
* Academia: Green infrastructure research, demonstration sites
________________


4.7.2 Urban forestry operations
Planting, maintaining, and protecting trees in urban and community environments.
Job Tasks:
1. Plant and establish trees using appropriate techniques for urban conditions
2. Prune trees following ISA standards for health, safety, and structure
3. Assess and manage urban tree health, pests, and diseases
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands urban tree care basics and safety
* Application: Plants and maintains urban trees
* Indicators:
   * Plants trees properly (hole size, depth, staking, mulching)
   * Waters and cares for newly planted trees
   * Performs basic pruning (deadwood, small branches)
   * Recognizes common tree problems (pests, disease, stress)
   * Uses tree care tools and equipment safely
   * Protects trees during construction
   * Documents tree care activities
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies arboriculture principles and tree biology
* Application: Manages urban tree care operations
* Indicators:
   * Completes ISA Certified Arborist certification
   * Performs structural pruning following ISA standards
   * Conducts tree risk assessments (Level 1 & 2)
   * Diagnoses tree health problems
   * Implements tree preservation during construction
   * Manages tree planting programs
   * Selects appropriate species for urban conditions
   * Uses aerial lift and climbing equipment safely
   * Operates stump grinders and wood chippers
   * Trains tree care crews
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in urban forestry and tree risk management
* Application: Leads urban forestry programs
* Indicators:
   * Holds ISA Certified Arborist and Tree Risk Assessment Qualification
   * Manages municipal or organizational urban forestry programs
   * Conducts Level 3 tree risk assessments
   * Develops urban forest management plans
   * Manages tree inventories and GIS databases
   * Implements tree protection ordinances
   * Addresses complex tree problems (large removals, utility conflicts)
   * Uses i-Tree or similar tools for urban forest assessment
   * Provides expert testimony on tree issues
   * Trains arborists and urban foresters
   * Publishes or presents urban forestry outcomes
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes urban forestry practices
* Application: Provides strategic urban forestry leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs urban forestry programs at city or regional level
   * Develops innovative urban forestry approaches
   * Influences urban forestry policy and standards
   * Achieves recognition for urban forestry leadership (ISA awards, etc.)
   * Demonstrates significant urban forest benefits
   * Mentors urban forestry professionals
   * Advances urban forestry science and practice
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Municipal forestry programs, parks tree care
* Private Sector: Commercial arboriculture, tree care companies
* NGOs: Urban greening programs, tree planting campaigns
* Consulting: Urban forestry consulting, tree risk assessment
* Academia: Urban forestry research, arboriculture programs
________________


4.7.3 Stormwater system management
Operating and maintaining urban drainage systems and best management practices for water quality.
Job Tasks:
1. Inspect and clean stormwater infrastructure (catch basins, pipes, outlets)
2. Monitor stormwater quality and implement pollution prevention
3. Maintain vegetated stormwater BMPs (bioswales, detention ponds)
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands urban stormwater systems and water quality issues
* Application: Maintains stormwater infrastructure
* Indicators:
   * Inspects catch basins and stormwater structures
   * Cleans debris from inlets and outlets
   * Maintains vegetation in stormwater BMPs
   * Recognizes pollution sources and illicit discharges
   * Documents stormwater maintenance activities
   * Responds to flooding or drainage problems
   * Uses stormwater maintenance equipment safely
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies stormwater management principles and regulations
* Application: Manages stormwater operations and compliance
* Indicators:
   * Develops stormwater maintenance schedules
   * Conducts stormwater inspections for MS4 compliance
   * Monitors stormwater quality
   * Investigates and eliminates illicit discharges
   * Maintains diverse stormwater BMPs
   * Manages stormwater maintenance contractors
   * Tracks stormwater assets in GIS
   * Implements pollution prevention programs
   * Educates public on stormwater protection
   * Ensures NPDES permit compliance
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in stormwater systems and regulatory compliance
* Application: Leads comprehensive stormwater programs
* Indicators:
   * Manages municipal stormwater programs
   * Develops Stormwater Management Plans
   * Ensures MS4 permit compliance
   * Designs stormwater system improvements
   * Uses stormwater modeling tools
   * Evaluates BMP effectiveness
   * Manages stormwater utility finances
   * Develops stormwater policies and ordinances
   * Provides stormwater training
   * Publishes or presents stormwater outcomes
   * Integrates stormwater with broader watershed management
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes urban stormwater practices
* Application: Provides strategic stormwater leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs stormwater programs at city or regional level
   * Develops innovative stormwater approaches
   * Influences stormwater policy and regulations
   * Achieves recognition for stormwater program excellence
   * Demonstrates significant water quality improvements
   * Mentors stormwater professionals
   * Advances stormwater technology and practice
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Municipal stormwater utilities, MS4 programs
* Private Sector: Industrial stormwater, commercial property management
* NGOs: Watershed protection, stormwater advocacy
* Consulting: Stormwater engineering and management services
* Academia: Stormwater research, urban hydrology
________________


4.7.4 Urban habitat restoration
Restoring ecological function and native biodiversity in urban parks, preserves, and greenspaces.
Job Tasks:
1. Remove invasive species and restore native plant communities in urban settings
2. Create wildlife habitat features (brush piles, nest boxes, pollinator gardens)
3. Engage urban residents in restoration through volunteer programs
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands urban ecology and restoration basics
* Application: Implements urban restoration activities
* Indicators:
   * Plants native species appropriate for urban conditions
   * Removes invasive species using appropriate methods
   * Installs wildlife habitat features
   * Works safely around urban infrastructure
   * Engages with urban residents respectfully
   * Documents restoration activities
   * Coordinates with volunteers
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies urban ecology and community engagement principles
* Application: Manages urban restoration projects
* Indicators:
   * Develops site-specific restoration plans for urban sites
   * Selects natives adapted to urban stressors
   * Addresses urban-specific challenges (compaction, contamination, vandalism)
   * Manages volunteer restoration events
   * Engages diverse urban communities in restoration
   * Monitors restoration outcomes in urban contexts
   * Integrates restoration with recreation and access
   * Addresses equity in urban greening
   * Uses social media and outreach for urban restoration
   * Secures funding for urban restoration projects
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in urban ecological restoration and community engagement
* Application: Leads comprehensive urban restoration programs
* Indicators:
   * Develops city-wide urban restoration strategies
   * Designs restoration addressing urban heat islands and climate
   * Integrates restoration with environmental justice
   * Builds long-term community stewardship
   * Evaluates urban restoration effectiveness
   * Addresses gentrification concerns in urban greening
   * Trains urban restoration practitioners
   * Develops urban restoration guidance
   * Publishes or presents urban restoration outcomes
   * Provides urban restoration consulting
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes urban ecological restoration practices
* Application: Provides strategic urban restoration leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs urban restoration at city or regional scale
   * Develops innovative urban restoration approaches
   * Influences urban environmental policy
   * Achieves recognition for urban restoration leadership
   * Demonstrates significant ecological and social benefits
   * Mentors urban restoration professionals
   * Advances just and equitable urban greening
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Parks restoration, urban natural areas management
* Private Sector: Corporate campus restoration, developer mitigation
* NGOs: Urban conservation, community greening programs
* Consulting: Urban restoration design and implementation
* Academia: Urban ecology research, community-engaged research
________________

3. Monitor building environmental system performance

   * Documents maintenance activities
   * Follows safety protocols for rooftop work
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)

* Indicators:
   * Develops maintenance schedules for green systems
   * Troubleshoots system problems
   * Manages irrigation and fertigation for green roofs/walls
   * Operates rainwater harvesting and greywater systems
   * Conducts system performance monitoring
   * Ensures regulatory compliance for water reuse
   * Manages contractors for specialized maintenance

   * Uses building management systems for green features
ADVANCED (6-10 years)

* Indicators:
   * Manages comprehensive building sustainability programs
   * Evaluates green building system performance
   * Optimizes systems for efficiency and outcomes
   * Develops maintenance protocols and standards
   * Integrates multiple green building systems
   * Uses building monitoring data for improvements
   * Provides LEED or similar certification support
   * Trains building operations professionals
   * Develops sustainability reporting for buildings
   * Provides green building consulting
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes green building operations practices
* Application: Provides strategic green building leadership
* Indicators:
   * Directs sustainability programs for building portfolios
   * Develops innovative green building approaches
   * Influences green building standards and policy
   * Achieves recognition for green building leadership
   * Demonstrates significant environmental benefits from green buildings
   * Mentors green building professionals
   * Advances green building technology and practice
Sector-Specific Applications:

DOMAIN-LEVEL COMPETENCY FRAMEWORK
Cross-Cutting Competencies (Applied across all skill areas in Domain 4)
Safety Consciousness
* Prioritizing personal and crew safety in all field activities
* Conducting hazard assessments before beginning work
* Using appropriate personal protective equipment
* Knowing when to stop work due to unsafe conditions
Environmental Awareness
* Understanding how field activities affect ecosystems
* Minimizing impacts during implementation
* Recognizing sensitive resources and conditions
* Adapting techniques to protect resources
Physical Fitness & Endurance
* Maintaining fitness for arduous field work
* Working effectively in challenging environmental conditions
* Managing fatigue and maintaining performance
* Understanding physical limitations and seeking help when needed
Adaptability & Problem-Solving
* Adapting plans to field conditions and unexpected challenges
* Troubleshooting equipment problems
* Finding creative solutions with available resources
* Learning from experience and adjusting approaches
Attention to Detail
* Following protocols precisely
* Maintaining data quality
* Ensuring work meets specifications
* Catching errors before they become problems
________________


IMPLEMENTATION GUIDANCE
For Organizations Using This Framework:
* Hiring: Use proficiency levels to write position descriptions matching field operations roles
* Training: Develop progressive training programs aligned with proficiency indicators
* Safety: Ensure foundational safety proficiency before advancing to complex operations
* Equipment: Provide appropriate equipment and ensure operator qualifications
* Supervision: Structure supervision based on proficiency levels
* Career Development: Create advancement pathways from technician to specialist to manager
For Individuals Using This Framework:
* Skill Building: Seek opportunities to develop proficiency across multiple skill areas
* Certifications: Obtain required certifications (pesticide applicator, First Aid, chainsaw, CDL, Part 107)
* Physical Fitness: Maintain fitness enabling safe field work
* Mentorship: Learn from experienced field practitioners
* Technology: Develop digital skills alongside traditional field craft
* Safety: Prioritize safety training and maintain currency
________________


ACKNOWLEDGMENTS & USAGE NOTES
This Domain 4 framework recognizes that:
* Field operations are where conservation happens on the ground
* Technology is transforming field work but hands-on skills remain essential
* Safety is paramount in all field operations
* Physical demands require fitness and self-awareness
* Weather and conditions affect all field activities
* Teamwork and communication are critical in field settings
Organizations may adapt this framework with appropriate citation, maintaining commitment to:
* Safety as the highest priority
* Integration of traditional skills with emerging technology
* Progressive skill development with appropriate supervision
* Equity and access to field careers
* Environmental protection during all field activities
FEEDBACK ENCOURAGED: Users are encouraged to provide feedback on framework utility, gaps, and improvements to support ongoing evolution.
________________


This completes Domain 4: Field Operations Skills. The framework provides comprehensive guidance for developing practical field capabilities from entry level through expert, integrating traditional field craft with modern technology while prioritizing safety and environmental protection.
________________


DOMAIN 5: COMMUNICATION & ENGAGEMENT SKILLS
Connecting with people about land and resource management, community needs, and stewardship goals




DOMAIN OVERVIEW


Communication and engagement skills are essential for translating environmental science into action, building support for conservation initiatives, and ensuring that diverse perspectives shape management decisions. These skills help environmental professionals work effectively across boundaries—between disciplines, organizations, cultures, and communities—to achieve shared environmental goals.


This domain encompasses six skill areas that span from basic information sharing to complex stakeholder processes and behavioral change strategies. Professionals may specialize in particular skill areas (such as environmental education or tribal consultation) or develop broad capabilities across multiple areas depending on their role and career stage.


Example Positions Within This Domain


Professionals whose work falls primarily within Domain 5 include (but are not limited to):


Public Communication & Media Roles:
* Public Information Officer / Public Affairs Specialist
* Communications Director
* Social Media Manager / Digital Communications Specialist
* Science Communicator / Science Writer
* Interpretive Ranger / Naturalist
* Environmental Journalist
* Media Relations Specialist


Stakeholder Engagement & Facilitation Roles:
Stakeholder Engagement Coordinator
Community Relations Manager
Public Involvement Specialist
Professional Facilitator / Mediator
Collaborative Conservation Coordinator
Consensus Building Practitioner
Partnership Coordinator


Cultural Competency & Tribal Relations Roles:
* Tribal Liaison Officer / Tribal Relations Coordinator
* Cultural Resource Specialist
* Indigenous Partnership Manager
* Environmental Justice Coordinator
* Diversity, Equity & Inclusion Manager
* Cultural Interpreter


Education & Outreach Roles:
* Environmental Educator
* Education Program Manager / Director
* Curriculum Specialist
* Volunteer Coordinator / Volunteer Program Manager
* Citizen Science Coordinator
* Outreach Coordinator
* Interpretive Planner
* Exhibits Developer


Customer Service & Visitor Services Roles:
* Visitor Services Manager
* Park Ranger (Interpretation)
* Visitor Center Manager
* Permit Coordinator
* Recreation Information Specialist
* Customer Service Representative


Social Science & Behavior Change Roles:
* Behavior Change Specialist
* Social Marketing Manager
* Community Organizer / Grassroots Organizer
* Social Scientist / Human Dimensions Specialist
* Community-Based Researcher
* Program Evaluator
* Public Engagement Researcher


How Advanced Technologies Are Changing This Domain


Communication and engagement practices are being transformed by technological innovation, creating both new opportunities and challenges for environmental professionals:


Artificial Intelligence & Automation:
* AI-powered chatbots handle routine visitor inquiries and permit questions 24/7
* Natural language processing helps analyze thousands of public comments in planning processes
* Machine learning identifies sentiment and themes in social media conversations about environmental issues
* Automated translation enables real-time multilingual engagement
* AI assists in creating personalized content for different audience segments


Digital Engagement Platforms:
* Virtual and augmented reality create immersive interpretive experiences accessible from anywhere
* 3D visualization tools help communities envision proposed projects and alternatives
* Online collaboration platforms enable stakeholder engagement across distances
* Interactive mapping tools allow public input on spatial planning decisions
* Digital storytelling platforms (StoryMaps, multimedia narratives) make complex information engaging


Data Analytics & Personalization:
* Audience analytics reveal what content resonates with different demographic groups
* Predictive modeling identifies communities most likely to engage in conservation behaviors
* Real-time dashboards track engagement metrics and inform adaptive strategies
* A/B testing optimizes messages and outreach approaches
* Behavioral data informs targeting of interventions


Mobile & Location-Based Technology:
* Mobile apps deliver context-aware interpretation triggered by visitor location
* Push notifications provide timely safety information or program updates
* Mobile data collection enables community science at unprecedented scales
* QR codes on trail signs link to rich multimedia content
* Geofencing enables location-specific messaging and engagement


Social Media & Influencer Marketing:
* Micro-influencers amplify conservation messages to niche audiences
* User-generated content creates authentic connections with places and issues
* Social listening tools monitor public sentiment and emerging concerns
* Live streaming brings field activities to remote audiences
* Algorithm optimization maximizes organic reach


Accessibility Technologies:
* Screen readers and alt-text make digital content accessible to visually impaired users
* Automated captioning makes video content accessible to deaf/hard-of-hearing audiences
* Voice interfaces provide hands-free access to information
* Adjustable text size and contrast improve readability
* Assistive devices connect to digital infrastructure for enhanced experiences


Challenges & Considerations:


While these technologies create powerful new capabilities, they also introduce challenges:
* Digital divide: Not all communities have equal access to technology
* Authenticity concerns: Virtual engagement may lack depth of in-person relationships
* Privacy & data ethics: Collection of user data raises ethical questions
* Misinformation: Digital platforms can spread false environmental information rapidly
* Algorithm bias: AI systems may perpetuate existing inequities
* Screen fatigue: Audiences may crave in-person connection after pandemic-era virtualization
* Technology dependence: Over-reliance on technology may erode traditional engagement skills


Future-Ready Skills:


Environmental communicators must now develop:
* Digital literacy and comfort with emerging platforms
* Data interpretation and visualization skills
* Understanding of AI capabilities and limitations
* Critical evaluation of technology's social and equity implications
* Ability to blend high-tech and high-touch engagement approaches
* Skills to counter misinformation and build digital trust


The most effective practitioners blend technological innovation with fundamental human relationship-building, using technology to enhance—not replace—authentic engagement.


Cross-Sector Applications


Communication and engagement skills are universally valuable across all environmental sectors:


Public Agencies: Community meetings, tribal consultation, public comment processes, visitor services
Private Sector: Stakeholder engagement for projects, ESG communications, community relations
NGOs: Fundraising narratives, volunteer coordination, coalition building, advocacy campaigns
Consulting: Facilitation services, public involvement planning, conflict resolution
Academia: Science communication, community-based research, extension services
Tribal Nations: Government-to-government consultation, traditional knowledge sharing


Career Progression in This Domain


Professionals typically develop communication and engagement capabilities along three dimensions:


1. Audience Complexity: From general public → diverse stakeholders → contested/conflict situations
2. Communication Mode: From written/one-way → presentations → facilitation → negotiation
3. Cultural Sophistication: From single culture → cross-cultural → Indigenous knowledge integration


---


DOMAIN-LEVEL CAREER PATHWAYS


Pathway 1: Public Engagement Specialist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Executive


Entry (0-3 years): Visitor Services Representative, Environmental Educator, Communications Coordinator
  Focus: 5.1, 5.4, 5.5
  Typical setting: Parks, nature centers, agency offices
  
Mid (3-7 years): Public Affairs Specialist, Stakeholder Engagement Coordinator, Interpretation Manager
  Focus: 5.1, 5.2, 5.4, 5.6
  Typical setting: Agency regional offices, NGO programs, corporate sustainability departments
  
Senior (7-15 years): Public Involvement Manager, Community Relations Director, Chief of Interpretation
  Focus: All skill areas, specialization in 5.2, 5.3, 5.6
  Typical setting: Agency headquarters, large NGOs, major corporations
  
Executive (15+ years): Director of Communications, Chief Public Affairs Officer, Executive Director (NGO)
  Focus: Strategic oversight of all communication functions
  Typical setting: Organization-wide leadership


Pathway 2: Cultural Resources & Tribal Relations Specialist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Tribal Liaison Assistant, Cultural Resources Technician
  Focus: 5.3, 5.5
  Typical setting: Agencies with tribal trust responsibilities, CRM firms
  
Mid (3-7 years): Tribal Relations Coordinator, Cultural Resources Specialist
  Focus: 5.2, 5.3
  Typical setting: Federal/state agencies, tribal organizations
  
Senior (7-15 years): Tribal Consultation Manager, Cultural Resources Program Manager
  Focus: 5.2, 5.3, plus leadership in 6.5 (Intergovernmental Relations)
  Typical setting: Agency leadership, tribal governments
  
Expert (15+ years): Director of Tribal Relations, Traditional Ecological Knowledge Coordinator
  Focus: Strategic partnership development, policy influence
  Typical setting: Senior agency positions, tribal leadership


Pathway 3: Facilitation & Conflict Resolution Professional
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Meeting Support Specialist, Junior Facilitator
  Focus: 5.1, 5.2
  Typical setting: Consulting firms, agencies
  
Mid (3-7 years): Professional Facilitator, Stakeholder Process Designer
  Focus: 5.2, 5.6
  Typical setting: Consulting, agency public involvement units
  
Senior (7-15 years): Lead Mediator, Collaborative Process Manager
  Focus: 5.2, 5.3, 5.6
  Typical setting: Private practice, senior consulting roles
  
Expert (15+ years): Master Facilitator, Conflict Resolution Consultant
  Focus: High-stakes negotiation, process design for complex multi-party situations
  Typical setting: Private practice, international consulting


Pathway 4: Environmental Education & Behavior Change Specialist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert


Entry (0-3 years): Environmental Educator, Outreach Coordinator, Citizen Science Assistant
  Focus: 5.4
  Typical setting: Nature centers, schools, NGOs
  
Mid (3-7 years): Education Program Manager, Community Outreach Manager, Social Marketing Specialist
  Focus: 5.1, 5.4, 5.6
  Typical setting: Environmental organizations, agencies, universities
  
Senior (7-15 years): Director of Education, Community Engagement Director, Behavior Change Strategist
  Focus: 5.4, 5.6, program design and evaluation
  Typical setting: Large NGOs, agencies, consulting firms
  
Expert (15+ years): Chief Education Officer, National Program Director, Social Science Consultant
  Focus: Strategy, research, organizational leadership
  Typical setting: National organizations, research institutions


---


REPRESENTATIVE JOB ROLE PROFILES


Profile 1: Public Affairs Specialist (Mid-Career)
Typical Employers: Federal/state agencies, large NGOs, corporations with environmental footprint


Primary Skill Areas: 5.1 (Public Communication), 5.2 (Stakeholder Engagement), 5.4 (Education & Outreach)


Proficiency Requirements:
5.1.1-5.1.5: Advanced level
5.2.1-5.2.3: Intermediate to Advanced
5.4.1, 5.4.5: Intermediate


Typical Responsibilities:
Lead public comment processes for major agency actions
Draft fact sheets, talking points, and press releases
Coordinate with media and respond to inquiries
Organize and facilitate community meetings
Manage social media presence
Support stakeholder identification and analysis


Related Credentials: 
Bachelor's in Communications, Environmental Studies, Public Relations, or related field
Professional certifications: IAP2 (International Association for Public Participation), APR (Accredited in Public Relations)


Salary Range: $60,000-$90,000 (varies by sector and region)


---


Profile 2: Tribal Liaison Officer (Senior)
Typical Employers: Federal agencies (BLM, Forest Service, BIA, EPA, DOE), state natural resource agencies, large infrastructure projects


Primary Skill Areas: 5.3 (Cultural Competency), 5.2 (Stakeholder Engagement), 6.5 (Intergovernmental Relations)


Proficiency Requirements:
5.3.1-5.3.3: Advanced to Expert level
5.2.1-5.2.4: Advanced
Strong cross-domain knowledge in policy and law


Typical Responsibilities:
Coordinate government-to-government consultation processes
Advise agency leadership on tribal interests and treaty rights
Facilitate integration of Traditional Ecological Knowledge into management plans
Manage cultural resource compliance and protection
Build and maintain long-term relationships with tribal governments
Navigate complex jurisdictional issues


Related Credentials:
Bachelor's or Master's in Native American Studies, Anthropology, Natural Resources, or related field
Specialized training in tribal consultation, NAGPRA, Section 106 compliance
Membership in or strong relationships with tribal communities often preferred


Salary Range: $75,000-$115,000


---


Profile 3: Community Engagement Manager (Mid-Senior)
Typical Employers: NGOs, consulting firms, corporate sustainability departments, municipalities


Primary Skill Areas: 5.2 (Stakeholder Engagement), 5.6 (Social Science & Behavioral Change), 5.4 (Education & Outreach)


Proficiency Requirements:
5.2.1-5.2.5: Advanced
5.6.1-5.6.5: Intermediate to Advanced
5.4.1, 5.4.3: Intermediate


Typical Responsibilities:
Design and implement community engagement strategies for environmental projects
Facilitate multi-stakeholder processes and collaborative planning
Apply social science methods to understand community needs and behaviors
Develop and manage community partnerships
Create culturally responsive engagement approaches
Evaluate engagement effectiveness and adapt strategies


Related Credentials:
Bachelor's or Master's in Environmental Science, Public Policy, Sociology, Urban Planning
Certifications in facilitation, community organizing, or collaborative processes
Bilingual/multilingual skills highly valued


Salary Range: $65,000-$100,000


---


Profile 4: Environmental Education Director (Senior)
Typical Employers: Nature centers, environmental education nonprofits, parks, museums, school districts


Primary Skill Areas: 5.4 (Education & Outreach), 5.1 (Public Communication), 5.6 (Social Science & Behavioral Change)


Proficiency Requirements:
5.4.1-5.4.5: Advanced to Expert
5.1.1, 5.1.3, 5.1.5: Advanced
5.6.1, 5.6.5: Intermediate to Advanced


Typical Responsibilities:
Oversee environmental education program portfolio
Design curriculum and interpretive experiences
Manage education staff and volunteers
Develop evaluation frameworks for learning outcomes
Secure funding through grants and partnerships
Integrate behavior change principles into programming


Related Credentials:
Bachelor's required; Master's preferred in Environmental Education, Education, or related field
Teaching certification and/or NAI (National Association for Interpretation) certification
Experience with program evaluation and grant management


Salary Range: $55,000-$85,000 (nonprofit); $70,000-$105,000 (government)


---


Profile 5: Facilitation & Mediation Consultant (Expert)
Typical Employers: Self-employed, consulting firms specializing in environmental conflict, agencies (contracted)


Primary Skill Areas: 5.2 (Stakeholder Engagement), 5.3 (Cultural Competency), 5.6 (Social Science & Behavioral Change)


Proficiency Requirements:
5.2.2-5.2.4: Expert level
5.3.1, 5.3.5: Advanced
5.6.2, 5.6.3: Advanced


Typical Responsibilities:
Design and facilitate complex multi-party environmental negotiations
Mediate disputes over natural resource management
Train organizational staff in conflict resolution and facilitation
Apply interest-based negotiation and consensus-building methods
Navigate high-stakes, values-based conflicts
Ensure equitable and inclusive processes


Related Credentials:
Master's degree often expected (conflict resolution, public policy, environmental management)
Professional mediation certification (state bar, USIECR, CDR Associates)
Extensive facilitation training and practice hours
10+ years experience in environmental conflicts


Salary Range: $100,000-$200,000+ (consulting rates vary widely)


---


# SKILL AREAS (5.1 5.6)


---


5.1 PUBLIC COMMUNICATION
Sharing information with the public in clear, compelling ways


Skills related to communicating effectively with diverse audiences about land management activities, natural resources, and environmental issues.


---


5.1.1 Written communication for diverse audiences
Creating clear written materials that effectively reach different groups, from technical reports for professionals to simple explanations for general audiences.


#Job Tasks:
1. Draft fact sheets and brochures for public land use guidelines
2. Translate scientific reports into plain-language summaries for the public
3. Tailor communication to different audiences, such as policymakers, youth, or local communities


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands basic principles of plain language and audience awareness
*Application*: Creates simple documents with supervision; identifies target audience
*Indicators*:
  Writes clear sentences with appropriate reading level for general audiences
  Uses templates effectively with guidance
  Incorporates feedback to revise drafts
  Identifies when technical jargon needs explanation


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies audience analysis frameworks; understands literacy levels and accessibility standards
*Application*: Independently creates diverse written products; adapts tone and format
*Indicators*:
  Produces publication-ready content with minimal revision
  Adapts complex technical information for multiple audiences without losing accuracy
  Designs documents following accessibility guidelines (readability, visual hierarchy)
  Conducts basic audience analysis to inform writing approach


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in strategic communication; understands cultural and linguistic nuances
*Application*: Leads writing projects; mentors others; ensures organizational consistency
*Indicators*:
  Translates highly complex or controversial topics for diverse stakeholders
  Develops style guides and writing standards for teams
  Crafts narratives that connect emotionally while maintaining scientific credibility
  Evaluates and improves others' written products
  Navigates sensitive topics with appropriate framing


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes organizational voice; influences field-wide communication practices
*Application*: Sets communication strategy; represents organization in high-stakes situations
*Indicators*:
  Develops innovative communication approaches that become models for the field
  Writes for national audiences, congressional testimony, or major media
  Advises leadership on communication strategy during crises or controversies
  Publishes on communication best practices


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: NEPA documents, resource management plans, visitor guides, public notices
Private Sector: Sustainability reports, ESG disclosures, community newsletters, impact assessments
NGOs: Annual reports, donor communications, advocacy materials, campaign messaging
Consulting: Technical memoranda, permit applications, public involvement summaries
Academia: Extension publications, policy briefs, research summaries for public audiences


---


5.1.2 Public speaking and presentation
Delivering effective talks and presentations about land management to groups of various sizes, from small community meetings to large conferences.


#Job Tasks:
1. Deliver presentations at community meetings or public hearings
2. Represent agency goals and findings at conferences or stakeholder forums
3. Prepare and practice accessible messages for diverse and non-technical audiences


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands presentation basics (structure, visual aids, timing)
*Application*: Delivers short presentations to supportive audiences with preparation
*Indicators*:
  Presents clearly to small, familiar groups (10-30 people)
  Uses slides or visual aids appropriately with guidance
  Responds to basic questions accurately
  Demonstrates appropriate body language and voice projection


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies principles of persuasive communication; adapts to audience dynamics
*Application*: Presents confidently to diverse audiences; handles most Q&A effectively
*Indicators*:
  Engages audiences of various sizes (50-100+) and types
  Adjusts presentation on the fly based on audience response
  Handles challenging or critical questions professionally
  Creates compelling visual presentations
  Uses storytelling techniques to maintain interest


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in audience engagement; skilled in managing group dynamics
*Application*: Presents in high-stakes or contentious settings; mentors speakers
*Indicators*:
  Effectively presents in heated public hearings or contentious meetings
  Facilitates interactive presentations that engage participants
  Coaches others on presentation skills
  Represents organization at major conferences or media interviews
  Uses advanced techniques (panel moderation, keynote delivery)


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Recognized thought leader; shapes how topics are communicated in field
*Application*: Keynote speaker; handles highest-stakes communication situations
*Indicators*:
  Delivers compelling keynotes to audiences of 500+
  Manages hostile or crisis communication situations effectively
  Provides expert testimony in legal or legislative settings
  Mentors senior staff on executive communication
  Develops organizational spokesperson programs


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Town halls, scoping meetings, board presentations, congressional testimony
Private Sector: Investor presentations, community forums for projects, industry conferences
NGOs: Fundraising events, advocacy rallies, board meetings, coalition gatherings
Consulting: Client presentations, public hearing representation, expert testimony
Academia: Conference presentations, extension workshops, webinars, classroom teaching


---


5.1.3 Environmental interpretation
Explaining natural and cultural features in ways that help people connect emotionally and intellectually with the landscape, often through tours, signs, or programs.


#Job Tasks:
1. Lead guided nature walks that highlight ecological or cultural features
2. Create interpretive signs and exhibits for visitor centers or trails
3. Incorporate storytelling techniques to connect audiences with environmental themes


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands basic interpretive principles; knows site-specific content
*Application*: Delivers scripted tours or programs with supervision
*Indicators*:
  Conducts short interpretive walks or programs (30-45 minutes)
  Follows interpretive plans or scripts effectively
  Engages visitors with enthusiasm and accurate information
  Responds to basic visitor questions
  Demonstrates safety awareness during programs


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies interpretive themes and techniques; understands diverse learning styles
*Application*: Designs and delivers original programs; adapts to audience needs
*Indicators*:
  Creates new interpretive programs aligned with site themes
  Adapts programs for different age groups and abilities
  Uses diverse interpretive techniques (props, activities, sensory experiences)
  Develops basic interpretive media (signs, trail guides)
  Facilitates meaningful visitor connections with place


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in interpretive design and evaluation; skilled in multiple media
*Application*: Leads interpretive program development; trains interpreters
*Indicators*:
  Designs comprehensive interpretive plans for sites
  Creates exhibits, wayside signs, and digital media
  Trains and mentors interpretive staff and volunteers
  Evaluates interpretive effectiveness and makes improvements
  Integrates cultural resource protection into interpretation
  Develops interpretive programs addressing complex or controversial topics


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes interpretive field practices; recognized leader in interpretation
*Application*: Oversees interpretive programs at organizational level
*Indicators*:
  Directs interpretive services for complex sites or entire systems
  Publishes on interpretive practices
  Provides expert consultation on major interpretive projects
  Achieves national recognition (NAI awards, leadership positions)
  Innovates new interpretive approaches that influence the field


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: National/state park programs, visitor center exhibits, ranger-led activities
Private Sector: Corporate nature centers, ecotourism interpretive services
NGOs: Preserve tours, conservation education, volunteer training
Consulting: Interpretive planning and design services, exhibit development
Academia: Natural history museums, botanical gardens, field station programs


---

  Uses basic data visualization tools

  Crafts compelling narratives from complex research or monitoring data

5.2 STAKEHOLDER ENGAGEMENT
Working effectively with people who have an interest in the land


Skills related to involving various groups and individuals who care about or are affected by land management decisions in meaningful ways.


---


5.2.1 Stakeholder identification and analysis
Figuring out who has an interest in particular land management issues and understanding their perspectives, concerns, and needs.


#Job Tasks:
1. Create stakeholder maps that categorize interests and levels of influence
2. Conduct interviews or surveys to gather stakeholder perspectives
3. Use power-interest matrices to guide engagement strategies


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands basic stakeholder categories and why identification matters
*Application*: Assists with stakeholder lists and basic research
*Indicators*:
  Creates stakeholder lists using obvious sources
  Conducts background research on stakeholder organizations
  Assists with outreach tracking
  Recognizes major stakeholder categories (agencies, tribes, NGOs, public)


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies stakeholder analysis frameworks systematically
*Application*: Conducts comprehensive stakeholder analysis independently
*Indicators*:
  Uses multiple methods to identify stakeholders (including hard-to-reach groups)
  Creates stakeholder maps showing relationships and influence
  Applies power-interest or salience frameworks
  Identifies stakeholder concerns, interests, and potential conflicts
  Designs differentiated engagement strategies for different stakeholders


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in stakeholder dynamics; anticipates complex relationships
*Application*: Leads stakeholder analysis for complex, multi-party situations
*Indicators*:
  Conducts sophisticated social network analysis
  Identifies hidden stakeholders and power dynamics
  Anticipates coalition formation and stakeholder evolution
  Integrates stakeholder analysis into strategic planning
  Adapts engagement approaches as stakeholder landscapes shift
  Recognizes and addresses issues of representation and equity


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes how stakeholder engagement is practiced in field
*Application*: Provides strategic guidance on highest-stakes stakeholder situations
*Indicators*:
  Advises on stakeholder strategies for major controversial projects
  Develops innovative stakeholder analysis methods
  Trains practitioners in stakeholder assessment
  Publishes or presents on stakeholder engagement practices


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: NEPA scoping, land management planning, permit review processes
Private Sector: Project stakeholder analysis, social license to operate assessments
NGOs: Coalition building, campaign planning, partnership development
Consulting: Stakeholder identification services for clients, social impact assessments
Academia: Community-based participatory research, extension program design


---


5.2.2 Meeting facilitation and design
Planning and leading effective gatherings where diverse stakeholders can productively discuss issues and work toward solutions.


#Job Tasks:
1. Plan inclusive public workshops or collaborative planning sessions
2. Use facilitation techniques to manage group dynamics and build trust
3. Document meeting outcomes and follow up on commitments


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands basic meeting design and facilitation techniques
*Application*: Supports meeting logistics; co-facilitates simple meetings
*Indicators*:
  Manages meeting logistics (space, materials, technology)
  Takes notes and documents discussions accurately
  Co-facilitates small group discussions with guidance
  Uses basic facilitation tools (flip charts, small group breakouts)
  Follows meeting plans effectively


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies participatory meeting design principles; understands group dynamics
*Application*: Independently facilitates routine stakeholder meetings
*Indicators*:
  Designs meeting agendas that achieve stated objectives
  Facilitates meetings of 20-50 participants effectively
  Uses diverse engagement techniques (brainstorming, prioritization, world café)
  Manages dominant participants and draws out quieter voices
  Navigates moderate disagreement constructively
  Creates inclusive, accessible meeting environments
  Produces clear meeting summaries and action items


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in facilitation theory; skilled in conflict and complexity
*Application*: Facilitates complex multi-day processes and contentious meetings
*Indicators*:
  Designs sophisticated multi-meeting processes for complex issues
  Facilitates contentious meetings where stakeholders have significant conflicts
  Uses advanced techniques (consensus building, interest-based negotiation, scenario planning)
  Co-designs processes collaboratively with stakeholders
  Manages power dynamics and ensures equitable participation
  Trains others in facilitation skills
  Adapts facilitation approach real-time to emerging dynamics


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Master facilitator; shapes facilitation practices in environmental field
*Application*: Facilitates highest-stakes processes; advises on facilitation strategy
*Indicators*:
  Facilitates complex multi-party negotiations on major controversies
  Serves as lead facilitator for national or regional planning processes
  Provides mediation in environmental disputes
  Develops innovative facilitation methods
  Achieves recognized credentials (IAF Certified Professional Facilitator, etc.)
  Mentors senior facilitators


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Public scoping meetings, collaborative forest planning, advisory committee meetings
Private Sector: Community advisory panels, project consultation meetings
NGOs: Coalition meetings, strategy sessions, community planning workshops
Consulting: Professional facilitation services, collaborative process design
Academia: Research partnership meetings, community engagement for studies


---


5.2.3 Collaborative decision-making processes
Using approaches that meaningfully involve stakeholders in developing land management plans and decisions.


#Job Tasks:
1. Convene working groups that co-develop land management strategies
2. Use tools like consensus-building or participatory modeling to include diverse voices
3. Ensure transparency and equity in decision-making steps


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands basic collaborative approaches and why they matter
*Application*: Supports collaborative processes with logistical and administrative help
*Indicators*:
  Assists with organizing collaborative meetings
  Documents stakeholder input accurately
  Understands difference between consultation and collaboration
  Tracks commitments and action items


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies collaborative decision-making frameworks and tools
*Application*: Manages components of collaborative processes
*Indicators*:
  Designs and implements working group processes
  Uses structured decision-making tools effectively
  Ensures stakeholder input is incorporated into drafts
  Manages public comment processes fairly
  Creates transparency through documentation and communication
  Recognizes when true collaboration is happening versus performative engagement


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in collaborative governance; understands systems change
*Application*: Designs and leads major collaborative initiatives
*Indicators*:
  Establishes collaborative governance structures (advisory committees, co-management)
  Uses sophisticated tools (participatory modeling, structured decision making)
  Ensures diverse voices have genuine influence on outcomes
  Addresses power imbalances in collaborative processes
  Evaluates collaborative process quality and outcomes
  Adapts processes to address emergent challenges
  Achieves durable agreements among diverse stakeholders


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes how collaboration is practiced in natural resource management
*Application*: Leads transformative collaborative initiatives at landscape scale
*Indicators*:
  Designs innovative collaborative governance models
  Leads multi-year, multi-stakeholder landscape-scale collaborations
  Achieves recognition for collaborative leadership
  Publishes or presents on collaborative practice
  Advises policy-makers on collaborative approaches
  Mentors collaborative leaders across organizations


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Collaborative forest restoration, watershed partnerships, co-management with tribes
Private Sector: Shared-value initiatives, multi-stakeholder certification processes
NGOs: Coalition-based advocacy, community-led conservation
Consulting: Collaborative process design and support services
Academia: Co-production of knowledge, participatory action research


---


5.2.4 Conflict resolution and mediation
Helping people with different views find common ground and work through disagreements about land management issues.


#Job Tasks:
1. Mediate disputes between stakeholders over land use or project impacts
2. Apply active listening and reframing techniques to de-escalate tensions
3. Facilitate agreements that reflect shared goals while honoring diverse needs


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands sources of environmental conflict; basic conflict resolution concepts
*Application*: Recognizes conflict; uses basic de-escalation techniques
*Indicators*:
  Remains calm and professional in tense situations
  Uses active listening skills
  Recognizes when to escalate to experienced conflict practitioners
  Understands difference between positions and interests
  Avoids inflaming conflicts through communication


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies conflict resolution frameworks; understands negotiation theory
*Application*: Manages moderate conflicts; facilitates resolution conversations
*Indicators*:
  Facilitates conversations between parties in moderate disputes
  Uses reframing and interest-based approaches
  Helps parties develop options that meet multiple interests
  Recognizes conflict escalation patterns
  Addresses conflicts before they become entrenched
  Distinguishes conflicts that can be resolved vs. managed


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in environmental conflict; skilled in mediation practice
*Application*: Mediates complex multi-party environmental disputes
*Indicators*:
  Serves as mediator in formal environmental dispute resolution
  Manages values-based conflicts effectively
  Uses advanced techniques (caucusing, shuttle diplomacy, consensus building)
  Addresses historical grievances and trust deficits
  Achieves durable agreements in contentious situations
  Trains others in conflict resolution
  Completes professional mediation training


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Master mediator; recognized expert in environmental conflict
*Application*: Mediates highest-stakes disputes; shapes conflict resolution practice
*Indicators*:
  Mediates major environmental controversies (large projects, lawsuits, policy disputes)
  Develops innovative conflict resolution approaches
  Achieves professional mediation credentials and recognition
  Provides expert testimony on conflict resolution processes
  Publishes on environmental conflict and resolution
  Influences conflict resolution policy and practice


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Dispute resolution in planning processes, appeals, interagency conflicts
Private Sector: Community relations conflicts, permit challenges, stakeholder disputes
NGOs: Intra-coalition conflicts, community organizing disputes
Consulting: Professional mediation services, conflict assessment
Academia: Research partnership conflicts, campus-community disputes


---

Building and maintaining productive relationships with organizations, agencies, or communities to achieve shared land management goals.


#Job Tasks:
1. Establish and maintain partnerships with NGOs, agencies, and community groups
2. Develop MOUs or agreements that outline shared roles and objectives
3. Coordinate joint initiatives and track partnership outcomes


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:

*Indicators*:
  Serves as point of contact for routine partner communications
  Coordinates logistics for partnership activities
  Tracks partnership activities and outcomes
  Represents organization appropriately in partnership meetings


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)

*Application*: Initiates and manages individual partnerships
*Indicators*:
  Identifies potential new partners strategically
  Negotiates partnership terms and agreements
  Develops work plans for joint initiatives
  Addresses partnership challenges proactively
  Manages partner expectations effectively
  Evaluates partnership effectiveness


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in strategic alliance development; understands collective impact
*Application*: Builds and manages complex partnership networks
*Indicators*:
  Develops comprehensive partnership strategies
  Builds and maintains portfolio of diverse partnerships
  Creates multi-stakeholder collaboratives and coalitions
  Secures resources for partnership initiatives
  Navigates complex organizational dynamics across partners
  Achieves significant outcomes through partnership
  Develops partnership frameworks and guidance for organization


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes how partnerships function in environmental sector
*Application*: Leads transformative multi-organization initiatives
*Indicators*:
  Establishes enduring cross-sector partnerships that become models
  Leads landscape-scale partnership initiatives
  Influences partnership policy and practice
  Builds organizational capacity for partnership across sector
  Achieves recognition for partnership leadership
  Mentors partnership professionals


#Sector-Specific Applications:

Academia: Research partnerships, extension partnerships, university-community collaborations


---


5.3 CULTURAL COMPETENCY
Respecting and working effectively across diverse cultures and perspectives


Skills related to understanding, appreciating, and appropriately incorporating different cultural perspectives and practices in land management.


---


5.3.1 Cross-cultural communication
Communicating effectively with people from different cultural backgrounds, being mindful of varying communication styles and cultural norms.


#Job Tasks:
1. Adapt language and communication styles to different cultural contexts
2. Seek input from cultural liaisons when planning public engagement activities
3. Respect cultural protocols during meetings, field visits, or ceremonies


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Awareness of own cultural perspective; basic understanding that cultures differ
*Application*: Shows respect for cultural differences; seeks guidance
*Indicators*:
  Demonstrates openness and humility in cross-cultural situations
  Recognizes own cultural assumptions and biases
  Asks appropriate questions about cultural protocols
  Shows willingness to adapt communication approach
  Seeks guidance from cultural experts or liaisons


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands cultural dimensions (communication styles, time orientation, hierarchy)
*Application*: Adapts communication effectively across cultural contexts
*Indicators*:
  Adjusts communication style based on cultural context
  Uses culturally appropriate forms of address and interaction
  Recognizes indirect communication and reads nonverbal cues
  Involves cultural liaisons appropriately in planning
  Avoids cultural faux pas in routine interactions
  Works effectively with interpreters


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Deep understanding of specific cultural contexts; recognizes systemic issues
*Application*: Builds authentic relationships across cultures; addresses structural barriers
*Indicators*:
  Develops trusted relationships with diverse cultural communities
  Recognizes and addresses power dynamics in cross-cultural work
  Designs culturally responsive engagement processes
  Identifies and mitigates cultural barriers to participation
  Trains others in cross-cultural competency
  Advocates for organizational cultural competency improvements


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Comprehensive cultural expertise; understands intersectionality
*Application*: Leads organizational cultural transformation
*Indicators*:
  Serves as organizational leader on cultural competency
  Mediates complex cross-cultural conflicts
  Develops organizational cultural competency frameworks
  Influences how cultural competency is practiced in sector
  Achieves recognition from diverse cultural communities for partnership quality


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Tribal consultation, environmental justice communities, immigrant communities
Private Sector: International operations, diverse workforce management, global supply chains
NGOs: Grassroots organizing, international conservation, community-based work
Consulting: Cultural resource management, cross-cultural facilitation services
Academia: International research, diverse student populations, community partnerships


---


5.3.2 Indigenous knowledge integration
Respectfully incorporating traditional ecological knowledge and indigenous perspectives into land management approaches.


#Job Tasks:
1. Collaborate with tribal representatives to incorporate Traditional Ecological Knowledge
2. Co-design restoration or monitoring plans with Indigenous partners
3. Cite and acknowledge Indigenous contributions in planning documents


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands that Indigenous knowledge exists and has value; aware of tribal sovereignty
*Application*: Shows respect for Indigenous knowledge; follows established protocols
*Indicators*:
  Recognizes tribal governments as sovereign nations
  Understands basics of government-to-government consultation
  Respects cultural protocols around knowledge sharing
  Understands sensitive nature of some traditional knowledge
  Recognizes need for proper attribution and consent


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands Traditional Ecological Knowledge (TEK) concepts; familiar with integration frameworks
*Application*: Facilitates TEK integration into projects with guidance
*Indicators*:
  Identifies opportunities to incorporate Indigenous knowledge
  Works respectfully with tribal representatives and knowledge holders
  Understands intellectual property considerations for traditional knowledge
  Documents Indigenous knowledge appropriately (with permission)
  Recognizes complementarity of TEK and Western science
  Supports Indigenous-led monitoring or management


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Deep understanding of TEK systems; skilled in co-management approaches
*Application*: Leads TEK integration initiatives and co-management projects
*Indicators*:
  Designs and implements co-management or collaborative management agreements
  Facilitates co-production of knowledge
  Ensures Indigenous knowledge holders are compensated and credited
  Addresses power imbalances in knowledge integration
  Navigates complex IP and data sovereignty issues
  Develops organizational capacity for TEK integration
  Builds long-term trusted relationships with tribal communities


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Recognized expert in TEK integration and Indigenous partnerships
*Application*: Leads transformative co-management initiatives; influences policy
*Indicators*:
  Establishes innovative co-management models
  Influences policy on Indigenous knowledge and co-management
  Receives recognition from tribal communities for partnership quality
  Publishes on TEK integration (with Indigenous co-authors)
  Mentors practitioners in Indigenous partnerships
  Advances tribal self-determination through professional practice


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Co-management agreements, Section 106 consultation, NAGPRA compliance
Private Sector: Free, Prior, and Informed Consent processes, Indigenous employment
NGOs: Support for Indigenous-led conservation, land back initiatives
Consulting: TEK documentation (with consent), co-management facilitation
Academia: Collaborative research with tribal colleges, Indigenous research methodologies


---


5.3.3 Cultural resource identification
Recognizing historic, archaeological, and culturally significant sites or features on the landscape that require special consideration.


#Job Tasks:
1. Conduct field surveys to identify historic or sacred sites
2. Document and map cultural features using approved protocols
3. Coordinate with tribal historic preservation officers and cultural monitors


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Awareness that cultural resources exist and are protected by law
*Application*: Recognizes obvious cultural features; reports findings appropriately
*Indicators*:
  Identifies obvious historic structures or archaeological features
  Understands need to avoid disturbing potential cultural sites
  Reports cultural resource discoveries appropriately
  Follows Section 106 and NAGPRA awareness training
  Maintains confidentiality about site locations


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Trained in cultural resource survey methods; understands regulatory requirements
*Application*: Conducts cultural resource surveys; coordinates with specialists
*Indicators*:
  Conducts pedestrian surveys for cultural resources
  Documents cultural features following professional standards
  Recognizes diverse types of cultural resources (prehistoric, historic, traditional cultural properties)
  Coordinates with archaeologists, historians, and THPOs
  Understands Section 106 process and requirements
  Manages cultural resource data sensitively


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in cultural resource management; deep regulatory knowledge
*Application*: Leads cultural resource compliance; manages complex consultations
*Indicators*:
  Leads Section 106 consultation processes
  Evaluates National Register eligibility
  Develops treatment plans for cultural resources
  Manages relationships with multiple THPOs and SHPOs
  Integrates cultural resource protection into project design
  Trains field staff in cultural resource awareness
  Addresses conflicts between development and protection


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Comprehensive cultural resource expertise; shapes CRM practice
*Application*: Provides expert guidance on complex cultural resource issues
*Indicators*:
  Serves as cultural resource program manager
  Provides expert consultation on major cultural resource controversies
  Influences cultural resource policy and practice
  Achieves Register of Professional Archaeologists (RPA) certification or equivalent
  Publishes on cultural resource management
  Mediates complex cultural resource disputes


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Section 106 compliance, cultural resource programs, heritage areas
Private Sector: Cultural resource surveys for development, NEPA compliance
NGOs: Historic preservation advocacy, cultural landscape conservation
Consulting: Cultural resource management services, archaeological consulting
Academia: Archaeology, historic preservation programs, museum curation


---


5.3.4 Diversity, equity, and inclusion practices
Ensuring that land management programs and decisions are fair, accessible, and responsive to all communities.


#Job Tasks:
1. Evaluate program accessibility and participation across demographic groups
2. Implement hiring or outreach practices that reduce barriers for underrepresented communities
3. Monitor and report on DEI goals in workforce or program outcomes


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Awareness of DEI concepts; understands why diversity matters
*Application*: Demonstrates inclusive behavior; participates in DEI initiatives
*Indicators*:
  Uses inclusive language
  Recognizes own privileges and biases
  Participates in DEI training
  Considers accessibility in routine work
  Treats colleagues and stakeholders equitably


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands structural inequities; familiar with DEI frameworks and tools
*Application*: Integrates DEI considerations into projects and programs
*Indicators*:
  Conducts equity assessments for programs or projects
  Identifies and addresses barriers to participation
  Ensures outreach reaches diverse communities
  Collects and analyzes demographic data
  Designs accessible communications and events
  Contributes to workplace DEI initiatives


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in DEI strategy; understands systemic approaches to equity
*Application*: Leads organizational DEI initiatives; embeds equity in operations
*Indicators*:
  Develops and implements comprehensive DEI strategies
  Conducts DEI audits and develops action plans
  Establishes DEI metrics and accountability systems
  Diversifies hiring, contracting, and partnership practices
  Addresses institutional barriers to equity
  Trains staff and leadership on DEI practices
  Centers equity in decision-making processes


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes how DEI is practiced in environmental sector
*Application*: Leads transformative organizational and sectoral change
*Indicators*:
  Drives organizational culture change toward equity and inclusion
  Influences sector-wide DEI practices and standards
  Achieves measurable progress on workforce and program diversity
  Publishes or presents on DEI in environmental fields
  Provides DEI consultation to other organizations
  Receives recognition for DEI leadership


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Workforce diversity, equitable service delivery, Title VI compliance
Private Sector: Corporate DEI programs, supplier diversity, workforce development
NGOs: Board and staff diversity, equitable programming, movement building
Consulting: DEI assessment and strategy services
Academia: Faculty diversity, inclusive pedagogy, equitable research partnerships


---


5.3.5 Environmental justice principles
Understanding how environmental benefits and burdens affect different communities and working to ensure that land management promotes fairness.


#Job Tasks:
1. Assess disproportionate environmental burdens and benefits across communities
2. Engage frontline communities early in planning and decision-making
3. Prioritize actions that address historical inequities in land access or management


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands environmental justice (EJ) concepts; aware of disproportionate impacts
*Application*: Recognizes EJ issues; considers equity in work
*Indicators*:
  Defines environmental justice and can give examples
  Recognizes environmental justice communities
  Understands concept of disproportionate impact
  Aware of Executive Order 12898 and other EJ policy
  Considers equity when planning outreach or projects


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies EJ screening tools; understands structural causes of environmental injustice
*Application*: Integrates EJ analysis into projects and planning
*Indicators*:
  Uses EJ screening tools (EJSCREEN, CalEnviroScreen, etc.)
  Identifies environmental justice communities and concerns
  Conducts meaningful engagement with frontline communities
  Analyzes distribution of benefits and burdens
  Considers cumulative impacts
  Documents EJ considerations in NEPA or similar processes


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in EJ analysis and policy; understands intersectionality
*Application*: Leads EJ initiatives; ensures equity is centered in decisions
*Indicators*:
  Conducts comprehensive EJ impact assessments
  Designs projects that affirmatively address environmental injustice
  Builds authentic partnerships with EJ communities
  Ensures EJ communities have meaningful role in decision-making
  Addresses historical harms and patterns of exclusion
  Influences organizational EJ policy and practice
  Applies participatory research and co-design approaches


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Recognized EJ leader; shapes sector understanding and practice
*Application*: Advances environmental justice at systemic/policy level
*Indicators*:
  Leads transformative EJ initiatives with measurable outcomes
  Influences EJ policy at agency or governmental level
  Develops innovative EJ frameworks and tools
  Achieves recognition from EJ communities for partnership quality
  Publishes on environmental justice practice
  Mentors EJ practitioners across sector
  Advocates for systemic change to address root causes


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: EJ analysis for permitting, equitable distribution of resources, Title VI
Private Sector: Community benefit agreements, impact mitigation, just transition planning
NGOs: EJ advocacy, community organizing, frontline community support
Consulting: EJ assessment services, community engagement for EJ communities
Academia: EJ research, community-based participatory research, policy analysis


---

3. Compile and analyze data contributions from citizen scientists

  Communicates results back to participants

  Designs citizen science projects aligned with research or monitoring needs

  Uses technology platforms effectively (mobile apps, online portals)
  Engages diverse participants
  Analyzes citizen science data for management applications
  Publishes or presents citizen science results

*Indicators*:
  Develops comprehensive citizen science programs with multiple projects

  Ensures data quality meets scientific standards
  Publishes peer-reviewed papers using citizen science data
  Secures funding for citizen science initiatives
  Builds networks of citizen scientists across regions
  Evaluates social and scientific outcomes of projects
  Mentors other citizen science coordinators

  Directs citizen science at organizational or national level

5.5 CUSTOMER SERVICE
Providing helpful, positive experiences for land users


Skills related to ensuring that people who visit or use public lands have their needs met and receive assistance when needed.


---


5.5.1 Visitor information services
Providing accurate and helpful information to people about natural areas, including orientation, safety information, and answers to questions.


#Job Tasks:
1. Greet and assist visitors at ranger stations, trailheads, or visitor centers
2. Provide maps, safety information, and recommendations tailored to visitor needs
3. Track visitor questions and concerns to inform communication improvements


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Knows site-specific information; understands basic customer service
*Application*: Provides routine information to visitors
*Indicators*:
  Greets visitors warmly and professionally
  Answers common questions accurately about site
  Provides appropriate maps and materials
  Recognizes when to refer questions to others
  Maintains organized information desk or station
  Documents visitor contacts


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Deep site knowledge; understands visitor needs and motivations
*Application*: Provides comprehensive information; handles complex situations
*Indicators*:
  Provides detailed, tailored recommendations to visitors
  Anticipates visitor needs based on conditions and circumstances
  Handles complaints or difficult situations diplomatically
  Educates visitors about regulations and safety
  Adapts information for visitors with different needs or abilities
  Uses multiple languages or interpretation services
  Tracks trends in visitor inquiries


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in visitor services; understands service quality management
*Application*: Leads visitor services operations
*Indicators*:
  Manages visitor contact stations or centers
  Develops visitor information strategies and materials
  Trains and supervises visitor services staff
  Implements service quality standards
  Analyzes visitor data to improve services
  Addresses systematic visitor service issues
  Represents organization in visitor service initiatives


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes visitor services practices in sector
*Application*: Directs visitor services at organizational level
*Indicators*:
  Develops visitor service standards and frameworks
  Leads visitor experience strategy
  Achieves recognition for service excellence
  Influences visitor services policy and practice
  Mentors visitor services professionals
  Publishes on visitor services best practices


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Visitor centers, entrance stations, ranger contacts
Private Sector: Resort information services, ecotourism operations
NGOs: Preserve visitor services, membership services
Consulting: Visitor services planning and training
Academia: Campus visitor services, field station orientation


---


5.5.2 Permit administration
Managing systems for issuing and tracking permits for activities like camping, special events, or resource use on public lands.


#Job Tasks:
1. Process applications and issue permits for recreation, research, or resource use
2. Ensure permit holders understand rules, regulations, and environmental responsibilities
3. Maintain accurate records and track permit compliance


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands permit types and basic regulations
*Application*: Processes routine permit applications
*Indicators*:
  Issues permits following established procedures
  Explains permit conditions clearly to applicants
  Maintains accurate permit records
  Handles routine permit questions
  Collects appropriate fees


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
