*Application*: Manages permit operations; addresses complex situations
*Indicators*:
  Makes permit decisions for non-routine applications
  Addresses permit violations appropriately
  Tracks permit compliance and conditions
  Analyzes permit data for management purposes
  Develops or improves permit procedures
  Trains others in permit administration
  Coordinates with enforcement staff


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in permit administration; understands regulatory frameworks
*Application*: Oversees permit programs; develops permit systems
*Indicators*:
  Designs and implements permit systems
  Develops permit policies and regulations
  Manages complex permit issues (large events, commercial use)
  Ensures permit program legal defensibility
  Uses permit data to inform management decisions
  Implements online permit systems
  Leads permit program evaluation and improvement


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes permit administration practices in sector
*Application*: Directs permit programs at organizational level
*Indicators*:
  Develops comprehensive permit management frameworks
  Influences permit policy at regional or national level
  Creates innovative permit approaches that others adopt
  Provides expert consultation on permit systems
  Publishes on permit administration
  Balances access, resource protection, and equity in permit systems


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Recreation permits, special use permits, research permits
Private Sector: Access permits, hunting leases, filming permits
NGOs: Preserve permits, event permits
Consulting: Permit system design, permitting services for clients
Academia: Research permitting support


---


5.5.3 Complaint resolution
Effectively addressing concerns or complaints from land users in a way that resolves issues and maintains positive relationships.


#Job Tasks:
1. Listen empathetically to public concerns or dissatisfaction with land use or services
2. Investigate complaints and respond with timely, respectful solutions
3. Escalate issues when needed and document outcomes for quality assurance


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands de-escalation basics; knows organizational policies
*Application*: Handles routine complaints with supervision
*Indicators*:
  Listens actively and empathetically to complaints
  Remains calm and professional in difficult situations
  Documents complaints accurately
  Recognizes when to escalate to supervisors
  Follows up on complaints appropriately


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies conflict resolution techniques; understands systemic issues
*Application*: Resolves most complaints independently
*Indicators*:
  Investigates complaints thoroughly
  Develops appropriate solutions within authority
  Communicates decisions clearly and respectfully
  Distinguishes between individual issues and systemic problems
  Tracks complaint patterns to identify improvement opportunities
  Handles emotionally charged situations effectively


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in complaint resolution and service recovery
*Application*: Handles complex, high-stakes complaints
*Indicators*:
  Resolves complex multi-party complaints
  Makes decisions on controversial issues
  Develops complaint resolution protocols and training
  Analyzes complaint data to drive organizational improvement
  Represents organization in formal complaint processes
  Turns complainants into advocates through excellent resolution
  Manages media attention on complaints appropriately


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes complaint resolution practices in sector
*Application*: Provides strategic oversight of complaint management
*Indicators*:
  Develops organizational complaint management systems
  Handles highest-stakes complaints with policy implications
  Influences customer service standards sector-wide
  Provides expert consultation on complex complaints
  Mentors complaint resolution professionals
  Uses complaint data strategically for organizational learning


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Public complaints about management, violations, access issues
Private Sector: Customer complaints, community concerns about operations
NGOs: Member complaints, community concerns
Consulting: Client satisfaction issues
Academia: Community complaints about research, campus operations


---

5.6 SOCIAL SCIENCE & BEHAVIORAL CHANGE (NEW)
Applying social science methods to understand and influence environmental behaviors


Skills related to understanding human dimensions of environmental issues and designing strategies that promote sustainable behaviors and practices.


---


5.6.1 Environmental psychology and behavior change theory
Understanding psychological factors that influence environmental behaviors and applying theories to design effective behavior change interventions.


#Job Tasks:
1. Apply behavior change frameworks (Theory of Planned Behavior, Social Cognitive Theory) to environmental programs
2. Conduct formative research to understand barriers and motivators for pro-environmental behaviors
3. Design interventions that address psychological, social, and structural influences on behavior


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Basic understanding of behavior change concepts
*Application*: Recognizes behavioral factors in environmental issues
*Indicators*:
  Explains common barriers to pro-environmental behavior
  Identifies target behaviors for change initiatives
  Understands difference between awareness and behavior change
  Recognizes when programs need behavioral approach


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies major behavior change theories and frameworks
*Application*: Designs behavior change interventions with guidance
*Indicators*:
  Selects appropriate behavior change theories for context
  Conducts audience segmentation for interventions
  Designs interventions using behavior change principles
  Uses techniques like social norming, commitment, feedback
  Evaluates behavior change program implementation


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in environmental psychology and behavior change science
*Application*: Leads comprehensive behavior change programs
*Indicators*:
  Designs theory-based, evidence-driven behavior change campaigns
  Integrates multiple strategies (education, incentives, norms, infrastructure)
  Conducts rigorous evaluation of behavior change outcomes
  Addresses structural and systemic barriers to behavior change
  Publishes or presents on behavior change practices
  Trains practitioners in behavior change approaches


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes behavior change practice in environmental sector
*Application*: Leads transformative behavior change initiatives at scale
*Indicators*:
  Develops innovative behavior change frameworks
  Demonstrates measurable population-level behavior change
  Influences behavior change policy and practice
  Conducts research advancing behavior change science
  Achieves recognition for behavior change leadership


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Visitor behavior management, conservation behaviors, energy/water conservation
Private Sector: Employee sustainability behaviors, consumer behavior change
NGOs: Campaign design, movement building, lifestyle change
Consulting: Behavior change strategy and evaluation services
Academia: Behavior change research, extension programs


---


5.6.2 Social science research methods
Conducting systematic research to understand human dimensions of environmental issues using qualitative and quantitative methods.


#Job Tasks:
1. Design and conduct surveys, interviews, or focus groups to understand stakeholder perspectives
2. Analyze quantitative and qualitative data using appropriate methods
3. Present research findings to diverse audiences in accessible formats


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands research basics; familiar with common methods
*Application*: Assists with data collection and basic analysis
*Indicators*:
  Conducts interviews or surveys following protocols
  Enters and cleans survey data
  Conducts basic descriptive analysis
  Understands research ethics (IRB, informed consent)
  Summarizes research findings


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies diverse social science research methods
*Application*: Designs and conducts research projects independently
*Indicators*:
  Designs survey instruments or interview protocols
  Selects appropriate sampling strategies
  Conducts qualitative analysis (coding, thematic analysis)
  Performs statistical analysis (regression, ANOVA, etc.)
  Ensures research quality and validity
  Navigates IRB processes
  Writes research reports


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in social science methodology; skilled in mixed methods
*Application*: Leads major research projects
*Indicators*:
  Designs comprehensive mixed-methods research projects
  Uses advanced qualitative methods (grounded theory, ethnography)
  Applies advanced statistics or modeling
  Publishes peer-reviewed research
  Secures research funding
  Integrates research into management decisions
  Trains researchers in methodology


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes social science research in environmental field
*Application*: Leads research programs; influences research-practice integration
*Indicators*:
  Directs research programs at organizational level
  Publishes extensively in peer-reviewed journals
  Develops innovative research methods
  Achieves recognition for research excellence
  Influences how human dimensions research informs environmental management
  Mentors early-career researchers


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Visitor surveys, economic valuation, human dimensions of wildlife
Private Sector: Market research, stakeholder assessment, impact evaluation
NGOs: Needs assessment, program evaluation, policy research
Consulting: Social science research services
Academia: Academic research programs, graduate student training


---


5.6.3 Community organizing and mobilization
Building grassroots power to advance environmental goals through community engagement and collective action.


#Job Tasks:
1. Identify and recruit community leaders and activists for environmental campaigns
2. Build organizational structures that sustain community engagement over time
3. Facilitate community-led action on environmental issues


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands community organizing principles
*Application*: Supports organizing activities
*Indicators*:
  Assists with outreach and recruitment
  Supports community meetings and events
  Builds relationships with community members
  Uses organizing tools (one-on-one conversations, house meetings)
  Understands power analysis basics


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies organizing frameworks and strategies
*Application*: Leads organizing campaigns
*Indicators*:
  Identifies and develops community leaders
  Designs and implements organizing campaigns
  Builds coalitions among diverse community groups
  Uses strategic power-building approaches
  Mobilizes community members for action
  Facilitates community-led decision-making
  Addresses internal group dynamics


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in community organizing; understands systems change
*Application*: Leads major organizing initiatives achieving policy change
*Indicators*:
  Designs comprehensive organizing strategies
  Builds enduring grassroots organizations
  Achieves measurable policy or practice changes through organizing
  Develops organizing infrastructure and capacity
  Navigates complex political landscapes
  Trains and mentors organizers
  Centers equity and justice in organizing


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes community organizing practices in environmental movement
*Application*: Leads transformative organizing at regional/national scale
*Indicators*:
  Directs major environmental organizing campaigns
  Builds multi-issue, multi-geography organizing networks
  Achieves recognition as organizing leader
  Influences organizing strategy and practice
  Develops next generation of organizing leaders
  Advances environmental justice through organizing


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Community-based co-management, advisory councils
Private Sector: Community relations, stakeholder organizing
NGOs: Grassroots campaigns, movement building, advocacy
Consulting: Organizing strategy services (for communities, not corporations)
Academia: Community-based participatory research, action research


---


5.6.4 Social marketing for sustainability
Applying commercial marketing techniques to promote voluntary behavior change for environmental and social good.


#Job Tasks:
1. Conduct audience research to understand motivations, barriers, and communication preferences
2. Develop marketing campaigns using the 4 Ps (product, price, place, promotion)
3. Test and refine messages and tactics based on performance data


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands social marketing basics and how it differs from commercial marketing
*Application*: Supports social marketing campaigns
*Indicators*:
  Assists with audience research
  Creates marketing materials following brand guidelines
  Implements marketing tactics (events, social media, etc.)
  Tracks basic metrics
  Understands the 4 Ps framework


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies social marketing frameworks systematically
*Application*: Designs and implements social marketing campaigns
*Indicators*:
  Conducts formative research for campaign design
  Segments audiences and develops personas
  Applies 4 Ps to design comprehensive campaigns
  Creates compelling messages and calls to action
  Uses multiple marketing channels strategically
  Tests messages and materials
  Evaluates campaign effectiveness


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in social marketing strategy and evaluation
*Application*: Leads major social marketing initiatives
*Indicators*:
  Develops comprehensive social marketing strategies
  Manages large marketing budgets
  Uses sophisticated targeting and segmentation
  Conducts A/B testing and optimization
  Achieves measurable behavior change outcomes
  Integrates digital and traditional marketing effectively
  Trains practitioners in social marketing


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes social marketing practice in environmental sector
*Application*: Leads transformative marketing initiatives at scale
*Indicators*:
  Directs organizational marketing strategy
  Develops innovative social marketing approaches
  Achieves recognition for marketing excellence
  Publishes on social marketing practices
  Demonstrates population-level behavior change through marketing
  Influences social marketing standards and ethics


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Conservation behavior campaigns, recreation behavior management
Private Sector: Sustainable consumption, corporate behavior change programs
NGOs: Issue campaigns, fundraising, advocacy
Consulting: Social marketing strategy and implementation services
Academia: Social marketing research, extension campaigns

5.6.5 Participatory methods and community-based research
Conducting research and planning processes in partnership with communities, ensuring community members shape questions, methods, and use of findings.


#Job Tasks:
1. Co-design research questions and methods with community partners
2. Build community capacity to collect and analyze data
3. Ensure research findings benefit and remain accountable to community


#Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:


FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
*Knowledge*: Understands participatory research principles; recognizes extractive research
*Application*: Supports community-engaged research
*Indicators*:
  Engages respectfully with community research partners
  Understands community priorities and concerns
  Contributes to participatory processes
  Recognizes power dynamics in research relationships
  Values community knowledge alongside academic knowledge


INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
*Knowledge*: Applies participatory research methods and frameworks
*Application*: Conducts participatory research projects
*Indicators*:
  Co-designs research with community partners
  Uses participatory methods (photovoice, participatory mapping, etc.)
  Builds community research capacity
  Shares decision-making power with community
  Ensures community benefit from research
  Navigates tensions between academic and community priorities


ADVANCED (6-10 years)
*Knowledge*: Expert in community-based participatory research (CBPR) and related approaches
*Application*: Leads major participatory research initiatives
*Indicators*:
  Establishes equitable community-academic partnerships
  Addresses systemic inequities through research partnerships
  Develops community research infrastructure
  Publishes collaborative research with community co-authors
  Uses research to advance community priorities
  Trains researchers in participatory approaches
  Achieves recognized success in community-engaged scholarship


EXPERT (10+ years)
*Knowledge*: Shapes participatory research practices in environmental field
*Application*: Leads transformative research partnerships at institutional level
*Indicators*:
  Directs community-engaged research programs
  Influences research institutions toward equitable partnerships
  Develops innovative participatory research frameworks
  Achieves recognition for community-engaged scholarship
  Publishes extensively on participatory research practice
  Demonstrates significant community outcomes from research partnerships
  Mentors participatory research practitioners


#Sector-Specific Applications:


Public Agencies: Co-management with tribes and communities, community science
Private Sector: Stakeholder-engaged impact assessment
NGOs: Community-led research, documentation of local knowledge
Consulting: Participatory planning and assessment services
Academia: Community-based participatory research, engaged scholarship

DOMAIN-LEVEL COMPETENCY FRAMEWORK


Cross-Cutting Competencies (Applied across all skill areas in Domain 5)


Cultural Humility
Awareness of one's own cultural lens and biases
Openness to learning from diverse perspectives
Recognition that cultural learning is lifelong


Emotional Intelligence
Self-awareness of emotions and triggers
Empathy and perspective-taking
Relationship management in diverse contexts


Systems Thinking
Understanding how communication fits within larger social-ecological systems
Recognizing interconnections and unintended consequences
Addressing root causes not just symptoms


Ethical Practice
Commitment to honesty, transparency, and accountability
Protection of vulnerable communities and sensitive information
Recognition of power and responsibility in communication roles

________________
DOMAIN 6: POLICY & REGULATORY SKILLS
Understanding and working with the rules and frameworks that govern environmental management and stewardship
________________


DOMAIN OVERVIEW
Policy and regulatory skills form the essential framework within which all environmental management operates. This domain encompasses the legal, economic, and institutional knowledge needed to navigate complex regulatory systems, develop effective policies, work across governmental boundaries, and leverage market-based mechanisms for conservation. Professionals with strong capabilities in this domain translate legal requirements into actionable management strategies, shape policy that advances environmental goals, and ensure that management decisions comply with applicable laws while serving broader societal objectives.
The policy and regulatory landscape is increasingly complex and interconnected. Environmental professionals must understand not only environmental law and land use planning, but also resource economics, environmental markets, climate finance, and environmental justice considerations. This domain has expanded significantly in recent decades to include sophisticated market mechanisms, climate adaptation finance, and explicit attention to equity and environmental health. Professionals who excel in this domain bridge the worlds of law, economics, science, and community engagement—translating between technical environmental knowledge and policy frameworks.
This domain encompasses eight skill areas spanning traditional regulatory compliance (environmental law, land use planning, intergovernmental coordination) and emerging frontiers (environmental markets, climate finance, environmental justice). While some professionals develop deep expertise in one area—such as specializing in carbon markets or environmental health assessment—the most effective policy practitioners integrate multiple skill areas and collaborate across disciplines to address complex challenges that don't respect jurisdictional or topical boundaries.
Example Positions Within This Domain
Professionals whose work falls primarily within Domain 6 include (but are not limited to):
Environmental Law & Regulatory Roles:
* Environmental Attorney / Legal Counsel
* Regulatory Compliance Manager
* Environmental Permitting Specialist
* NEPA Coordinator / Compliance Specialist
* Environmental Enforcement Officer
* Environmental Policy Analyst
* Regulatory Affairs Manager
* Environmental Compliance Auditor
Land Use Planning Roles:
* Land Use Planner / Environmental Planner
* Regional Planner
* Urban Planner (Environmental Focus)
* Public Land Planning Specialist
* Zoning Administrator
* Community Development Director
* Comprehensive Planning Manager
* Scenario Planning Specialist
Economics & Finance Roles:
* Natural Resource Economist
* Environmental Economist
* Ecosystem Services Specialist
* Conservation Finance Manager
* Carbon Markets Analyst
* Environmental Markets Specialist
* Green Finance Analyst
* Natural Capital Accountant
* Payment for Ecosystem Services (PES) Coordinator
Policy Development Roles:
* Policy Advisor / Policy Specialist
* Legislative Analyst
* Policy Research Analyst
* Government Relations Specialist
* Policy Development Manager
* Regulatory Affairs Specialist
* Public Policy Director
Intergovernmental & Tribal Relations Roles:
* Tribal Liaison / Consultation Coordinator
* Intergovernmental Affairs Manager
* Federal-State Coordinator
* Regional Coordinator
* International Conservation Specialist
* Transboundary Conservation Coordinator
Environmental Markets Roles:
* Carbon Credit Developer
* Biodiversity Credit Specialist
* Environmental Trading Specialist
* Carbon Accountant
* Offset Project Manager
* Conservation Finance Specialist
* Green Bond Analyst
* Voluntary Carbon Market Specialist
Environmental Health & Justice Roles:
* Environmental Health Scientist
* Environmental Justice Coordinator
* Community Health Specialist
* Environmental Health Risk Assessor
* Air Quality Specialist
* Toxicologist
* Environmental Epidemiologist
* Community-Based Participatory Research Coordinator
* Environmental Justice Policy Analyst
Climate Finance Roles:
* Climate Finance Specialist
* Green Bond Manager
* Climate Investment Analyst
* Adaptation Finance Coordinator
* Climate Risk Analyst
* Sustainable Finance Specialist
* Climate Policy & Finance Advisor
How Advanced Technologies Are Changing This Domain
Policy and regulatory work is being transformed by digital tools, data analytics, and new approaches to transparency and stakeholder engagement:
Policy Informatics & Big Data:
* Natural language processing analyzes thousands of public comments in hours rather than months
* Machine learning identifies patterns in regulatory compliance and enforcement data
* Big data integration reveals cumulative impacts across multiple regulatory programs
* Text mining extracts key themes from legislative and regulatory documents
* Predictive analytics forecast policy impacts and regulatory trends
* Automated policy tracking systems monitor changes across jurisdictions
* Data visualization makes complex regulatory information accessible to non-experts
Geospatial Analysis & Mapping:
* GIS enables sophisticated land use scenario modeling and planning
* Spatial analysis identifies environmental justice communities and cumulative impact zones
* Web-based mapping platforms facilitate public participation in planning processes
* Real-time data overlays show regulatory boundaries, sensitive areas, and land use
* 3D visualization helps stakeholders understand proposed developments and plans
* Mobile GIS enables field-based regulatory compliance inspections
* Geospatial tools map ecosystem services and economic values across landscapes
Environmental Markets & Blockchain:
* Blockchain technology creates transparent, verifiable carbon credit registries
* Smart contracts automate environmental market transactions
* Remote sensing and AI verify carbon sequestration and biodiversity outcomes
* IoT sensors provide real-time monitoring data for offset projects
* Digital MRV (monitoring, reporting, verification) reduces transaction costs
* Tokenization enables fractional ownership of conservation assets
* Distributed ledger technology tracks environmental commodities through supply chains
Screening Tools & Decision Support:
* Environmental justice screening tools (EJSCREEN, CalEnviroScreen) identify overburdened communities
* Climate vulnerability assessment tools support adaptation planning
* Health impact assessment software predicts policy outcomes
* Multi-criteria decision analysis helps evaluate complex policy tradeoffs
* Scenario planning software models alternative regulatory futures
* Cumulative impact assessment tools aggregate exposures across sources
* Risk assessment models evaluate chemical exposures and health effects
Public Engagement Platforms:
* Online comment platforms enable broader public participation
* Social media monitoring tracks public sentiment on policy issues
* Virtual public meetings increase accessibility for remote participants
* Collaborative online platforms facilitate multi-stakeholder planning
* Crowdsourcing tools gather local knowledge for planning
* Digital surveys reach broader audiences than traditional methods
* Translation software enables multilingual engagement
Climate & Economic Modeling:
* Integrated assessment models link climate, economy, and policy
* Carbon accounting software tracks greenhouse gas inventories
* Economic impact models assess policy costs and benefits
* Climate scenario tools evaluate adaptation finance needs
* Natural capital accounting platforms value ecosystem services
* Life cycle assessment software evaluates environmental footprints
* Cost-benefit analysis tools evaluate regulatory alternatives
Compliance & Enforcement Technology:
* Drones monitor compliance with environmental permits
* Satellite imagery detects unpermitted activities
* Automated reporting systems streamline regulatory compliance
* Mobile apps enable real-time field inspections and documentation
* Digital permitting systems reduce processing time and increase transparency
* Compliance tracking software manages complex regulatory requirements
* AI flags potential violations for enforcement follow-up
Challenges & Considerations:
These technological advances also create new challenges:
* Digital divide: Not all communities have equal access to technology-based engagement
* Data quality concerns: Automated tools depend on accurate, comprehensive data
* Transparency vs. complexity: Advanced models may be "black boxes" to stakeholders
* Privacy issues: Geospatial and health data raise confidentiality concerns
* Algorithm bias: AI tools may perpetuate existing inequities if not carefully designed
* Cybersecurity: Digital systems for environmental markets need robust security
* Capacity gaps: Smaller organizations may lack resources for advanced tools
* Over-reliance on technology: Digital tools supplement but don't replace human judgment and relationships
Future-Ready Skills:
Policy and regulatory professionals increasingly need:
* Data analysis and visualization skills
* Understanding of environmental markets and finance mechanisms
* Ability to use screening and decision-support tools
* Digital engagement and facilitation capabilities
* Knowledge of blockchain and emerging financial technologies
* Statistical literacy to evaluate policy evidence
* Understanding of AI capabilities and limitations
* Cross-cultural competency for diverse stakeholder engagement
* Systems thinking to address complex, interconnected policy challenges
* Communication skills to translate technical policy to diverse audiences
The most effective policy professionals blend technological competency with deep understanding of legal frameworks, economic principles, and community relationships—using technology to enhance but not replace human-centered policy development and implementation.
Cross-Sector Applications
Policy and regulatory skills are essential across all environmental sectors:
* Public Agencies: Regulatory development and enforcement, land use planning, environmental compliance, intergovernmental coordination, environmental justice programs
* Private Sector: Regulatory compliance, permit management, environmental markets participation, sustainability reporting, government relations, risk management
* NGOs: Policy advocacy, conservation finance, community engagement, litigation support, voluntary market programs, environmental justice organizing
* Consulting: Permitting services, policy analysis, economic analysis, environmental market development, regulatory strategy, compliance auditing
* Academia: Policy research, economic valuation studies, regulatory effectiveness evaluation, teaching future policy professionals
* Tribal Nations: Government-to-government consultation, co-management agreements, cultural resource protection, tribal environmental regulation, treaty rights
Career Progression in This Domain
Professionals typically develop policy and regulatory expertise along three dimensions:
1. Breadth of Knowledge: Single jurisdiction/regulation → multiple jurisdictions → comprehensive policy frameworks → systems-level integration
2. Influence Level: Technical implementation → policy development → strategic influence → policy leadership
3. Complexity: Routine compliance → complex negotiations → multi-stakeholder facilitation → transformative policy change
________________


DOMAIN-LEVEL CAREER PATHWAYS
Pathway 1: Environmental Attorney / Policy Counsel
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
* Entry (0-3 years): Legal Assistant, Junior Attorney, Policy Analyst
   * Focus: 6.1 (Environmental Law), 6.4 (Policy Development)
   * Typical setting: Law firms, government agencies, legal NGOs
* Mid (3-7 years): Environmental Attorney, Policy Advisor, Regulatory Counsel
   * Focus: 6.1, 6.4, 6.5 with expertise in specific regulatory areas
   * Typical setting: Legal practice, agency legal counsel, corporate environmental departments
* Senior (7-15 years): Senior Counsel, Policy Director, Chief Legal Officer
   * Focus: Strategic legal and policy leadership across multiple areas
   * Typical setting: Lead roles in agencies, corporations, large NGOs
* Expert (15+ years): General Counsel, Policy Vice President, Agency Director
   * Focus: Organizational legal/policy strategy, regulatory influence
   * Typical setting: Executive leadership, policy thought leadership
Pathway 2: Environmental Economist / Markets Specialist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
* Entry (0-3 years): Junior Economist, Research Analyst, Market Analyst
   * Focus: 6.3 (Resource Economics), 6.6 (Environmental Markets)
   * Typical setting: Consulting firms, research institutions, government agencies
* Mid (3-7 years): Environmental Economist, Carbon Markets Specialist, Conservation Finance Manager
   * Focus: 6.3, 6.6, 6.8 with economic analysis and market development
   * Typical setting: Consulting, environmental market organizations, finance sector
* Senior (7-15 years): Senior Economist, Markets Program Director, Climate Finance Lead
   * Focus: Program leadership, market design, climate finance strategy
   * Typical setting: Lead roles in market institutions, finance, international organizations
* Expert (15+ years): Chief Economist, Markets Policy Director, Strategic Finance Advisor
   * Focus: Market policy development, strategic climate finance, economic thought leadership
   * Typical setting: International organizations, policy leadership, senior advisory roles
Pathway 3: Land Use Planner / Regional Coordinator
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
* Entry (0-3 years): Assistant Planner, Planning Technician, Junior Coordinator
   * Focus: 6.2 (Land Use Planning), basic GIS and community engagement
   * Typical setting: Local/regional planning agencies, planning consultants
* Mid (3-7 years): Planner, Regional Coordinator, Community Development Specialist
   * Focus: 6.2, 6.5, comprehensive planning and intergovernmental coordination
   * Typical setting: City/county planning, regional agencies, tribal planning departments
* Senior (7-15 years): Senior Planner, Planning Director, Regional Planning Manager
   * Focus: Large-scale planning initiatives, policy integration, leadership
   * Typical setting: Planning department leadership, regional planning organizations
* Expert (15+ years): Planning Director, Regional Administrator, Policy Advisor
   * Focus: Strategic planning policy, land use transformation, regional vision
   * Typical setting: Executive planning leadership, policy advisory roles
Pathway 4: Environmental Justice / Community Health Specialist
Entry → Mid → Senior → Expert
* Entry (0-3 years): Community Health Worker, EJ Research Assistant, Outreach Coordinator
   * Focus: 6.7 (Environmental Health & Justice), community engagement
   * Typical setting: Community organizations, health departments, academic institutions
* Mid (3-7 years): Environmental Justice Coordinator, Community Health Specialist, EJ Policy Analyst
   * Focus: 6.7, 6.4, community-based participatory research, policy advocacy
   * Typical setting: Government EJ offices, NGOs, community health organizations
* Senior (7-15 years): EJ Program Director, Environmental Health Manager, Senior Policy Advisor
   * Focus: Program leadership, policy development, multi-sector collaboration
   * Typical setting: Lead EJ programs in agencies, major NGOs, health departments
* Expert (15+ years): Chief EJ Officer, Environmental Health Director, National EJ Advocate
   * Focus: Strategic EJ leadership, policy transformation, systemic change
   * Typical setting: Executive leadership, national policy influence, major advocacy organizations

REPRESENTATIVE JOB ROLE PROFILES
Profile 1: Environmental Compliance Manager (Mid-Career)
Typical Employers: Manufacturing companies, utilities, resource extraction companies, large landowners, municipal governments
Primary Skill Areas: 6.1 (Environmental Law & Regulation), 6.4 (Policy Development), 6.5 (Intergovernmental Relations)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 6.1.1-6.1.5: Advanced level
* 6.4.4: Intermediate to Advanced
* 6.5.2: Intermediate
Typical Responsibilities:
* Ensure organizational compliance with federal, state, and local environmental regulations
* Manage permit applications, renewals, and reporting requirements
* Conduct internal compliance audits and develop corrective action plans
* Coordinate with regulatory agencies during inspections and enforcement actions
* Train staff on environmental compliance procedures and requirements
* Develop and update environmental management systems
* Track regulatory changes and assess implications for operations
* Manage relationships with environmental consultants and legal counsel
Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's in Environmental Science, Engineering, or related field; Master's often preferred
* Certified Hazardous Materials Manager (CHMM)
* ISO 14001 Lead Auditor certification
* Professional Engineer (PE) or Certified Professional in Environmental Compliance (CPEC)
Salary Range: $70,000-$105,000 (varies by sector, company size, and region)
________________


Profile 2: Carbon Markets Specialist (Mid-Senior)
Typical Employers: Carbon offset developers, environmental markets firms, consulting companies, corporations with climate commitments, NGOs
Primary Skill Areas: 6.6 (Environmental Markets), 6.3 (Resource Economics), 6.8 (Climate Finance)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 6.6.1-6.6.5: Advanced level
* 6.3.5: Advanced
* 6.8.1-6.8.3: Intermediate to Advanced
Typical Responsibilities:
* Develop carbon offset projects from concept through verification
* Conduct carbon accounting and quantification for projects
* Navigate voluntary and compliance carbon market standards (VCS, CAR, Gold Standard)
* Manage relationships with verifiers, registries, and project stakeholders
* Assess market opportunities and pricing for carbon credits
* Ensure additionality, permanence, and co-benefits of offset projects
* Prepare project design documents and monitoring plans
* Negotiate carbon credit purchase agreements
* Stay current on evolving carbon market regulations and standards
Related Credentials:
* Master's in Environmental Science, Economics, Finance, or related field preferred
* Greenhouse Gas Management Institute (GHGMI) certification
* CFA or similar finance credential helpful
* Specialized training in carbon accounting standards
Salary Range: $80,000-$130,000
________________


Profile 3: Environmental Justice Coordinator (Mid-Career)
Typical Employers: Government agencies (EPA, state environmental departments), NGOs, community-based organizations, health departments
Primary Skill Areas: 6.7 (Environmental Health & Justice), 6.4 (Policy Development), 6.5 (Intergovernmental Relations)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 6.7.1-6.7.5: Advanced level
* 6.4.1, 6.4.4: Intermediate to Advanced
* Strong community engagement skills
Typical Responsibilities:
* Identify and assess environmental justice communities using screening tools
* Facilitate community-based participatory research projects
* Develop and implement agency environmental justice policies and programs
* Review permits and projects for environmental justice impacts
* Build relationships with community organizations and leaders
* Conduct cumulative impact assessments in overburdened communities
* Coordinate interagency environmental justice initiatives
* Advocate for policy changes to address environmental health disparities
* Provide technical assistance to communities on environmental health issues
Related Credentials:
* Bachelor's or Master's in Public Health, Environmental Science, Urban Planning, or related field
* Community organizing or CBPR training
* Certified Health Education Specialist (CHES) helpful
* Language skills often valuable for community engagement
Salary Range: $60,000-$95,000
________________


Profile 4: Natural Resource Economist (Senior)
Typical Employers: Government agencies (USDA, DOI, NOAA), consulting firms, universities, international organizations, NGOs
Primary Skill Areas: 6.3 (Resource Economics), 6.6 (Environmental Markets), 6.8 (Climate Finance)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 6.3.1-6.3.5: Advanced to Expert level
* 6.6.2-6.6.3: Advanced
* Strong quantitative and modeling skills
Typical Responsibilities:
* Conduct economic valuation of natural resources and ecosystem services
* Perform cost-benefit analyses for environmental policies and projects
* Develop and apply non-market valuation methods (contingent valuation, hedonic pricing, travel cost)
* Model economic impacts of environmental regulations and conservation programs
* Design market-based conservation mechanisms and payment schemes
* Evaluate climate change economic impacts and adaptation costs
* Publish economic research in peer-reviewed journals
* Provide expert testimony on economic aspects of environmental decisions
* Advise policymakers on economic dimensions of environmental policy
Related Credentials:
* Ph.D. in Economics, Agricultural Economics, or Environmental Economics typically required
* 10+ years experience
* Publication record in peer-reviewed journals
* Professional societies (Association of Environmental and Resource Economists)
Salary Range: $90,000-$150,000+
________________


Profile 5: Land Use Planning Director (Senior-Expert)
Typical Employers: County/city governments, regional planning organizations, tribal governments, large NGOs, planning consultancies
Primary Skill Areas: 6.2 (Land Use Planning), 6.5 (Intergovernmental Relations), 6.4 (Policy Development)
Proficiency Requirements:
* 6.2.1-6.2.5: Advanced to Expert level
* 6.5.2, 6.5.4: Advanced
* 6.4.1-6.4.2: Advanced
* Strong leadership and public engagement skills
Typical Responsibilities:
* Lead comprehensive planning efforts for cities, counties, or regions
* Oversee development and implementation of general plans, zoning codes, and growth management strategies
* Manage interdisciplinary planning teams and consultants
* Facilitate public participation in planning processes
* Integrate climate adaptation, environmental justice, and sustainability into plans
* Coordinate planning across jurisdictions and with multiple agencies
* Present plans to elected officials and planning commissions
* Oversee development review and permitting processes
* Manage planning department budgets and operations
* Build partnerships with diverse stakeholders for plan implementation
Related Credentials:
* Master's in Urban/Regional Planning, Geography, or related field
* AICP (American Institute of Certified Planners) certification
* 10-15+ years progressive planning experience
* Leadership and management training
Salary Range: $95,000-$145,000 (higher in large metropolitan areas)
SKILL AREAS (6.1 6.4)
________________


6.1 ENVIRONMENTAL LAW & REGULATION
Working with the legal frameworks that protect natural resources
Skills related to understanding and applying environmental laws, regulations, and compliance requirements that govern environmental management activities.
________________


6.1.1 Environmental policy analysis
Examining existing environmental policies to understand their requirements, impacts, and effectiveness for guiding environmental management.
Job Tasks:
1. Review existing policies to assess alignment with conservation objectives
2. Compare federal, state, and local environmental laws affecting a project area
3. Prepare memos or policy briefs summarizing legal considerations
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands major environmental laws and their basic purposes (Clean Water Act, Clean Air Act, ESA, NEPA)
* Application: Reads and summarizes policy documents with guidance
* Indicators:
   * Identifies which environmental laws apply to specific activities
   * Explains basic requirements of major environmental statutes
   * Locates relevant regulations and guidance documents
   * Summarizes policy provisions in plain language
   * Recognizes when activities may trigger regulatory requirements
   * Understands hierarchy of federal, state, and local regulations
   * Tracks policy changes through federal register and agency websites
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies legal and policy analysis frameworks; understands regulatory interpretation
* Application: Conducts independent policy analyses and identifies implementation challenges
* Indicators:
   * Analyzes gaps and overlaps between multiple regulatory requirements
   * Evaluates effectiveness of policies in achieving environmental outcomes
   * Interprets regulatory language and agency guidance
   * Identifies unintended consequences or implementation barriers
   * Compares policy approaches across jurisdictions
   * Synthesizes legal requirements into actionable guidance
   * Prepares comprehensive policy briefs for decision-makers
   * Tracks legislative and regulatory developments proactively
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in environmental law; understands judicial interpretation and regulatory history
* Application: Provides authoritative policy analysis and strategic guidance
* Indicators:
   * Evaluates complex interactions between multiple regulatory frameworks
   * Analyzes case law and its implications for policy implementation
   * Predicts likely regulatory interpretations and enforcement approaches
   * Identifies opportunities for regulatory reform or streamlining
   * Conducts comparative policy analysis across states or countries
   * Provides expert consultation on policy interpretation
   * Publishes policy analysis in professional venues
   * Trains staff on complex policy requirements
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes understanding of environmental policy and its evolution
* Application: Influences policy development and regulatory reform
* Indicators:
   * Provides expert testimony on policy matters
   * Influences policy reform through analysis and advocacy
   * Publishes influential policy analysis shaping debates
   * Advises elected officials and agency leadership on policy strategy
   * Recognized as thought leader in environmental policy
   * Contributes to development of model policies or legislation
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Policy guidance development, regulatory interpretation, legislative analysis
* Private Sector: Regulatory compliance strategy, policy risk assessment, advocacy positions
* NGOs: Policy advocacy, litigation support, reform recommendations
* Consulting: Policy analysis services, regulatory advisory, strategic counsel
* Academia: Policy research, regulatory effectiveness evaluation, teaching
________________

   * Interprets regulatory requirements in context
   * Develops corrective action plans
   * Coordinates with regulatory agencies professionally
   * Trains staff on compliance procedures

ADVANCED (6-10 years)

   * Provides compliance training and capacity building
   * Anticipates regulatory changes and adjusts compliance programs

6.1.4 NEPA process implementation
Following the National Environmental Policy Act requirements for assessing environmental impacts of proposed actions on federal lands or using federal funds.
Job Tasks:
1. Develop or review environmental assessments (EAs) or environmental impact statements (EISs)
2. Lead public scoping and comment periods in accordance with NEPA regulations
3. Coordinate interdisciplinary teams and documentation for NEPA compliance
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands NEPA process and documentation requirements
* Application: Supports NEPA analyses with guidance
* Indicators:
   * Explains NEPA process and purpose
   * Distinguishes categorical exclusions, EAs, and EISs
   * Assists with data collection for NEPA analyses
   * Compiles information for NEPA documents
   * Tracks NEPA process timelines and requirements
   * Understands public participation requirements
   * Maintains NEPA project files
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies NEPA requirements to project planning
* Application: Prepares NEPA documents and manages public processes
* Indicators:
   * Determines appropriate level of NEPA analysis
   * Writes sections of EAs or EISs
   * Coordinates interdisciplinary NEPA teams
   * Manages public scoping and comment processes
   * Analyzes and responds to public comments
   * Ensures NEPA compliance with Council on Environmental Quality (CEQ) regulations
   * Integrates NEPA with other regulatory requirements
   * Manages NEPA consultants
   * Develops alternatives analysis for NEPA documents
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in NEPA law, policy, and best practices
* Application: Leads complex NEPA analyses and organizational NEPA programs
* Indicators:
   * Leads preparation of EISs for controversial or complex projects
   * Develops agency NEPA procedures and guidance
   * Provides NEPA training and quality control
   * Manages programmatic NEPA analyses
   * Defends NEPA documents in administrative appeals or litigation
   * Integrates NEPA with adaptive management
   * Coordinates NEPA across multiple agencies
   * Develops innovative approaches to NEPA challenges
   * Provides expert consultation on NEPA requirements
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes NEPA policy and practice
* Application: Influences NEPA implementation nationally
* Indicators:
   * Contributes to CEQ guidance or regulations
   * Provides expert testimony on NEPA matters
   * Publishes influential NEPA analysis or guidance
   * Leads NEPA program for agency or organization
   * Recognized national expert in NEPA
   * Advises on NEPA reform or modernization
   * Trains NEPA professionals nationally
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: NEPA compliance, document preparation, process management
* Private Sector: NEPA coordination for projects requiring federal approvals
* NGOs: NEPA review and comments, litigation, advocacy
* Consulting: NEPA document preparation, technical analysis, public facilitation
* Academia: NEPA research, teaching, effectiveness evaluation
________________

   * Publishes on enforcement best practices
   * Recognized expert in environmental enforcement
   * Advises on enforcement law reform

6.2 LAND USE PLANNING
Creating comprehensive frameworks for how land should be managed
Skills related to developing formal plans that guide decisions about how lands will be used, managed, and conserved over time.
________________


6.2.1 Comprehensive land use planning
Creating overarching plans that address all aspects of how an area will be managed, including recreation, conservation, resource use, and development.
Job Tasks:
1. Develop multi-use plans that balance conservation, recreation, and development
2. Engage stakeholders to define land use goals and compatible uses
3. Synthesize biological, cultural, and economic data into planning documents
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands land use planning concepts and processes
* Application: Supports planning efforts with data and analysis
* Indicators:
   * Explains comprehensive planning concepts and elements
   * Collects and organizes data for planning (land use, demographics, environmental conditions)
   * Assists with stakeholder engagement activities
   * Prepares maps and graphics for planning documents
   * Reviews existing plans to understand approach
   * Understands relationship between plans and zoning/regulations
   * Documents public input from meetings and surveys
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies planning principles and methods
* Application: Develops sections of comprehensive plans independently
* Indicators:
   * Conducts existing conditions analysis
   * Facilitates stakeholder engagement processes
   * Develops land use alternatives for evaluation
   * Writes sections of comprehensive plans
   * Uses GIS for spatial planning and analysis
   * Integrates multiple objectives (environment, economy, equity)
   * Evaluates consistency between plans and implementation tools
   * Presents planning concepts to public and decision-makers
   * Coordinates with subject matter experts across disciplines
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in comprehensive planning across contexts
* Application: Leads comprehensive planning efforts
* Indicators:
   * Designs and manages comprehensive planning processes
   * Leads interdisciplinary planning teams
   * Facilitates complex multi-stakeholder negotiations
   * Integrates climate adaptation and resilience into plans
   * Develops innovative planning approaches for novel challenges
   * Ensures plan implementation through regulations and programs
   * Evaluates and updates existing plans
   * Provides planning training and capacity building
   * Publishes planning case studies
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes planning practice and policy
* Application: Influences planning at regional or national level
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative planning frameworks adopted widely
   * Influences planning policy and standards
   * Leads major regional or landscape-scale planning initiatives
   * Provides expert consultation on complex planning challenges
   * Publishes influential planning research
   * Recognized thought leader in comprehensive planning
   * Advises on planning reform and modernization
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: General plans, resource management plans, public land planning
* Private Sector: Master planning for large properties, corporate land use strategy
* NGOs: Conservation planning, community vision development
* Consulting: Comprehensive plan preparation, technical analysis, facilitation
* Academia: Planning research, teaching, plan evaluation
________________


6.2.2 Zoning and land classification
Designating different areas for different uses or management approaches based on their characteristics and values.
Job Tasks:
1. Apply zoning designations to reflect ecological sensitivity or legal mandates
2. Update land classifications based on new assessments or use trends
3. Maintain geospatial data showing zoning boundaries and constraints
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands zoning concepts and classification systems
* Application: Applies zoning codes with guidance
* Indicators:
   * Explains purpose and types of zoning
   * Interprets zoning maps and ordinances
   * Determines which zoning applies to specific parcels
   * Reviews development proposals for zoning compliance
   * Updates zoning GIS data
   * Understands nonconforming uses and variances
   * Researches zoning history for parcels
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies zoning principles and develops classifications
* Application: Develops zoning recommendations and manages implementation
* Indicators:
   * Develops zoning ordinances and map amendments
   * Analyzes zoning for consistency with comprehensive plans
   * Evaluates requests for rezoning or variances
   * Designs overlay zones for special purposes (environmental, historic)
   * Uses GIS for zoning analysis and mapping
   * Presents zoning recommendations to planning commissions
   * Manages zoning code updates
   * Mediates zoning conflicts between stakeholders
   * Ensures zoning supports plan implementation
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in zoning law and innovative zoning approaches
* Application: Leads comprehensive zoning program reform
* Indicators:
   * Leads zoning code rewrite or major updates
   * Develops form-based codes or innovative zoning approaches
   * Designs zoning to achieve climate, equity, and sustainability goals
   * Defends zoning decisions in appeals or litigation
   * Evaluates zoning effectiveness and barriers
   * Integrates environmental protection with development potential
   * Provides training on zoning administration
   * Develops model zoning provisions
   * Publishes on zoning best practices
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes zoning policy and innovation
* Application: Influences zoning practice regionally or nationally
* Indicators:
   * Develops influential zoning frameworks or model codes
   * Provides expert testimony on zoning matters
   * Influences zoning law and policy reform
   * Recognized expert in specialized zoning (environmental, climate, etc.)
   * Publishes influential zoning research
   * Advises communities on zoning transformation
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Zoning administration, code development, public land classification
* Private Sector: Zoning due diligence, rezoning advocacy, development strategy
* NGOs: Zoning advocacy, conservation zoning, watchdog activities
* Consulting: Zoning code development, compliance analysis, appeal support
* Academia: Zoning research, law, planning education
________________


6.2.3 Public land management planning
Developing formal management plans for publicly owned lands through established planning processes.

3. Incorporate climate, wildfire, and equity considerations into plan revisions

* Indicators:
   * Explains agency planning frameworks (e.g., Forest Service planning rule)
   * Compiles existing data for planning
   * Assists with public scoping and engagement
   * Documents interdisciplinary team discussions
   * Prepares maps and appendices for plans
   * Understands relationship between plans and NEPA
   * Tracks planning milestones and deliverables
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies public land planning frameworks
* Application: Develops plan components and coordinates processes
* Indicators:
   * Writes chapters of management plans
   * Coordinates interdisciplinary planning teams
   * Manages public engagement for planning efforts
   * Integrates best available science into plans
   * Develops management direction and standards
   * Ensures consistency with higher-level plans and policies
   * Manages planning consultants
   * Responds to public comments on draft plans
   * Coordinates NEPA compliance for plans
ADVANCED (6-10 years)

* Application: Leads major public land planning efforts
* Indicators:
   * Leads development of comprehensive public land plans

   * Addresses equity and access in public land plans
   * Facilitates resolution of conflicting resource uses
   * Ensures plan implementation and monitoring
   * Provides planning training within agencies
   * Develops innovative planning approaches
   * Evaluates plan effectiveness and adaptive management
EXPERT (10+ years)

* Application: Influences planning at agency or national level
* Indicators:
   * Develops or revises planning regulations and guidance
   * Leads agency planning programs
   * Provides expert consultation on complex planning challenges
   * Influences planning policy reform
   * Publishes on public land planning
   * Recognized expert in public land planning
   * Leads planning innovation across agencies
Sector-Specific Applications:

* Academia: Planning research, teaching, effectiveness evaluation

   * Publishes on collaborative planning

Creating and evaluating different possible future scenarios to inform land management planning decisions.
Job Tasks:
1. Model future land use scenarios under varying ecological, social, or economic conditions

   * Documents scenario planning workshops
   * Understands difference between scenarios and predictions
   * Compiles information on scenario assumptions
   * Prepares scenario comparison tables

   * Develops plausible future scenarios with stakeholders
   * Uses GIS to model spatial scenarios

   * Facilitates scenario planning workshops
   * Uses scenario planning software and tools
   * Evaluates trade-offs between scenarios
   * Communicates scenario findings to diverse audiences
   * Integrates scenario results into planning decisions
   * Documents assumptions and uncertainties
ADVANCED (6-10 years)

* Indicators:

   * Facilitates multi-stakeholder scenario development
   * Uses scenarios to test policy robustness
   * Develops decision-support tools based on scenarios
   * Evaluates scenario planning effectiveness
   * Publishes scenario planning case studies
   * Trains practitioners in scenario methods
EXPERT (10+ years)

* Application: Influences scenario planning at broad scale

   * Develops innovative scenario planning approaches
   * Influences use of scenarios in policy and planning
   * Leads major regional scenario planning initiatives
   * Publishes influential scenario planning research
   * Recognized expert in scenario planning

Sector-Specific Applications:
