6.3 RESOURCE ECONOMICS
Understanding the financial and economic aspects of natural resources
Skills related to the economic dimensions of natural resources and ecosystem services, including how to value, incentivize, and analyze economic aspects of conservation and environmental management.
________________


6.3.1 Natural resource valuation
Determining the economic value of natural resources, including both market values (like timber) and non-market values (like scenic beauty or clean air).
Job Tasks:
1. Estimate the economic value of timber, water, recreation, or other resources using market and non-market methods
2. Conduct willingness-to-pay studies or contingent valuation surveys
3. Present valuation results to inform budget planning or policy proposals
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands economic valuation concepts and why resources have value beyond markets
* Application: Assists with data collection for valuation studies
* Indicators:
   * Explains difference between market and non-market values
   * Describes valuation methods (travel cost, hedonic pricing, contingent valuation)
   * Collects data for valuation studies (visitor surveys, market prices)
   * Understands concept of total economic value (use and non-use values)
   * Compiles economic data from secondary sources
   * Assists with survey administration
   * Recognizes importance of valuation for decision-making
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies economic valuation methods appropriately
* Application: Conducts valuation studies independently
* Indicators:
   * Conducts stated preference studies (contingent valuation, choice experiments)
   * Applies revealed preference methods (travel cost, hedonic pricing)
   * Uses benefit transfer to estimate values for unstudied sites
   * Designs and implements valuation surveys
   * Analyzes valuation data using statistical software
   * Interprets valuation results and limitations
   * Presents valuation findings to non-economists
   * Applies valuation to cost-benefit analysis
   * Addresses equity considerations in valuation
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in valuation theory and advanced methods
* Application: Leads complex valuation projects and develops methodology
* Indicators:
   * Conducts sophisticated valuation studies using advanced methods
   * Develops site-specific valuation functions
   * Addresses methodological challenges (strategic bias, embedding effects)
   * Evaluates quality and transferability of existing valuation studies
   * Integrates multiple valuation methods for robustness
   * Publishes valuation research in peer-reviewed journals
   * Provides expert testimony on economic values
   * Trains practitioners in valuation methods
   * Develops valuation guidance and protocols
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes valuation science and policy applications
* Application: Influences how valuation informs policy and decisions
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative valuation approaches
   * Influences valuation policy and guidance (e.g., EPA, OMB)
   * Publishes influential valuation research
   * Provides expert consultation on major valuation questions
   * Recognized authority in natural resource valuation
   * Advances valuation methodology
   * Leads major valuation research programs
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Cost-benefit analysis, damage assessment, resource pricing
* Private Sector: Natural capital accounting, resource asset valuation
* NGOs: Conservation prioritization, advocacy based on values
* Consulting: Valuation studies, economic analysis, litigation support
* Academia: Valuation research, methodology development, teaching
________________


6.3.2 Ecosystem services assessment
Identifying and measuring the benefits that ecosystems provide to people, such as clean water, flood control, pollination, or recreation opportunities.
Job Tasks:
1. Quantify services such as flood mitigation, carbon storage, or pollination
2. Use tools like InVEST or ARIES to model ecosystem service flows
3. Communicate ecosystem service benefits in planning or outreach materials
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands ecosystem services framework and major service categories
* Application: Supports ecosystem service assessments with data collection
* Indicators:
   * Explains concept of ecosystem services
   * Identifies provisioning, regulating, cultural, and supporting services
   * Recognizes ecosystem services provided by different ecosystems
   * Collects data for ecosystem service assessments
   * Uses basic ecosystem service assessment tools
   * Understands beneficiaries of ecosystem services
   * Maps ecosystem service supply areas
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies ecosystem services science and assessment methods
* Application: Conducts ecosystem service assessments independently
* Indicators:
   * Quantifies ecosystem services using biophysical models
   * Uses ecosystem service modeling tools (InVEST, ARIES, i-Tree)
   * Maps ecosystem service provision and beneficiaries
   * Conducts ecosystem service trade-off analysis
   * Values ecosystem services economically when appropriate
   * Integrates ecosystem services into planning and decision-making
   * Communicates ecosystem service concepts to non-specialists
   * Evaluates management impacts on ecosystem services
   * Addresses equity in ecosystem service distribution
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in ecosystem services science and applications
* Application: Leads comprehensive ecosystem service programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs sophisticated ecosystem service assessments
   * Models complex service interactions and trade-offs
   * Integrates ecosystem services with natural capital accounting
   * Develops ecosystem service indicators for monitoring
   * Evaluates effectiveness of interventions on services
   * Addresses uncertainty in ecosystem service assessments
   * Publishes ecosystem services research
   * Trains practitioners in ecosystem service assessment
   * Influences integration of ecosystem services into policy
   * Develops decision-support tools for ecosystem services
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes ecosystem services science and policy
* Application: Influences ecosystem services at national/international level
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative ecosystem service frameworks or methods
   * Influences ecosystem service policy (e.g., IPBES, national assessments)
   * Leads major ecosystem service research programs
   * Publishes influential ecosystem services research
   * Contributes to international ecosystem service standards
   * Recognized authority in ecosystem services
   * Advances mainstreaming of ecosystem services
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Ecosystem service assessments for decisions, natural capital accounting
* Private Sector: Natural capital protocols, ecosystem service dependencies and impacts
* NGOs: Ecosystem service-based conservation, communication, advocacy
* Consulting: Ecosystem service assessments, modeling services, corporate advisory
* Academia: Ecosystem services research, methodology development, teaching
________________


6.3.3 Market-based conservation mechanisms
Using economic tools and incentives to encourage conservation on private lands, such as conservation easements, habitat credits, or payment for ecosystem services.
Job Tasks:
1. Develop and implement programs like conservation easements or habitat credits
2. Structure contracts and financial incentives to encourage stewardship on private lands
3. Monitor compliance and ecological outcomes of enrolled properties
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands market-based conservation concepts and major mechanisms
* Application: Supports market-based conservation programs
* Indicators:
   * Explains conservation easements, habitat banks, PES programs
   * Assists with landowner outreach and enrollment
   * Collects baseline data for conservation agreements
   * Monitors properties for compliance
   * Maintains records for conservation programs
   * Understands additionality and conditionality concepts
   * Recognizes different conservation finance mechanisms
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies market-based conservation principles and program design
* Application: Develops and manages conservation incentive programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs conservation easement terms and conditions
   * Structures payment for ecosystem service contracts
   * Determines appropriate incentive levels for conservation actions
   * Negotiates conservation agreements with landowners
   * Develops habitat credit methodologies
   * Monitors and verifies conservation outcomes
   * Manages conservation program implementation
   * Evaluates cost-effectiveness of conservation incentives
   * Ensures additionality and permanence of conservation
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in conservation finance and market mechanisms
* Application: Leads market-based conservation programs at scale
* Indicators:
   * Designs innovative market-based conservation programs
   * Establishes conservation banks or mitigation programs
   * Structures complex conservation finance deals
   * Evaluates ecological effectiveness of market mechanisms
   * Addresses market failures and barriers to participation
   * Develops adaptive management for conservation programs
   * Publishes on market-based conservation
   * Trains practitioners in conservation finance
   * Influences policy on conservation incentives
   * Builds partnerships for conservation finance
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes conservation finance policy and practice
* Application: Influences market-based conservation at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Develops influential conservation finance frameworks
   * Influences policy on conservation incentives and markets
   * Leads major landscape-scale conservation finance initiatives
   * Publishes influential research on market-based conservation
   * Recognized expert in conservation finance
   * Advises governments on conservation market design
   * Advances innovation in conservation finance
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Conservation incentive programs (EQIP, CRP, etc.), habitat banking, wetland mitigation
* Private Sector: Conservation easements, habitat credits, corporate PES programs
* NGOs: Conservation easement acquisition, PES program development, landowner assistance
* Consulting: Conservation finance design, easement services, mitigation banking
* Academia: Conservation economics research, program evaluation
________________


6.3.4 Economic impact analysis
Evaluating how environmental management decisions or policies affect local economies, including jobs, income, tax revenues, and business activity.
Job Tasks:
1. Analyze how proposed projects or policies affect local jobs, income, and business activity
2. Prepare economic sections for environmental assessments or grant applications
3. Communicate economic findings to stakeholders and elected officials
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands economic impact concepts and data sources
* Application: Assists with economic impact data collection
* Indicators:
   * Explains direct, indirect, and induced economic effects
   * Collects economic data (employment, visitor spending, production)
   * Uses secondary economic data sources
   * Understands economic multipliers
   * Compiles information for economic impact studies
   * Recognizes stakeholders affected by economic changes
   * Assists with survey administration
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies economic impact analysis methods
* Application: Conducts economic impact studies independently
* Indicators:
   * Conducts economic impact analyses using input-output models (IMPLAN, REMI)
   * Estimates employment, income, and output effects
   * Evaluates economic impacts of alternative scenarios
   * Analyzes distributional impacts across sectors and communities
   * Presents economic findings clearly to non-economists
   * Integrates economic analysis with environmental assessment
   * Addresses limitations and uncertainty in impact estimates
   * Evaluates economic benefits and costs of policies
   * Considers equity in economic impact distribution
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in economic impact assessment and regional economics
* Application: Leads complex economic impact analyses
* Indicators:
   * Conducts sophisticated economic impact studies
   * Evaluates dynamic and long-term economic effects
   * Assesses fiscal impacts on local governments
   * Analyzes economic resilience and diversification
   * Develops custom economic models for specific contexts
   * Evaluates cumulative economic impacts of multiple actions
   * Publishes economic impact research
   * Provides expert testimony on economic matters
   * Trains practitioners in economic impact analysis
   * Influences how economic analysis informs decisions
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes economic impact assessment practice
* Application: Influences economic analysis policy and methods
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative economic assessment approaches
   * Influences guidance on economic analysis (e.g., OMB, CEQ)
   * Leads major economic research programs
   * Publishes influential economic impact research
   * Recognized authority in regional economics
   * Advises on economic analysis policy
   * Advances integration of economic and environmental analysis
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Economic analysis for NEPA, policy evaluation, program justification
* Private Sector: Project economic analysis, economic benefits communication
* NGOs: Economic arguments for conservation, community benefits analysis
* Consulting: Economic impact studies, cost-benefit analysis, expert testimony
* Academia: Regional economics research, impact assessment methodology
________________


6.3.5 Carbon credit and offset programs
Working with systems that assign economic value to carbon sequestration and allow landowners to earn credits for practices that reduce greenhouse gases.
Job Tasks:
1. Measure and verify carbon sequestration from land management practices
2. Navigate voluntary or regulatory carbon markets for credit sales
3. Prepare offset documentation and reports for third-party verifiers
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands carbon markets and offset concepts
* Application: Supports carbon project development
* Indicators:
   * Explains voluntary and compliance carbon markets
   * Describes carbon offset standards (VCS, CAR, Gold Standard)
   * Collects field data for carbon accounting
   * Assists with carbon project documentation
   * Understands additionality, permanence, and leakage
   * Monitors carbon project activities
   * Tracks carbon market prices and trends
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies carbon accounting and project development methods
* Application: Develops carbon offset projects
* Indicators:
   * Quantifies carbon sequestration using approved methodologies
   * Prepares project design documents for carbon standards
   * Ensures project additionality and permanence
   * Navigates carbon offset verification processes
   * Develops monitoring plans for carbon projects
   * Evaluates carbon project financial viability
   * Coordinates with verifiers and registries
   * Markets carbon credits to buyers
   * Addresses leakage and reversal risks
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in carbon markets and project development
* Application: Leads carbon offset programs at scale
* Indicators:
   * Develops complex or novel carbon offset projects
   * Designs aggregated or landscape-scale carbon programs
   * Evaluates co-benefits of carbon projects (biodiversity, water, community)
   * Navigates evolving carbon market regulations
   * Develops new carbon methodologies
   * Manages carbon project portfolios
   * Publishes on carbon markets and offsets
   * Trains practitioners in carbon project development
   * Influences carbon market standards and policy
   * Builds partnerships for carbon finance
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes carbon market policy and practice
* Application: Influences carbon markets at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Develops influential carbon offset methodologies
   * Influences carbon market standards and regulations
   * Leads major carbon market initiatives
   * Publishes influential carbon market research
   * Serves on carbon standard boards or committees
   * Recognized expert in carbon markets
   * Advises governments on carbon market design
   * Advances carbon market integrity and effectiveness
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Carbon programs on public lands, carbon market oversight
* Private Sector: Corporate carbon offsetting, carbon project development, forest carbon
* NGOs: Community carbon projects, carbon market advocacy, co-benefit standards
* Consulting: Carbon project development, verification services, carbon strategy
* Academia: Carbon market research, offset effectiveness evaluation
________________


6.4 POLICY DEVELOPMENT
Creating and implementing rules and guidelines for environmental management
Skills related to developing, analyzing, and implementing the policies that guide environmental management decisions and activities.
________________


6.4.1 Policy research and analysis
Gathering and evaluating information about policy options, including their potential effectiveness, implementation challenges, and implications.
Job Tasks:
1. Review policy literature and case studies to inform management strategies
2. Analyze administrative and legislative proposals for environmental implications
3. Summarize policy options and trade-offs in briefing documents
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands policy research methods and information sources
* Application: Conducts basic policy research with guidance
* Indicators:
   * Locates relevant policy documents and research
   * Summarizes policy proposals and their provisions
   * Reviews policy literature systematically
   * Compiles case studies of policy implementation
   * Tracks policy developments through news and databases
   * Organizes policy information for analysis
   * Identifies stakeholders affected by policies
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies policy analysis frameworks and research methods
* Application: Conducts independent policy research and analysis
* Indicators:
   * Analyzes policy options using systematic frameworks
   * Evaluates policy effectiveness using evidence
   * Identifies implementation challenges and barriers
   * Compares policies across jurisdictions
   * Synthesizes research findings for policymakers
   * Evaluates policy trade-offs and unintended consequences
   * Prepares policy briefs and issue papers
   * Uses quantitative and qualitative research methods
   * Engages stakeholders in policy research
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in policy analysis and research methodology
* Application: Leads comprehensive policy research programs
* Indicators:
   * Designs sophisticated policy research studies
   * Evaluates complex policy interactions and systems effects
   * Uses advanced methods (econometrics, case study analysis, stakeholder analysis)
   * Publishes policy research in peer-reviewed venues
   * Provides expert policy analysis to decision-makers
   * Evaluates policy implementation and effectiveness
   * Trains practitioners in policy analysis
   * Influences policy research agendas
   * Builds partnerships for policy research
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes policy research practice and priorities
* Application: Influences policy research at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative policy analysis frameworks
   * Influences policy research funding and priorities
   * Leads major policy research programs
   * Publishes influential policy research
   * Recognized authority in policy analysis
   * Advises governments on policy research approaches
   * Advances evidence-based policymaking
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Policy analysis for decision-makers, program evaluation
* Private Sector: Policy research for advocacy, impact assessment
* NGOs: Policy research for advocacy, alternatives analysis
* Consulting: Policy research services, analysis for clients
* Academia: Policy research, evaluation studies, teaching
________________


6.4.2 Policy drafting and review
Writing or evaluating the actual language of policies, ensuring they are clear, legally sound, and achieve intended objectives.
Job Tasks:
1. Write clear and legally sound policy language for internal or public use
2. Review proposed policies to ensure consistency with existing frameworks
3. Facilitate stakeholder review of draft policies to build buy-in
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands policy structure and drafting conventions
* Application: Assists with policy drafting and review
* Indicators:
   * Follows policy templates and formats
   * Drafts simple policy provisions with guidance
   * Reviews policies for clarity and consistency
   * Compiles stakeholder comments on draft policies
   * Checks policies for internal consistency
   * Understands policy hierarchy (laws, regulations, guidance)
   * Maintains version control for policy drafts
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies policy drafting principles and legal requirements
* Application: Drafts policies independently and manages review processes
* Indicators:
   * Drafts clear, implementable policy language
   * Ensures policies are legally sound and consistent with statutes
   * Reviews policies for ambiguities and loopholes
   * Coordinates stakeholder review processes
   * Reconciles conflicting stakeholder comments
   * Revises policies based on feedback
   * Ensures policy enforceability
   * Documents policy development process
   * Presents policies to decision-makers
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in policy drafting and legal frameworks
* Application: Leads development of major policies and regulations
* Indicators:
   * Drafts complex policies and regulations
   * Ensures policies achieve intended objectives while avoiding unintended consequences
   * Anticipates implementation challenges in policy language
   * Manages contentious policy development processes
   * Builds consensus among diverse stakeholders
   * Provides policy drafting training
   * Develops model policies adopted elsewhere
   * Testifies on policy proposals
   * Influences policy drafting standards
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes policy drafting practice
* Application: Influences major policy development
* Indicators:
   * Drafts transformative policies or legislation
   * Influences policy development processes and standards
   * Provides expert consultation on complex policy drafting
   * Publishes on policy drafting best practices
   * Recognized authority in policy development
   * Leads major policy reform initiatives
   * Advises elected officials on legislative drafting
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Regulatory development, internal policy, guidance documents
* Private Sector: Corporate policy development, model policies
* NGOs: Model policy development, policy recommendations
* Consulting: Policy drafting services, review and analysis
* Academia: Limited direct application; teaching policy process
________________

3. Prepare impact statements to support rulemaking or legislative action

   * Assists with compliance cost estimation

   * Estimates costs and benefits of regulatory alternatives

   * Assesses environmental and health impacts of regulations
   * Analyzes uncertainty in impact estimates
   * Prepares regulatory impact analyses for rulemaking
   * Presents impact findings to decision-makers
   * Addresses equity in regulatory impacts
   * Uses economic models for impact assessment
ADVANCED (6-10 years)

   * Assesses regulatory alternatives comprehensively

   * Recognized authority in regulatory analysis
   * Advises on regulatory reform and improvement
   * Advances evidence-based regulation
Sector-Specific Applications:

3. Maintain public transparency by publishing comment summaries and responses

   * Evaluates effectiveness of public participation
   * Addresses equity in public engagement
   * Influences comment process improvements
EXPERT (10+ years)

* Indicators:
   * Develops public participation frameworks and guidance

   * Publishes on public engagement methods
   * Leads major public participation initiatives
   * Advances meaningful public involvement
Sector-Specific Applications:

* Consulting: Public engagement facilitation, comment analysis services

________________

SKILL AREAS (6.5 6.8)

6.5 INTERGOVERNMENTAL RELATIONS
Working across different levels and types of government
Skills related to coordinating and collaborating with various governmental entities that have roles in environmental management.
________________


6.5.1 Tribal consultation and coordination
Working respectfully and effectively with sovereign tribal nations on environmental management issues affecting tribal interests or rights.
Job Tasks:
1. Engage tribal governments early in project planning and decision-making
2. Facilitate government-to-government consultation meetings
3. Document and incorporate tribal input into environmental and land use plans
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands tribal sovereignty and consultation requirements
* Application: Supports tribal consultation processes with guidance
* Indicators:
   * Explains tribal sovereignty and trust responsibilities
   * Understands legal requirements for tribal consultation
   * Identifies when tribal consultation is required
   * Assists with consultation meeting logistics
   * Documents tribal input accurately and respectfully
   * Recognizes importance of government-to-government relationships
   * Understands treaty rights and their implications
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies tribal consultation protocols and cultural awareness
* Application: Coordinates tribal consultation independently
* Indicators:
   * Conducts early and meaningful tribal engagement
   * Facilitates respectful government-to-government consultation
   * Incorporates traditional ecological knowledge appropriately
   * Addresses tribal concerns in planning and decisions
   * Maintains ongoing relationships with tribal governments
   * Understands tribal environmental programs and priorities
   * Navigates consultation for projects affecting sacred sites or resources
   * Documents consultation process thoroughly
   * Coordinates consultation across multiple tribes
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in tribal relations and co-management
* Application: Leads tribal consultation programs and partnerships
* Indicators:
   * Develops comprehensive tribal consultation policies
   * Builds enduring government-to-government partnerships
   * Negotiates co-management agreements
   * Addresses complex issues at intersection of tribal and federal/state authority
   * Integrates traditional knowledge with western science
   * Provides tribal consultation training
   * Resolves conflicts between tribal and other interests
   * Advances tribal self-determination in environmental management
   * Publishes on tribal consultation and co-management
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes tribal relations policy and practice
* Application: Influences tribal consultation at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Influences tribal consultation policy and requirements
   * Leads transformative tribal partnership initiatives
   * Recognized expert in tribal relations
   * Advises on tribal sovereignty and consultation matters
   * Advances tribal capacity and leadership in environmental management
   * Influences national tribal consultation standards
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Tribal consultation programs, co-management agreements, treaty rights
* Private Sector: Tribal engagement for projects, partnership opportunities
* NGOs: Tribal partnerships, collaborative conservation, advocacy
* Consulting: Tribal consultation services, cultural resource assessments
* Academia: Tribal relations research, traditional ecological knowledge studies
________________

6.5.4 Cross-jurisdictional planning
Developing management plans that work across political boundaries to address landscape-scale issues like watersheds or wildlife corridors.
Job Tasks:
1. Lead planning efforts that span watershed or ecosystem boundaries
2. Harmonize regulatory requirements and priorities across jurisdictions
3. Facilitate multi-jurisdictional stakeholder engagement processes

   * Builds trust among diverse jurisdictions
   * Implements cross-boundary plans
   * Evaluates cross-jurisdictional planning effectiveness

* Indicators:

   * Builds governance structures for cross-boundary management
   * Resolves conflicts between jurisdictions
   * Coordinates implementation across multiple agencies and organizations
   * Secures funding for cross-boundary initiatives
   * Evaluates long-term effectiveness of landscape planning
   * Provides training on cross-jurisdictional planning
   * Publishes on landscape planning and governance
   * Influences cross-boundary planning policy

6.6 ENVIRONMENTAL MARKETS
Creating and participating in market-based mechanisms for environmental outcomes
Skills related to developing, participating in, and managing environmental market systems that assign economic value to environmental benefits like carbon sequestration, biodiversity, and ecosystem restoration.
________________


6.6.1 Carbon accounting and quantification
Measuring, calculating, and documenting greenhouse gas emissions and carbon sequestration for markets, inventories, and reporting.
Job Tasks:
1. Conduct greenhouse gas inventories for organizations, projects, or jurisdictions
2. Quantify carbon sequestration from land management or conservation activities
3. Develop monitoring and verification plans for carbon accounting
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands carbon accounting concepts and GHG protocols
* Application: Assists with carbon measurements and calculations
* Indicators:
   * Explains greenhouse gas accounting and reporting frameworks (GHG Protocol, ISO 14064)
   * Identifies emission sources and carbon pools
   * Collects field data for carbon calculations
   * Uses basic carbon estimation tools and equations
   * Understands Scope 1, 2, and 3 emissions
   * Distinguishes carbon stocks from fluxes
   * Maintains accurate carbon accounting records
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies carbon accounting methodologies and standards
* Application: Conducts carbon inventories and quantification
* Indicators:
   * Conducts comprehensive GHG inventories for organizations
   * Quantifies carbon stocks and sequestration rates
   * Applies carbon accounting protocols (VCS, CAR, Gold Standard)
   * Uses specialized carbon models and tools
   * Ensures accuracy and quality control in carbon accounting
   * Calculates emissions factors and activity data
   * Addresses uncertainty in carbon estimates
   * Prepares carbon accounting reports
   * Understands carbon accounting for different sectors (forestry, agriculture, energy)
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in carbon accounting science and verification
* Application: Leads carbon accounting programs and develops methodologies
* Indicators:
   * Develops carbon accounting methodologies for novel project types
   * Conducts third-party verification of carbon quantification
   * Designs monitoring plans ensuring accuracy and permanence
   * Evaluates uncertainty and risk in carbon accounting
   * Provides carbon accounting training and quality assurance
   * Stays current with evolving carbon accounting standards
   * Publishes carbon accounting research or guidance
   * Influences carbon accounting standards development
   * Leads organizational carbon inventory programs
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes carbon accounting standards and practice
* Application: Influences carbon accounting at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Contributes to development of carbon accounting standards
   * Serves on standard-setting bodies or technical committees
   * Publishes influential carbon accounting research
   * Recognized authority in carbon quantification
   * Influences carbon accounting policy
   * Advances carbon accounting methodology
   * Provides expert testimony on carbon accounting
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: GHG inventories, carbon monitoring programs, climate action planning
* Private Sector: Corporate carbon accounting, carbon footprint reduction, supply chain emissions
* NGOs: Carbon project development, carbon accounting for advocacy
* Consulting: Carbon accounting services, verification, methodology development
* Academia: Carbon science research, carbon cycle studies
________________


6.6.2 Biodiversity credits and habitat banking
Developing and trading credits that represent biodiversity conservation or habitat restoration outcomes.

1. Develop biodiversity credit methodologies quantifying conservation outcomes
2. Establish or participate in habitat mitigation banks or conservation banks
3. Facilitate biodiversity credit transactions between buyers and sellers

* Knowledge: Understands biodiversity credit concepts and mitigation banking
* Application: Supports biodiversity credit projects
* Indicators:
   * Explains biodiversity credits, habitat banking, and species credits
   * Understands mitigation hierarchy (avoid, minimize, restore, offset)
   * Assists with baseline biodiversity assessments
   * Monitors biodiversity outcomes on credit projects
   * Maintains records for credit tracking
   * Understands equivalency and additionality concepts
   * Tracks biodiversity credit markets and prices
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies biodiversity credit methodologies and market mechanisms
* Application: Develops biodiversity credit projects
* Indicators:
   * Develops biodiversity credit or habitat bank proposals
   * Quantifies biodiversity outcomes using appropriate metrics
   * Ensures equivalency between impacts and offsets
   * Navigates regulatory approval processes for credit projects
   * Develops monitoring plans for biodiversity outcomes
   * Facilitates credit transactions between parties
   * Evaluates long-term stewardship for credit sites
   * Addresses permanence and risk in biodiversity credits
   * Markets biodiversity credits to potential buyers
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in biodiversity markets and credit methodologies
* Application: Leads biodiversity market initiatives and policy development
* Indicators:
   * Designs innovative biodiversity credit methodologies
   * Establishes conservation banks or biodiversity programs
   * Develops guidance for biodiversity credit markets
   * Evaluates ecological effectiveness of biodiversity credits
   * Addresses market challenges (liquidity, equivalency, additionality)
   * Provides training on biodiversity markets
   * Publishes on biodiversity credits and habitat banking
   * Influences biodiversity market policy
   * Builds partnerships for biodiversity market development
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes biodiversity market practice and policy
* Application: Influences biodiversity markets at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Develops influential biodiversity credit frameworks
   * Influences policy on biodiversity markets and offsetting
   * Leads major biodiversity market initiatives
   * Contributes to international biodiversity credit standards
   * Recognized expert in biodiversity markets
   * Publishes influential research on biodiversity credits
   * Advances biodiversity market integrity and effectiveness
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Mitigation banking oversight, biodiversity credit policy
* Private Sector: Conservation banking, biodiversity offsetting, credit development
* NGOs: Biodiversity credit standards, conservation credit programs
* Consulting: Habitat bank development, biodiversity credit brokerage
* Academia: Biodiversity offset research, ecological equivalency studies

6.7 ENVIRONMENTAL HEALTH & JUSTICE
Addressing environmental health risks and equity in environmental decision-making
Skills related to assessing environmental health risks, identifying and supporting environmental justice communities, and ensuring equitable distribution of environmental benefits and burdens.
________________


6.7.1 Environmental health risk assessment
Evaluating potential health impacts from environmental exposures to chemicals, pollutants, or other hazards.
Job Tasks:
1. Conduct exposure assessments for environmental contaminants
2. Calculate health risks from environmental exposures using standard methods
3. Communicate health risk findings to affected communities and decision-makers
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands environmental health risk assessment concepts
* Application: Supports risk assessments with data collection
* Indicators:
   * Explains risk assessment steps (hazard identification, dose-response, exposure assessment, risk characterization)
   * Identifies environmental health hazards
   * Collects environmental sampling data
   * Assists with exposure pathway analysis
   * Understands dose-response relationships
   * Uses risk assessment databases and tools
   * Recognizes vulnerable populations
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies risk assessment methods and exposure modeling
* Application: Conducts environmental health risk assessments
* Indicators:
   * Conducts exposure assessments for environmental contaminants
   * Uses risk assessment models (EPA IRIS, CalTOX, RAGS)
   * Calculates cancer and non-cancer health risks
   * Evaluates multiple exposure pathways
   * Addresses uncertainty and variability in risk estimates
   * Assesses cumulative exposures and risks
   * Identifies sensitive subpopulations
   * Prepares risk assessment reports
   * Communicates risk findings to diverse audiences
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in toxicology and risk assessment methodology
* Application: Leads complex risk assessments and develops approaches
* Indicators:
   * Conducts sophisticated health risk assessments
   * Evaluates cumulative impacts from multiple stressors
   * Develops site-specific risk assessment approaches
   * Addresses emerging contaminants and novel exposures
   * Evaluates effectiveness of risk mitigation measures
   * Provides expert consultation on health risks
   * Publishes risk assessment research
   * Trains practitioners in risk assessment
   * Influences risk assessment policy and guidance
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes risk assessment science and policy
* Application: Influences risk assessment at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative risk assessment methodologies
   * Influences risk assessment guidance (EPA, ATSDR)
   * Publishes influential risk assessment research
   * Provides expert testimony on health risks
   * Recognized authority in environmental health risk assessment
   * Advances risk assessment science
   * Influences environmental health policy
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Site cleanup risk assessment, regulatory standard-setting, public health assessment
* Private Sector: Contaminated site assessment, product safety evaluation, liability assessment
* NGOs: Community health advocacy, exposure studies, risk communication
* Consulting: Risk assessment services, remediation planning, expert testimony
* Academia: Toxicology research, exposure science, risk assessment methodology
________________

3. Engage environmental justice communities meaningfully in decision-making

   * Uses EJ screening tools (EJSCREEN, CalEnviroScreen, CEJST)

   * Collects demographic and environmental data
   * Maps cumulative environmental burdens
   * Understands procedural and distributive justice
   * Documents community concerns
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)

* Indicators:
   * Conducts environmental justice analyses for projects
   * Evaluates disproportionate and adverse impacts
   * Assesses cumulative burdens in EJ communities
   * Facilitates meaningful community engagement
   * Identifies mitigation measures for EJ impacts
   * Integrates EJ considerations into decision-making
   * Evaluates procedural justice in processes
   * Addresses power imbalances in engagement
   * Prepares environmental justice assessments
ADVANCED (6-10 years)

   * Evaluates effectiveness of EJ interventions
   * Provides EJ training and capacity building
   * Publishes environmental justice research
   * Influences environmental justice policy
   * Advances procedural and substantive justice
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes environmental justice practice and policy
* Application: Transforms environmental justice at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Influences environmental justice policy (federal, state)
   * Develops innovative EJ frameworks and approaches
   * Leads transformative environmental justice initiatives
   * Publishes influential environmental justice research
   * Recognized leader in environmental justice
   * Advances environmental justice movement
   * Influences equity in environmental decision-making
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: EJ programs, screening, policy development, Title VI compliance
* Private Sector: EJ assessments for projects, community engagement, impact mitigation
* NGOs: EJ advocacy, community organizing, litigation, policy reform
* Consulting: EJ analysis, community engagement facilitation, impact assessment

3. Ensure research findings are accessible and actionable for communities

   * Builds respectful relationships with community members
   * Supports community engagement activities
   * Collects data with community input
   * Recognizes power dynamics in research
   * Understands community-identified priorities
   * Follows ethical protocols for community research

* Indicators:
   * Co-develops research questions with community partners

   * Designs culturally appropriate research methods
   * Engages community members as co-researchers
   * Ensures community benefit from research
   * Interprets findings with community input
   * Communicates results to community audiences
   * Addresses power imbalances in research
   * Builds community research capacity
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in CBPR and community-engaged scholarship
* Application: Leads transformative CBPR initiatives
* Indicators:
   * Designs comprehensive CBPR programs
   * Builds long-term community research partnerships

   * Translates research to community action
   * Evaluates CBPR effectiveness and impact
   * Mentors researchers in CBPR practice
   * Publishes CBPR research with community co-authors
   * Influences CBPR standards and practice
   * Advances community-driven research
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes CBPR practice and community-engaged scholarship
* Application: Transforms research-community relationships
* Indicators:
   * Influences CBPR policy and funding
   * Develops innovative CBPR approaches
   * Leads major community-engaged research initiatives
   * Publishes influential CBPR research
   * Recognized leader in community-engaged scholarship
   * Advances community empowerment through research
   * Transforms academic-community relationships
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Community health studies, participatory monitoring, citizen science
* Private Sector: Limited application; community engagement for projects
* NGOs: Community-led research, advocacy research, participatory assessment
* Consulting: Community engagement, participatory planning
* Academia: CBPR research, community-engaged scholarship, participatory action research
________________


6.7.4 Toxicology and exposure pathways
Understanding how toxic substances affect human health and the routes by which people are exposed to environmental contaminants.
Job Tasks:
1. Evaluate toxicity of environmental contaminants
2. Assess exposure pathways for chemicals in air, water, soil, and food
3. Identify vulnerable populations and exposure scenarios
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands basic toxicology and exposure concepts
* Application: Supports exposure assessments
* Indicators:
   * Explains dose-response relationships and toxicity concepts
   * Identifies exposure pathways (inhalation, ingestion, dermal)
   * Understands toxicity endpoints (acute, chronic, carcinogenic)
   * Recognizes vulnerable populations
   * Collects exposure data
   * Uses toxicity databases (IRIS, ATSDR)
   * Understands environmental fate and transport
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies toxicology principles and exposure assessment methods
* Application: Conducts exposure assessments and evaluates toxicity
* Indicators:
   * Evaluates toxicity of environmental contaminants
   * Conducts exposure pathway analyses
   * Estimates exposure doses for different scenarios
   * Assesses exposures for vulnerable populations
   * Uses exposure models (CalTOX, SHEDS)
   * Evaluates bioavailability and uptake
   * Addresses uncertainty in exposure estimates
   * Integrates toxicity and exposure for risk assessment
   * Communicates exposure findings to communities
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in toxicology and exposure science
* Application: Leads complex toxicological and exposure assessments
* Indicators:
   * Evaluates complex mixtures and cumulative exposures
   * Assesses emerging contaminants and novel exposures
   * Conducts biomonitoring studies
   * Develops exposure models for specific scenarios
   * Evaluates toxicity of understudied chemicals
   * Provides expert consultation on exposures and health effects
   * Publishes toxicology or exposure research
   * Trains practitioners in exposure assessment
   * Influences exposure assessment guidance
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes toxicology and exposure science
* Application: Influences exposure science at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Develops innovative exposure assessment approaches
   * Influences toxicology and exposure guidance
   * Publishes influential toxicology research
   * Recognized authority in environmental toxicology
   * Provides expert testimony on toxic exposures
   * Advances exposure science methodology
   * Influences environmental health policy
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Toxicity assessment, exposure evaluation, standard-setting
* Private Sector: Product safety assessment, occupational exposure, liability
* NGOs: Community exposure studies, health advocacy
* Consulting: Exposure assessment, toxicology services, expert testimony
* Academia: Toxicology research, exposure science, environmental health
________________


6.7.5 Air quality management
Managing and improving air quality to protect public health and meet regulatory standards.
Job Tasks:
1. Monitor air pollutant concentrations and assess compliance with standards
2. Develop air quality improvement plans and emission reduction strategies
3. Evaluate health impacts of air pollution on communities
Proficiency Levels & Assessment Indicators:
FOUNDATIONAL (Entry-level, 0-2 years)
* Knowledge: Understands air quality standards and monitoring
* Application: Supports air quality monitoring and assessment
* Indicators:
   * Explains criteria air pollutants and standards (NAAQS)
   * Operates air quality monitoring equipment
   * Collects air samples and maintains monitors
   * Analyzes air quality data
   * Understands emission sources and controls
   * Recognizes health effects of air pollution
   * Tracks air quality index and public advisories
INTERMEDIATE (3-5 years)
* Knowledge: Applies air quality management and emissions control principles
* Application: Conducts air quality assessments and develops strategies
* Indicators:
   * Conducts comprehensive air quality assessments
   * Evaluates emission inventories and source contributions
   * Develops air quality improvement plans
   * Assesses effectiveness of emission control strategies
   * Models air pollutant dispersion
   * Evaluates health impacts of air pollution
   * Implements air quality monitoring networks
   * Ensures compliance with air quality standards
   * Communicates air quality information to public
ADVANCED (6-10 years)
* Knowledge: Expert in air quality science and policy
* Application: Leads air quality programs and policy development
* Indicators:
   * Develops comprehensive air quality management programs
   * Models complex air quality scenarios
   * Evaluates emerging air quality issues (wildfire smoke, ultrafine particles)
   * Develops innovative emission reduction strategies
   * Addresses environmental justice in air quality
   * Provides air quality training and capacity building
   * Publishes air quality research
   * Influences air quality policy
   * Leads multi-jurisdictional air quality initiatives
EXPERT (10+ years)
* Knowledge: Shapes air quality practice and policy
* Application: Influences air quality at systemic level
* Indicators:
   * Influences air quality standards and policy
   * Develops innovative air quality management approaches
   * Leads major air quality programs (state, regional)
   * Publishes influential air quality research
   * Recognized expert in air quality management
   * Provides expert testimony on air quality
   * Advances air quality science and policy
Sector-Specific Applications:
* Public Agencies: Air quality monitoring, SIP development, permitting, enforcement
* Private Sector: Emissions management, compliance, air quality impact assessment
* NGOs: Air quality advocacy, community monitoring, clean air campaigns
* Consulting: Air quality modeling, permitting support, impact assessment
* Academia: Air quality research, atmospheric science, health impact studies
